## Added

- **Stage** ([Release v4.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.77.0)): Stage admits exact ESM v13 language/semantic pairs (Screenplay 4.141.0) in the renderer, the Host runtime and the specification executor. Later and mixed versions are still refused with `STAGE-ESM-016`. (#345)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.77.0)): Generated concepts carry their compliance from the model: personal data renders `[PII]` (also when the concept is a secret too), and a secret renders `[Encrypted]` with its scope plus `[NotAudited]`. `[ComplianceDetails]` is not emitted from the executable model. (#345)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.77.0)): Command inputs that contain personal-data or secret concepts, directly or inside collections, optional values and nested types, render `[property: NotAudited]`, so their values stay out of the causation chain. (#345)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.77.0)): `STAGE-ESM-040` refuses personal-data or secret concepts used as identities (command and read-model identifiers, event destinations, projection, join and child keys, event-source and stream identifier types) and on enumeration concepts. (#345)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.142.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.142.0)): `propose-property` can add a property along its chain with `action: "add"`: name the owning declaration, the `type`, whether it is `optional`, and the `hops` that add the property to other declarations or map it in a `produces`, `from` or `append` block. A projection that AutoMaps the same name needs no hop. Producers or projections left without a mapping refuse with `SourcesRequired`, and a required property needs a `fixtureValue` for every existing specification and example occurrence, or the proposal refuses with `FixtureValueRequired`. Adding to an event needs `newGeneration` or `eventNeverPersisted`, as rename and remove do (#389)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.77.0)): The Screenplay generator reports `SP0071` when a reactor executes a command and handles its outcome in code (reading, matching or discarding the `CommandResult`), and `SP0072` when a reactor recovers from redelivery through `ReactorDelivery` receipts, instead of keeping its `file` reference without saying why. (part of #3094)
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): A product sync ledger (`.cratis/ai/product-sync.json`) records, for each Cratis product, the release the corpus was last synced to and the skills and rules that depend on it; verification keeps it complete and well formed (#619)
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): A drift report (`yarn workspace @cratis/ai-verification run drift`) and a weekly workflow keep one "AI corpus product drift" issue up to date with the releases not yet read into the corpus; private repositories without access are listed as not checked, and an optional `PRODUCT_DRIFT_TOKEN` secret lets the workflow check them
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): New skills: `cratis-arc-hosting`, `cratis-chronicle-hosting`, `cratis-chronicle-decision-reads`, `cratis-arc-testing-kotlin`, `cratis-arc-identity-and-tenancy-kotlin` and `cratis-arc-spring-data-kotlin`
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): Experimental Go guidance: `cratis/language/go`, `cratis/engineering/go`, `cratis/chronicle/client-go` and `cratis/arc/go` profiles with Go rules and skills for Chronicle.Go 0.9.1, Arc.Go 0.1.1 and Fundamentals.Go 0.3.2 (part of #479)
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): An `elixir.md` language rule, so the Elixir language profile carries guidance
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): The Screenplay skills teach `propose-property` rename and remove (Screenplay 4.140.0) (part of #597)
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): Specification examples typed by an event, command or read model imported from another document, with PLAY0520 (#604)
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): The auth skill names AuthProxy and its header contract; the application profile points to `dotnet new cratis` templates; the C# rules name the Cratis architecture analyzers (CRARCH)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.51.0)): `ChronicleFixture.UsesPerContextMongoDBHost` in `Cratis.Chronicle.XUnit.Integration`: an in-process MongoDB fixture that opts in starts a fresh host for every test context and gives its databases a unique prefix, so writes a previous context's kernel flushes after shutdown cannot change the next context's results. It defaults to `false`; a fixture that overrides `GetStorageConfigurator` passes `DatabaseNamePrefix` to `WithMongoDB`. See the [integration tests guide](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/contributing/integration-tests.md#per-context-storage-isolation).
- **Arc** ([Release v22.76.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.76.0)): The Screenplay generator keeps the integer identity of every enum member. Each member is written with its number (`pending = 0`, `granted = 3`, `declined = 6`), so sparse, negative and reordered enums keep their stored values, and documents with plain positional enums also show their numbers. Non-positional numbers need ESM v12; under a `MaximumExecutableModelVersion` cap below v12, or for numbers outside int32, the members fall back to positions and warning `SP0068` says so. Members that share a number keep the one scenario values resolve to, and warning `SP0069` names the dropped aliases. See [Generating a Screenplay](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/generating-a-screenplay.md). (#3175)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.76.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.76.0)): Warning `SP0067` reports a `[Flags]` enum whose unnamed member combinations cannot be stated in the model, naming the enum and the properties that use it. Member names are emitted as before. (#3132)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.141.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.141.0)): Concept compliance markers (`pii`, `pii special <category>`, `pii criminal`, `secret`, `secret scope subject|namespace|global`) bind into the executable model as ESM v13, as an optional `compliance` member on the concept that applies wherever the concept is used (part of #141)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.141.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.141.0)): Only models that use a marker select ESM v13; models without markers keep their exact executable-model bytes and revision (part of #141)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.141.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.141.0)): ESM v13 golden vector and concept compliance canonical corpus for consumers to verify against (part of #141)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.141.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.141.0)): Concept documentation on when to mark a concept as personal data or a secret, when not to, the refusals below and how to test a protected model (part of #141)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.140.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.140.0)): `propose-property` plans a rename or removal of explicitly selected command, current-event and read-model properties in one reviewable proposal. It updates proven references, preserves AutoMap relationships, and refuses removal while readers remain. Removing an example value that a `then` step inherits is disclosed with `weakensAssertion: true`; when those steps cannot be determined, the removal is refused instead. Review the retained proposal before applying it (part of #389)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.140.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.140.0)): Event-property edits require `newGeneration` or `eventNeverPersisted`. The proposal reports every selected event's revision pair and mode in an ordered `generations` array, preserves historical generations, and discloses in-place edits. New generations require transformations before deployment to Chronicle (part of #389)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.76.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.76.0)): Stage admits exact ESM v12 language/semantic pairs (Screenplay 4.139.0) in the renderer, the Host runtime and the specification executor. Later and mixed versions are still refused with `STAGE-ESM-016`. (#296)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.76.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.76.0)): Enum members with explicit numbers render as `Member = N` in every generated C# enum, including sparse and negative numbers; `-0` renders as `0`. Unnumbered enums render exactly as before. (#296)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.75.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.75.0)): The Screenplay generator now keeps a reactor's execution identity and observation scope. `[ExecuteCommandsAsSystem]` becomes `runs as system` with its exact roles, and `[FromEventSource<T>]`, `[EventSourceType]` and `[EventStreamType]` become `from Source` or `from Source.Stream`, also when the handler body stays code. Observer-only event source and stream declarations are emitted from their actual definitions. Invalid roles report `SP0064`, event results with explicit append routing stay code with `SP0065`, and a model version cap below ESM v10 withholds the new directives with `SP0066` while keeping event-only `produces` bodies. See [Generating a Screenplay](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/generating-a-screenplay.md). (#3156)
- **AI** ([Release v7.34.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.34.1)): Corpus verification fails when a current Stage scaffold or Arc.Screenplay pin is restated outside the central version tables, unless the occurrence is listed with a reason; a listed occurrence that no longer exists also fails (#616)
- **AI** ([Release v7.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.34.0)): A `cratis-screenplay-delivery-planning` skill: plan and estimate delivery of an accepted Screenplay model by slices, run independent slices in parallel along their event contracts, and estimate from the team's own throughput (part of #611)
- **AI** ([Release v7.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.34.0)): New method references adapted from agentic-engineer: a model inventory report, a core-rules card, derived-condition events, screen patterns, a domain analysis template, a slice delivery checklist for reactors and view lifecycle checks for read models (part of #611)
- **AI** ([Release v7.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.34.0)): The legacy extraction, render and review skills teach the released `cratis screenplay conform` drift check, `cratis render --scope` and `--check`, `cratis screenplay validate --executable`, the MCP `propose-move` refactoring and the `--check field-origins`, `event-consumers` and `personas` rules (part of #611)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.73.0)): `[ReadModel(RequireQueryAttribute = true)]` lets a read model expose only methods marked `[Query]`, keeping reduction methods and private, internal or public helpers out of its endpoints and generated proxies. The setting defaults to false, preserving existing conventional query discovery. Adding `[Query]` alone does not change discovery of sibling methods. (#3163)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.73.0)): `ARC0026` and runtime validation report invalid query markers and ambiguous selected query names. Unmarked helpers on an opted-in read model are not subject to query-signature diagnostics. (#3163)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.73.0)): `ARCCHR0019` warns when a conventional read model contains a likely event-fold method that would become a query. Its previewable code fix opts the type into marked queries, supports editable partial declarations, and refuses migrations that would introduce compilation errors or require editing generated declarations. (#3163)
- **AI** ([Release v7.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.33.0)): The Screenplay skills teach explicit enum member numbers (`draft = 1`, Screenplay 4.137.0): the all-or-none int32 rule, diagnostics PLAY0667 to PLAY0671, the `enum-pinning` check and the `propose-number-enum-members` refactoring, and when numbering is worth it (#605)
- **AI** ([Release v7.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.33.0)): The skills teach ESM v12 (Screenplay 4.139.0), where non-positional numbers travel as a canonical `numbers` array beside `values`, and that the current Cratis CLI and Stage do not admit v12 yet (#605)
- **AI** ([Release v7.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.33.0)): The model-authoring skill teaches property-level `find-references` and `dependencies` (`kind: "Property"`, Screenplay 4.138.0): addressing members and historical generations, the evidence roles, and reading `complete: false` as unfinished evidence rather than "no references" (part of #597)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.75.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.75.0)): The renderer, semantic Host and specification executor now explicitly admit Screenplay ESM v11's [exact-number literal contract](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/v4.135.0/decisions/0064-exact-number-literals-claim-esm-v11.md). Precision-sensitive non-route literals, including the safe-integer bounds ±9007199254740991 and fractions such as 0.10000000000000002, retain their semantic values through generated `long`/`decimal` code and execution. Existing v1–v10 and route-only contracts remain unchanged; this does not introduce arbitrary precision or exact source mode. (#331)
- **AI** ([Release v7.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.32.0)): Screenplay extraction guidance explains Arc 22.70.0’s secret-scope recovery, SP0061–SP0063 classification diagnostics, and compiler-upgrade self-checks so generated models preserve protection and make losses explicit (#609)
- **AI** ([Release v7.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.31.0)): The Chronicle .NET client and event-type-migration skills teach opt-in pinned generation delivery (`ChronicleOptions.EventGenerationDelivery = EventGenerationDelivery.Pinned`, Chronicle 19.41.0 for reactors and reducers, 19.48.0 for typed materialized projections): what each observer receives, the one-generation-per-event-identifier rule and its registration errors, which projections stay in Compatibility mode, how bare event names in a saved projection definition resolve to a pinned generation, and why scenario tests do not prove generation delivery (#607)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.74.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.74.0)): Render server-generated UUID concepts as Arc generated-value parameters, with deterministic scenario pins and fresh values in the live runtime. (part of #175)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.74.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.74.0)): Render scalar and named command responses and carry accepted responses through the runtime to Scene. (part of #175)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.72.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.72.0)): Reactors can return a sequence mixing events, `EventForEventSourceId` envelopes and commands. Events append before commands run in order; append rejection prevents command execution, and the first failed command stops the remaining commands. Explicit event routes are preserved. Earlier effects remain committed if a later command fails, so retries require idempotent effects. (part of #3156)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.71.0)): Response value handlers can declare server-consumed values by attribute using `ICommandResponseValueHandlerForAttributed<TAttribute>` for scalars and `ICommandResponseValueHandlerForAttributedEnumerable<TAttribute>` for reference-element enumerables. Chronicle's event handlers use these declarations. (#2885)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.139.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.139.0)): ESM v12 carries explicit enum member numbers, preserving sparse and negative int32 identities alongside member names. Non-positional numbers select v12; enums pinned to their existing positions retain their previous model version and bytes. Specifications continue to use member names. Consumers must admit v12 explicitly. (#643)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.139.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.139.0)): Strict model reading and validation reject enum numbers in older versions, duplicate or out-of-range numbers, mismatched member lists and non-canonical positional arrays. (#643)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.73.0)): A screen records where it sits as Scene's `Screen.Path`: the modules and features, outermost first, it was declared in. A module's own `contribute to` reaches every screen beneath the module, and a contribution records the hierarchy it was declared in, whether it sits on a module, a feature at any depth or a screen. (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.73.0)): A navigation entry's `id`, `icon`, `presentation`, `group` and `destination outlet|dialog|external` reach the runtime, composed with its `navigate to` into one Scene destination. The menu honours `presentation` (`icon`, `text` or `both`), and an entry that opens an outlet or a dialog is executed by the Scene host. (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.73.0)): A `toolbar` item carries the destination it opens - the screen a `navigate` item targets, the dialog template a `dialog` item targets - with its parameters as route parameter bindings, so it runs through the Scene host like any other navigation. Command items render as the Stage's command action. Presentation hints are honoured (`mode` of `icon`, `text` or `both`; any other hint is a `data-scene-presentation-<name>` attribute). (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.73.0)): A screen no navigation entry lists is shown under its own module and feature, with the breadcrumb leading to it, instead of a single "Other screens" group. (part of #236)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.73.0)): A dialog opened through the host is a real modal: a backdrop above the shell, `aria-modal`, and Escape closes it. Before, it rendered unstyled at the end of the page where the shell's sidebar covered its Close button - found by the browser proof. (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.73.0)): The Stage's `core:outlet` renders the screen the host placed in the named outlet inside the Stage's data scope. (part of #237)
- **cli** ([Release v3.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.52.0)): `cratis render --package-set <FILE>` takes an approved package set (the Scene document Stage and a React host share) naming custom runtime packages beyond `core`, `scene.web` and `Cratis.Components`. The profile is resolved against the shipped and the approved packages together with Stage's planner (`CLI-RENDER-007` to `CLI-RENDER-010`), a malformed set or one that reuses a shipped package name is refused before anything is published, and the published application gets `approved-packages.json`, one extra `package.json` dependency per package and a `.frontend/approvedPackages.ts` that loads them through Scene before the first render. See [Custom runtime packages](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/blob/main/Documentation/reference/screenplay.md#custom-runtime-packages). (part of #284)
- **cli** ([Release v3.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.52.0)): The packed-CLI rendered-React check approves a custom package (`Integration/Cli/RenderedReact/CustomPackage`), renders a screen that names its component, builds and launches the application and asserts in a browser that the component rendered from the package with its style sheet and no load diagnostics. (part of #284)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.138.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.138.0)): `find-references` and `dependencies` accept `kind: "Property"` for a command, event or read-model property, addressed as its owner's address plus the member name (`owner.generationN.member` for a historical event generation). Results list every place the property flows: mappings, AutoMap, conditions and keys, screen and form bindings, specification fixtures and assertions, and opaque code. A reader that cannot be resolved is reported with its location and marks the result incomplete instead of being left out (part of #389)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.137.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.137.0)): Enum members can carry explicit numbers, `draft = 1`, so a stored enum keeps its numbers when members are reordered or inserted. Numbers are whole int32 values, unique within the enum, and either every member has one or none does. `-0` prints as `0`. Enums without numbers keep their positional meaning, and enums numbered by position compile exactly as before. Numbers that differ from positions are not admitted by any supported executable model version yet; binding refuses them until a later release admits them. See https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0065-explicit-enum-member-numbers.md (part of #643)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.137.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.137.0)): New diagnostics: PLAY0667 (mixed numbering), PLAY0668 (duplicate number), PLAY0669 (number outside int32) and PLAY0670 (malformed number). They are reported by both compilers and the editors. (part of #643)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.137.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.137.0)): The MCP refactoring `propose-number-enum-members` numbers every member of an unnumbered enum by its current position. The opt-in completeness check `enum-pinning` (PLAY0671) suggests it for unnumbered enums used by event properties. (part of #643)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.72.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.72.0)): `CratisRendering.CreateProfile(..., approvedPackages)` carries an approved package set in the render profile as `approved-packages.json` and generates the frontend that installs and loads it: one extra `package.json` dependency per package, `.frontend/approvedPackages.ts` (literal imports, package style sheets, `loadApprovedComponents()` over Scene's `loadApprovedPackages`) and a `main.tsx` that awaits it before the first render. An application with no approved packages plans byte-for-byte what it planned before. Admission regenerates the whole frontend from the document and refuses a profile that differs (`STAGE-CRATIS-001`). See [Custom runtime packages](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/custom-packages.md). (part of #236)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.72.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.72.0)): `CratisRendering.ShippedPackages`, `ShippedPackageNames` and `CatalogWith(set)`: the catalog a `ui profile` resolves against is the packages the target ships plus the approved ones, so an approved package's layouts, themes, dependencies and version ranges are checked by the same planner. (part of #236)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.72.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.72.0)): `CratisBackendApplicationScaffoldRequest.WithApprovedPackages` refuses a package that reuses a shipped package name or an npm package the scaffold already installs. (part of #236)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.72.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.72.0)): The Stage `App` takes a `packageCatalog`, so an authored `component Acme.Charts.chart` (or a bare name only an approved package declares) resolves to the key the package registers its component under instead of rendering "Unresolved component". (part of #237)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.21.0)): `Screen.path` (`Screen.Path` in .NET): the modules and features, outermost first, a screen was declared in. A destination's `module`/`feature` identity resolves against it and a host can group an unlisted screen by it. (part of #74)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.21.0)): `NavigationItem.hierarchy` (`NavigationItem.Hierarchy` in .NET): the modules and features a navigation contribution was declared in. (part of #74)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.21.0)): `core:toolbar` and `core:toolbarItem` in the `core` package. An `Action` item announces its command with its parameters resolved; `Navigate` and `Dialog` items execute their destination through the Scene navigation host (a screen in an outlet, a dialog) and are announced with `cratis.scene.navigate` outside one. `presentation` is honoured: `mode` is `icon`, `text` or `both`, and every hint is exposed as `data-scene-presentation-<name>`. See [the application hierarchy and toolbars](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/blueprints/application-hierarchy.md). (part of #74)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.71.0)): Rendered read-model and query specifications whose givens are routed now render and run, where they used to be refused with `STAGE-ESM-030`. Each given is seeded on its own event source, stream type and stream identity through `ReadModelScenario`, so a reducer filtered to a source or stream replays exactly the facts it would see in production. (#327)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.71.0)): A navigation contribution's `group` is carried into the Scene output. (#327)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): `ApprovedPackageSet` / `ApprovedPackageSetJson` (`Cratis.Scene.Engine`): the approved package set document (`manifest` + `module` per package). `Read` validates it - unknown members, document version, names the host already ships, duplicates, icon libraries, invalid npm names/exports, URL or foreign style sheets - and `Catalog` gives planning the manifests without loading code; `Write` is canonical. See [Approved package sets](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/package-hosting.md#approved-package-sets-for-custom-runtime-packages). (part of #75)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): `loadApprovedPackages` and `resolveApprovedPackageHost` (`@cratis/scene.react`): load the set's bundles through an explicit map of literal `import()`s, only when the host policy allows executable imports, refuse a bundle that differs from its declaration, and block the host with a diagnostic when a package is missing, fails to import or lacks its export. Packages load in ordinal name order. (part of #75)
- **AI** ([Release v7.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.30.0)): The Screenplay skills teach ESM v9 public events and translation direction: `public event`, `event X from "store"`, `direction inbound|outbound` on Translate slices, outbound projections and reducers that target a public event, and inbound `source events` captures, with imports of public or foreign events still refused with `PLAY0268` (#567)
- **AI** ([Release v7.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.30.0)): The toolchain skill lists the public-event and translation diagnostics `PLAY0607`–`PLAY0620` with their repairs (#562)
- **AI** ([Release v7.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.30.0)): The Screenplay skills teach ESM v10 read-model `key` modifiers and `by` blocks, per-production `stream` route overrides and observer `from` filters with `PLAY0660`–`PLAY0666`, state that composite keys still bind with `PLAY0268`, and describe exactly how a read model's identity is chosen when no key is marked (#580)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.48.0)): Projections can opt in to read every event in the generation each of their clauses handles, on live delivery, catch-up and replay alike. The kernel selects, decrypts and, when needed, migrates the event to that generation for `From`, joins, children, keys and removals. Projections registered from a .NET client with `ChronicleOptions.EventGenerationDelivery` set to `EventGenerationDelivery.Pinned` opt in; other projections are unchanged. See [event type migrations](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/concepts/event-type-migrations.mdx) (part of #4710)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.69.0)): `QueryScenario.Observe(...)` subscribes to an observable or `IAsyncEnumerable<T>` query and returns a `QueryObservation`. `Initial` is the first result, and `Next(timeout)` waits for the next one. Results are buffered from the moment of subscription, so a specification never has to sleep, and each result goes through read-model interceptors and emission guards the same way it does over SSE and WebSocket. (#3153)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.69.0)): Documentation for observable queries in `QueryScenario`, including the limits: a result from `Next` is the next emission, not the next distinct value, and MongoDB `.Observe()` queries need a real database. (#3153)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.70.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.70.0)): Keyed absence assertions for admitted read-model projections pass only when the specified instance is missing; instances at other keys do not affect the result. (part of #15)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.70.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.70.0)): Protected optional snapshot queries by read-model identifier evaluate portable authorization policies using the specification's caller, query argument and key as the subject. Denials match `then denied`, not an empty result or `then error`; reached opaque policies remain `Unsupported(Authorization)`. (part of #15)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.47.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.47.0)): The .NET client logs what it discovered at startup: totals per artifact kind at Information, and the ids of each kind at Debug. The same report is available as `IEventStore.Discovery`. (#4733)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.47.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.47.0)): Before registering, the .NET client compares its artifacts with what the event store already holds and flags likely renames or moves. A renamed reactor is a Warning, because its new id makes it catch up from the start and re-run its side effects, which `[OnceOnly]` does not prevent. A renamed reducer or projection is Information. An event type whose id is new but whose schema matches exactly one existing event type is a Warning. Findings are also available as `Registration.IdentityDrift`. (#4733)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.47.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.47.0)): `ChronicleOptions.ArtifactIdentityDrift` chooses what happens on a finding: `Warn` (the default) logs and continues, `Fail` stops registration with `ArtifactIdentityDriftDetected` for reactor and event-type findings, and `Ignore` skips the check. If the event store cannot be reached for the comparison, registration continues as before. (#4733)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.47.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.47.0)): Documentation on reactor identity: give side-effecting reactors an explicit id with `[Reactor("…")]`, and remove orphaned observers with `Observers.Remove`. (#4733)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.134.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.134.0)): `SemanticExecutionPlan.Compile(model, selectedSlices)` compiles an execution plan for a selection of slices. The plan keeps the original model and revision. Admission issues in the selected slices and in application-level declarations block it. Issues in unselected slices are reported in `UnselectedIssues` and do not block. An empty or unknown selection is refused. `Compile(model)` is unchanged. See https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/interoperability.md (#656)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.134.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.134.0)): The reference evaluator returns `SemanticUnsupported` when execution reaches a declaration a scoped plan did not admit. This covers a deferred command, query or projection, including projections that would observe published facts, so unadmitted semantics never run silently. (#656)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.69.0)): Read-model browser at `<stage base>/read-models`, including behind Studio's play-session proxy. Browse every modeled read model, including those without queries or instances, inspect keys and values, and refresh the current state. (#228)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.69.0)): Semantic-engine endpoints `GET /stage/read-models` for property contracts and live instance counts, and `GET /stage/read-models/{id}` for instance keys and values. These diagnostic endpoints expose the whole session independently of query authorization; keep the session private behind a trusted proxy. (#228)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.69.0)): Explicit unavailable states: loading returns HTTP 503; faulted sessions return HTTP 503 with `state: "faulted"` and no rows; refused models return the same HTTP 501 refusal as specification runs. (#228)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.69.0)): Under the `eventmodel` engine, both endpoints return HTTP 404 JSON directing you to the Chronicle Workbench, and the browser displays that guidance instead of rows. (#228)
- **cli** ([Release v3.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.49.0)): `cratis render --scope <Module[.Feature][.Slice]>` renders only the selected modules, features or slices; the option can be repeated. A renderer refusal (`STAGE-ESM-*`) in an unselected slice no longer blocks the render, while execution-plan errors (`PLAN-*`) anywhere in the model still do until [Cratis/Screenplay#656](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/issues/656) is available (part of #247)
- **cli** ([Release v3.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.49.0)): Scoped renders never delete or overwrite files that belong to other scopes. The ownership manifest records which module, feature and slice each file comes from, and a later full render takes over every file a scoped render produced (part of #247)
- **cli** ([Release v3.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.49.0)): A scoped render is refused before anything is written when a selected slice depends on declarations that are neither selected nor already in the destination (`CLI-RENDER-014` names them and the scope to add), or when the destination still uses the aggregate policy layout, which needs one full render first (part of #247)
- **cli** ([Release v3.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.49.0)): A scoped render into an empty destination includes the application scaffold so the result builds, and the receipt lists the scope, the slices outside it and any orphaned files; `--check` works with `--scope` (part of #247)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.46.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.46.0)): `ReactorScenario` throws `ReadModelNotSeededForReactorScenario` when a handler takes a read model that was never seeded. Before, the handler silently ran with `null`. The message names the read model and the key it looked for, and lists the keys you did seed, so a mistyped key is obvious. Call `Given.ForEventSourceId(id).NoReadModel<TReadModel>()` when a handler should deliberately see no instance. Production behaviour is unchanged: a running reactor still receives `null` for an absent read model (#3912)
- **AI** ([Release v7.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.28.0)): The Stage rendering skill teaches case tables (one generated specification class per case, named `<Spec>_<Case>`, from Stage 4.59.0; a name collision is refused with `STAGE-CRATIS-004`) and whole numbers rendered as `long` from ESM v8 (Stage 4.61.0; below v8 an out-of-`int` literal is refused with `STAGE-ESM-031`), and states that the CLI 3.46.1 default renderer (Stage 4.52.0) applies neither, with one evaluation per rule (#591)
- **AI** ([Release v7.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.28.0)): The model-authoring and model-review skills teach `semantic-diff` `before`/`after` sources from Screenplay 4.131.0: the active workspace, a model folder or registered worktree, or an export, so a model is compared with `main` through a worktree path instead of two exports, and when to use `read-proposal` with `view: "semantic-diff"` instead (#592)
- **AI** ([Release v7.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.28.0)): The `cratis-arc-command` and `cratis-specifications-csharp` skills teach Arc generated values: `[GeneratedValue]` on `Handle()` parameters for server-owned identifiers, when not to generate, analyzers ARC0022–ARC0025, and pinning generated values in `CommandScenario` specs with `PinGeneratedValue`, `AllowGeneration` and `GeneratedValues` (part of #576)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.68.0)): `CratisPlanOptions.Profile` lets `PlanFrom` and `PlanScaffold` use the caller's own render profile, with its authored documentation, strings catalog and scaffold inputs. A plan for a module, feature or slice is then byte-identical to the same files in a full render through that profile, and a scaffold-only render plus scoped renders with the same profile assemble into the same application. Scene composition is still produced only by application-scope plans; `PlanScaffold` reports `STAGE-PLAN-031` when a profile carries one. Without a profile, output is unchanged. (#321)
- **cli** ([Release v3.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.48.0)): A guarded screen action the Stage runtime can evaluate, such as `Close` shown only while `item.status == "open"`, is rendered with its guard and its hidden fallback instead of being removed. Only a guarded action the runtime cannot evaluate is left out, and it is reported as a `STAGE-SCENE-ACTION-001` warning. (part of #284)
- **cli** ([Release v3.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.48.0)): The rendered application's menu and breadcrumb follow the authored navigation hierarchy, and `cratis run` starts `cratis/stage:4.67.0` by default. (part of #284)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.45.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.45.0)): The PDL compiler adds a "did you mean" hint to a read model or property it cannot find, naming the registered read model whose title, short type name or casing matches, or the property whose casing differs (#4729)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.45.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.45.0)): The reducer analyzer reports public reducer-like methods that will never be dispatched, for example `Reduce(TReadModel? current, object @event)`, through the existing `CHR0006` and `CHR0007` diagnostics. It recognizes the same method shapes as the runtime, including previous event generations marked `[EventTypeGenerationFor<T>]` (#4726)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.67.0)): A feature's `contribute to Navigation` entries carry the module and features they were declared in, and the Stage menu renders them as a nested tree: groups for the module and each feature, entries ordered by their `order`, each linking to the screen it opens with the current screen marked. The breadcrumb shows the groups leading to the current screen. A feature's entry arrives on each of its screens and is shown once. See [Authored navigation hierarchy](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/stage-rendering.md#authored-navigation-hierarchy). (part of #237)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.44.0)): `ReactorScenario` can seed events on a route: `Given.ForEventSource(id).OnStream(eventSourceType, eventStreamType, eventStreamId).Events(...)`. The reactor sees the route in `EventContext.EventSourceType`, `EventStreamType` and `EventStreamId`. Every argument is optional and defaults to what appending to the event log uses. The same `OnStream` is available on reactors registered fluently (#4760)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.44.0)): A new `ProducedEvents` list on `ReactorScenario` records every event a reactor produces together with its destination (event source id, event source type, event stream type and event stream id), resolved the same way a running reactor appends it. Assert it with `ShouldHaveProduced<TEvent>(eventSourceId)` or `ShouldHaveProduced<TEvent>((produced, @event) => ...)`. See [Asserting produced destinations](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/testing/reactors/scenario.mdx#asserting-produced-destinations)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.66.0)): Reducers in models with named event sources and streams can now be rendered with a `from` filter. The generated Chronicle reducer only sees events routed to that event source, or to that event source and stream, so unrouted events and events on other streams no longer change the read model. Plans for a module, feature or slice include the event source definitions a filter refers to. Reactions with a `from` filter are still refused with `STAGE-ESM-035`, and the Stage Host and specification runs still refuse reducers. (part of #297) (part of #207)
- **AI** ([Release v7.27.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.27.3)): The corpus verification job replays the screens authoring workflow on the pinned public vector: it installs the pinned `cratis` CLI, materializes the published `Cratis.Screenplay.CanonicalCorpus` folder source (the corpus stays Screenplay-owned and is not copied), replays the stdio MCP transcript with the revision-checked column edit, renders the edited folder and requires the column in the generated `scene.json`. It runs locally with `screens-smoke.sh <work-folder>`; the pins live in `screens-smoke.json`. The browser step still needs Docker and Chromium and stays a documented manual step. (part of #536)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.19.0)): `PropertyDescriptor.open` and `CollectionItemDescriptor.open` mark an `Object` property or the items of a `Collection` as open: fields the descriptor does not describe are kept exactly as stored and never edited, instead of being refused. Described fields are still checked. See [Descriptors](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/editing/descriptors.md#value-types). (part of #70)
- **AI** ([Release v7.27.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.27.2)): The authoring workflow now previews an edited model's render with `cratis render --check` (CLI 3.44.0: exit `0` current, `6` changes pending, `5` refused) and reads the receipt's `sources` and `bySource` (CLI 3.45.0) and MCP `semantic-diff` (Screenplay 4.131.0) to confirm an edit changed only what it should. They are labeled published, not executed on the verified vector. (part of #536)
- **AI** ([Release v7.27.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.27.2)): The UI composition contract explains what Scene 4.17 does with an authored destination: nested `core:outlet` composition with deep links and innermost back, toolbar buttons that open a dialog or slice screen, the `SettingsPage` template and the embedded `WebViewPackageHost`. (part of #536)
- **AI** ([Release v7.27.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.27.2)): Routing evaluations cover a build, edit and run request for a whole business application. (part of #536)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.18.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.18.1)): Every business template preview in both blueprints is audited with axe-core's WCAG 2.0 and 2.1 A and AA rules, in the populated, empty, loading and error states for the default blueprint. A control spec proves the audit reports a violation when there is one. Colour contrast and landmark rules need a real layout and are documented as not asserted. See [The template set](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/blueprints/template-set.md#accessibility-audit). (part of #73)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.65.0)): Guarded actions run in the Stage frontend runtime: the action executes the first alternative whose guard holds for the item its screen's single-item `data` declaration reads, with its `with` values taken from that item. When none holds, `otherwise hidden` renders nothing and `otherwise execute <Command>` offers that command. Without an item nothing is offered, and the action follows the data as it changes. (part of #236)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.65.0)): Guarded `on click`, `on double click` and `on select` interactions run exactly the branch that holds for the row they happened on, or their `otherwise` actions, through the Scene interaction engine. A table that declares a double click no longer selects or navigates on the click that starts it. (part of #237)
- **cli** ([Release v3.47.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.47.0)): `cratis render` renders Screenplay ESM v8 models: event sources, streams, routed commands and routed specifications render through the bundled Stage 4.62.0. ESM v9 and v10 are still refused with `CLI-RENDER-004`, naming the version (#290)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): `PropertyDescriptor.properties` describes the fields of an `Object` property. A field's `path` is a single name relative to the object and a field may itself be an `Object` or a `Collection`, so a structured value of any depth is describable. See [Descriptors](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/editing/descriptors.md#value-types). (part of #70)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.68.0)): ARC0022 (error): a `[GeneratedValue]` parameter's type cannot generate itself, or is nullable. (part of #3128)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.68.0)): ARC0023 (error): `[GeneratedValue]` is used outside a command's `Handle` method, for example on `Provide`, a static method, a read-model method or a controller action. (part of #3128)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.68.0)): ARC0024 (error): two generated values on the same `Handle` have the same name. (part of #3128)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.68.0)): ARC0025 (warning): the command's `Provide` method also supplies the generated value's type, which is never used for that parameter. (part of #3128)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.68.0)): A documentation page for each of these analyzer rules. (part of #3128)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.43.0)): `ReadModelScenario` can seed events on a route: `Given.ForEventSource(id).OnStream(eventSourceType, eventStreamType, eventStreamId).Events(...)`. The projection or reducer sees the route in `EventContext.EventSourceType`, `EventStreamType` and `EventStreamId`. Every argument is optional and defaults to what appending to the event log uses (#4738)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.132.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.132.0)): `SemanticReactionSettlement` lets a host that runs a model live settle event-triggered reactions after a command, with the same semantics as specifications: facts in append order, reactions in semantic-id order, `from` and `where` checks, productions before invocations, the declared `runs as` identity and the fact limit. The result is all-or-nothing. On acceptance it returns the settled world and only the facts the reactions appended, for the host to persist together with the command's facts. On a rejection, conflict or unsupported capability it returns the input world. Invoked commands run through the evaluator the host passes in, so host checks also apply to them. See https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/reactions.md (#644)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.42.0)): `ReadModelScenario` and `ReactorScenario` honour artifacts registered through `ChronicleOptions.ExplicitArtifacts`. Pass the options to the new `Defaults(clientArtifactsProvider, serviceProvider, options)` constructor, and specify a reactor registered with `RegisterReactor` through the new `ReactorScenario(reactorId, defaults)`. (#4736)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.42.0)): `InMemoryClientArtifactsProvider` lets a specification state exactly which artifact types a scenario sees. (#4736)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.42.0)): Analyzer `CHR0057` warns when a class implements more than one `IProjectionFor<>` or more than one `IReducerFor<>`. (#4734)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.67.0)): `CommandScenario<TCommand>.PinGeneratedValue(name, value)` pins a generated value by its parameter name, and `PinGeneratedValue<TCommand, TOtherCommand>(name, value)` pins one for a command that runs inside the scenario's command. Pins are fixed once the first command starts executing. (part of #3128)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.67.0)): Generated values are strict by default in a scenario: a command whose generated value is not pinned fails with a message that names the command, the parameter and how to fix it. `AllowGeneration()` lets the scenario generate real values instead. (part of #3128)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.67.0)): `CommandScenario<TCommand>.GeneratedValues.Last<T>(name)` and `.All<T>(name)` return the values a scenario generated or pinned. (part of #3128)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.67.0)): Documentation on testing generated values, and an upgrade guide for moving from `New()` inside `Handle` and from the removed `IIdentitySource` and `CommandScenario.Generate(...)` to `[GeneratedValue]` and `PinGeneratedValue`. (part of #3128)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.41.0)): .NET reactors and reducers can opt in to receive every event in the generation they handle, on live delivery, catch-up, replay and partition retries alike. Set `ChronicleOptions.EventGenerationDelivery` to `EventGenerationDelivery.Pinned`; the kernel then selects, decrypts and, when needed, migrates the event to that generation. Without the option, delivery is unchanged. See [event type migrations](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/concepts/event-type-migrations.mdx) (part of #4710)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.41.0)): The event context carries `AppendedGeneration`, the generation the event was originally appended in (part of #4710)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.66.0)): `[GeneratedValue]` and `[GeneratedValue("name")]` on a model-bound command's `Handle` parameter: Arc creates the value with the type's `IGeneratable<T>.New()` (for example a UUID-backed concept), validates it with the concept's validator, and passes it to the handler. It never becomes a request property, so clients cannot choose it. A value the concept's validator rejects is a server error, not a validation response. (part of #3128)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.66.0)): `CommandContext.GeneratedValues` lists the values generated for the command, in resolution order. Generated values are never written to logs or exception messages. (part of #3128)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.66.0)): A source generator registers an AOT-safe factory for each generatable type used in a `[GeneratedValue]` parameter. Without one, Arc falls back to reflection, and an AOT application refuses to start with an error that names the command and parameter. (part of #3128)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.66.0)): A documentation page on generated values in commands: when to use them, how they are created and validated, and the pitfalls. (part of #3128)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.40.0)): `EventScenario` constructors that take an explicit event sequence, event store and namespace, and still discover the constraints your application declares: `new EventScenario(sequenceId, eventStore, @namespace)` and `new EventScenario(defaults, sequenceId, eventStore, @namespace)`. Passing an explicit `null` constraint provider to the existing constructor still means no constraints (#4520)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.40.0)): `ReadModelScenario`, `ReactorScenario` and `EventStoreForTesting` implement `IDisposable`, like `EventScenario`. Dispose a scenario to release its in-memory event store, for example from your specification's `Destroy()`
- **Stage** ([Release v4.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.61.0)): `STAGE-ESM-031` refuses a whole-number literal outside the `int` range in a model before ESM v8, naming v8 as the fix instead of failing as a C# compile error in the rendered application. Moving an existing model from v7 to v8 widens its stored event schemas, which Chronicle treats as a new event generation. (#263)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.131.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.131.0)): `semantic-diff` compares the open workspace, a model folder or worktree, or an export: pass `before` and `after` as `{ "workspace": "active" }`, `{ "path": "<folder>" }` or `{ "workspaceJson": "<export>" }`. A folder is read without writing anything, uses its persisted identities when it has them, and a folder with a pending interrupted write is refused (#558)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.60.0)): Specifications that Stage renders for commands in models with named event sources and streams now check routes.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.60.0)): Givens are seeded on their event source and stream.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.60.0)): Each expected event's event source type, stream type and stream id is asserted, or its absence when the specification says `unrouted`.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.60.0)): Expected events in any order are matched one to one, as in Screenplay's reference runner, so an event can't satisfy two expectations.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.60.0)): Read-model and query specifications whose givens need routes are still refused with `STAGE-ESM-030` until Chronicle's read-model scenario can seed routed events (https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/issues/4738). (#177)
- **cli** ([Release v3.46.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.46.0)): `cratis ai install` and `cratis ai update` print a note when the `cratis/codescene` profile is selected: the CLI does not register the CodeScene MCP server, so register `cs-mcp` in your own user-level MCP configuration (#312)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.59.0)): Specification case tables (Screenplay decision 0046) render as one specification class per case, named from the derived `<Specification>_<Case>` specification. Two case names that normalize to the same generated file are refused with `STAGE-CRATIS-004` instead of overwriting each other. (#256)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.59.0)): A failing case in a Stage specification run names its case and its effective fixtures, in the same `Case '<Case>' of '<Specification>': … Effective fixtures: …` form as Screenplay's reference runner, in both the SpecRunner report and the Host's run results. (#256)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.59.0)): `SpecificationExpansion.ExpandAll` returns every effective specification of an authored specification, including each case of a table. `LoadedSemanticModel.SpecificationOrigins` and `SemanticSpecificationRunOptions.SpecificationOrigins` carry the compiler's case provenance into a specification run. (#256)
- **cli** ([Release v3.45.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.45.0)): `cratis render` and `cratis render --check` receipts list, for every path, the semantic declarations the file realizes (`sources`: id, kind and a readable `Module/Feature/Slice/Name` address), plus a `bySource` view that maps each declaration to its files; scaffold files have no sources. The receipt schema version is now `2` (#246)
- **cli** ([Release v3.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.44.0)): `cratis render --check` plans exactly like a normal render. It then reports, per path, what a real render would write, delete, leave unchanged or refuse, and why. It writes nothing to the destination and does not recover an interrupted publication. It exits `0` when the destination is current, the new `6` (`changes_pending`) when a render would write, delete or rewrite the ownership manifest, and `5` when planning fails, the render would be refused or recovery is pending, so a CI step can gate on stale generated code (#245)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.39.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.39.2)): A Help menu in the Workbench top bar with links to the Chronicle documentation and the Cratis Discord (#4722)
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.4)): Projects created from the Cratis templates say where to ask questions: the "project is ready" message and the generated README point to the documentation and the Cratis Discord (#75)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.58.0)): Stage now renders Screenplay models that declare named event sources and streams and route commands to them.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.58.0)): Each event source becomes a Chronicle `IEventSource` definition under `EventSources/`.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.58.0)): Routed commands carry `[EventSource<T>("<stream>")]` and set the stream id on the events they append. The stream id can be a UUID, a text, a whole-number or a composite value, or a literal. Authorization and validation run before the route.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.58.0)): A text stream id that cannot be used is returned as a validation error rather than an exception.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.58.0)): Plans for a module, feature or slice include the event source definitions their commands use.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.58.0)): Generated specifications for routed commands are refused with `STAGE-ESM-030` until they can assert routes. See https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/event-source-routing.md. (part of #177)
- **AI** ([Release v7.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.27.0)): `cratis/codescene` profile, not part of `cratis` or `cratis/full`. It provides the CodeScene MCP server (`cs-mcp`), four CodeScene skills (`safeguarding-ai-generated-code`, `guiding-refactoring-with-code-health`, `prioritizing-technical-debt`, `risk-based-testing-with-code-health`) and a Code Health policy rule (#584)
- **AI** ([Release v7.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.27.0)): The skills are taken from [codescene-oss/codescene-mcp-server](https://github.com/codescene-oss/codescene-mcp-server) under Apache-2.0. Apart from licence metadata and modification notices, the only changes are an opt-in guard and a pointer to the Cratis policy. Each skill applies only in repositories that select the profile and have the CodeScene MCP tools available
- **AI** ([Release v7.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.27.0)): The Code Health policy judges the change, not the file:
- **AI** ([Release v7.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.27.0)): changed files must not decline in Code Health, and new files must score at least 9.0
- **AI** ([Release v7.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.27.0)): files that already score low are improved only through dedicated, tested work
- **AI** ([Release v7.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.27.0)): generated code is excluded, and Cratis conventions take precedence over findings
- **AI** ([Release v7.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.27.0)): state-changing CodeScene tools need the user's approval
- **Ante** ([Release v1.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Ante/releases/tag/v1.3.0)): The loading screen shown before the application mounts is written by the server: `Ante:PageTitle` sets the tab title from the first paint, `Ante:LogoUrl` and `Ante:CustomCssUrl` apply immediately, `Ante:SplashMessage` sets the announced name, and `Ante:SplashHtml` replaces the screen with the host's own markup. The default screen follows the `--ante-splash-*` custom properties.
- **cli** ([Release v3.43.2](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.43.2)): `cratis --help` ends with where to ask questions: the Cratis Discord (part of Cratis/.github#48)
- **cli** ([Release v3.43.2](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.43.2)): `cratis chronicle diagnose` points to the Cratis Discord when it finds problems, and the error report command mentions Discord for questions alongside the GitHub issue flow (part of Cratis/.github#48)
- **cli** ([Release v3.43.2](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.43.2)): A "Questions and help" link to the Cratis Discord when opening a new issue (part of Cratis/.github#48)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.17.0)): A `core:outlet` component declares a named nested outlet on a screen. A destination into it opens its screen there and keeps the declaring screen on the page, and the opened screen may declare outlets of its own, so composition nests to any depth. A deep link to the innermost level composes every level above it, and back closes the innermost level. See [Navigation diagnostics](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/blueprints/navigation-diagnostics.md#nested-outlets). (part of #74)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.17.0)): `SceneNavigationContextValue` exposes `primaryScreen` and `outlets`, which say which screen fills the primary region and each nested outlet. (part of #74)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.17.0)): A `SettingsPage` screen template in `@cratis/scene.blueprint.components`: a module's settings as a generated command form with save and discard. (part of #73)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.17.0)): A descriptor for the Cratis Components toolbar button, so designers configure its label, icon, text, presentation and destination. (part of #74)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.17.0)): A runnable embedded webview example renders a custom package set with `WebViewPackageHost` and forwards `cratis.scene.*` events to VS Code's `acquireVsCodeApi` or the parent frame. See [Package hosting](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/package-hosting.md#embedded-host-example). (part of #75)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): `useValueOutput` and `valueOutputProperty` in `@cratis/scene.react` publish and declare an input's `value` output: on mount with the initial value and on every change, as JSON (dates as ISO 8601 strings). (part of #69)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): The PrimeReact text, textarea, password, number, date, dropdown, multi-select, radio, checkbox and toggle inputs publish and declare their `value` output, so a designer can offer it as a binding source. (part of #69)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): `resolveArgumentSource` (`@cratis/scene.engine`) and `ArgumentSources.Resolve` (`Cratis.Scene.Engine`) resolve a command argument source: `component.<id>.<output>` reads another element's output, choosing the longest element id since ids may contain dots, and any other source is a data-context path. (part of #69)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): `@cratis/scene.components/runtime` exports `cratisComponentsRuntimePackage` with the components, descriptors and manifest, and loads no designer, property editor or design-time action. `cratisComponentsDesignTime` holds the design-time contributions. (part of #70)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): `createHashSceneHistory` keeps navigation entries in the URL fragment, such as `index.html#/WorkItemDetails?workItemId=B`, for hosts served as a single file or from a path they do not control. It also follows a fragment changed by hand. See [Navigation diagnostics](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/blueprints/navigation-diagnostics.md#history-deep-links-and-dialogs-in-the-navigation-host). (part of #74)
- **AI** ([Release v7.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.26.0)): The Screenplay toolchain skill ships the machine-readable Screenplay contract for its pinned release, so agents can look up whether a diagnostic code, MCP tool or CLI option exists. (#552)
- **AI** ([Release v7.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.26.0)): Corpus verification checks the Screenplay skills against that contract and fails on unknown or retired PLAY codes (including codes inside ranges), MCP tools and parameters, CLI commands and options, and stated tool counts, with the file and line of each finding. It also fails when the contract version differs from the pinned Screenplay version, and prints a coverage report of contract features no skill mentions. (#552) (#529)
- **AI** ([Release v7.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.26.0)): Each Screenplay release, and a weekly run, checks the skills against the newest contract and opens, updates or closes one `screenplay-sync` tracking issue with the findings. (#529)
- **AI** ([Release v7.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.25.0)): Corpus verification compiles every `screenplay` example in the skills with the pinned standalone Screenplay tool. Complete models must compile with no diagnostics, examples that demonstrate a diagnostic must produce exactly the declared codes, models with specifications run `screenplay test`, and fragments compile inside a declared parent or are listed as skipped. A skill opts in with a `play-compiles` assertion in its `verification.json`. (#416)
- **AI** ([Release v7.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.25.0)): CI installs the Screenplay version pinned in the toolchain skill and requires the check; locally a missing tool is reported as skipped, never as passed, and a different tool version fails. (#416)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.39.0)): `IEventSequence.CompleteStream(ClosedStreamScope)` closes a scope made of any non-empty combination of event source id, event source type, event stream type and event stream id; an append covered by a closed scope is refused with `StreamClosed`. A new `EmptyScope` error, and the same or an already covered scope returns `AlreadyCompleted`. See [Scoped closing](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/closing-streams/scoped-closing.mdx). (#4668)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.39.0)): An optional `expectedTailSequenceNumber` on the scoped `CompleteStream` closes only when nothing was appended to the scope after your last fact, so an ad-hoc close cannot race a late append. (part of #4669)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.39.0)): `[ClosesStream]` on an event type and `IConstraintBuilder.ClosesStreamOn<TEvent>()` declare a closing event: appending it closes the declared scope in the same append, later covered events (also later in the same `AppendMany`) are refused, `ReopenedBy` event types reopen the scope, and `EventStreamIdFrom` takes the stream id from an event property. Closures from closing events are rebuilt by constraint reindexing. See [Closing events](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/closing-streams/closing-events.mdx). (#4671)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.39.0)): `IEventSequence.IsStreamCompleted(ClosedStreamScope)` and `GetClosedStreams()` tell whether a scope is closed and list closures; the Workbench lists the closed streams of an event sequence; the kernel's `ReopenStreamScope` command repairs a mistaken manual close and records the reason and actor as a `StreamScopeReopened` system event. See [Inspecting and repairing closed streams](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/closing-streams/inspecting-and-repairing.mdx). (#4670)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.39.0)): A "close the books" recipe shows how a closing or carry-over fact ends one period's stream and the application starts the next. See [Close the books](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/scenarios/close-the-books.mdx). (#1448)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.39.0)): Event seeding can target an event source type, event stream type and event stream id through new `ForEventSource`, `For<TEvent>` and `ForEvents` overloads; routing takes part in seeding idempotency, and existing seed data is not seeded again. (#4642)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.39.0)): `GetMetadataAt` on the event sequence reads the metadata of events by exact sequence number (up to 500 per call) without reading or decrypting payloads, and resolves the caused-by identity, including its on-behalf-of chain, from the identity store at read time with an explicit resolution status for missing, unnamed and system identities. (#4683)
- **cli** ([Release v3.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.43.0)): `cratis chronicle auth status` shows the server your login belongs to and whether the selected server matches it, with `tokenServer` and `loginMatchesServer` in JSON output (#198)
- **cli** ([Release v3.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.43.0)): Commands print a notice when your stored login belongs to another server, so you know they are running without it, instead of reporting this only with `--debug` (#198)
- **AI** ([Release v7.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.24.0)): Specification `description`, persona callers (`given caller as <Persona>`) and specification case tables, with their diagnostics. (#556)
- **AI** ([Release v7.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.24.0)): Fenced Markdown `documentation` on modules, features, slices, commands, events, read models and reactions as the home for durable reasoning, with the MCP summary fields and diagnostics. (#538)
- **AI** ([Release v7.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.24.0)): Whole-document authoring with `propose-source`, and the released MCP tool count. (#537)
- **AI** ([Release v7.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.24.0)): The `find-specification-obligations` and `find-modeling-smells` review tools in scenario coverage and model review. (#541)
- **AI** ([Release v7.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.24.0)): Routes in `when redelivered` locators, whole-route overrides and route origins in fixtures. (#550)
- **AI** ([Release v7.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.24.0)): Guarded `when`/`otherwise` alternatives on `on click`, `on double click` and `on select`, the `where` deprecation on item triggers, and their diagnostics. (#551)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.38.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.38.2)): Chronicle records which version of an event type's migration definitions produced each generation added by the backfill, and keeps every version of those definitions instead of replacing them (part of #4189)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.57.0)): `CratisRendering.CheckPublication(plan, readExistingFile)` tells a publisher whether it is safe to write a module, feature or slice plan into an existing application. It returns `CratisPublicationCheck.Compatible`, or `CratisPublicationCheck.RequiresApplicationScope` with the affected paths and a reason when the destination still has the policy layout from before Stage 4.50.0. An application-scope plan is always compatible. The check only reads the shared policy files the plan would overwrite, recognizes the current layout from their content, and treats unrecognized content as requiring application scope. The reader returns null only for a confirmed-absent file and must throw on a read failure. (#291)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.57.0)): `ArtifactRenderPlan.Scope` records the scope a plan was planned for.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.129.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.129.0)): Read-model properties take a trailing `key` modifier. Several properties marked `key` form a composite key. Queries name every key part in a `by` block, and command and trigger `reads` map each part by name (`part = source`) under `by`. A lookup that misses a part, names an unknown part or supplies an incompatible, optional, collection or literal value is reported (PLAY0660, PLAY0661, PLAY0662) (#459)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.129.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.129.0)): A partial command `reads … by` can be completed by a typed repair when each missing part has exactly one same-named, compatible command property. Queries and trigger reads are not repaired (#459)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.129.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.129.0)): A plain or inline `produces` can carry its own `stream Source.Stream` line with `streamId` mappings. It replaces the command route for that production only, and other productions keep the command route. A route line under a reaction or refusal production is an error (PLAY0663), and an override equal to the command route is a warning with a removal repair (PLAY0664) (part of #302)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.129.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.129.0)): Reactions and reducers take `from Source` or `from Source.Stream` and then see only facts routed there. Unrouted facts never match. An invalid filter is an error (PLAY0665), and a filter that excludes every known producer of a trigger event is a warning (PLAY0666) (part of #302)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.129.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.129.0)): ESM v10 admits production route overrides and observer filters next to the reaction system identity. A model selects v10 when it uses any of the three. Every other model keeps its version and bytes, and strict readers for schema 9 and earlier reject the new members (part of #302)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.129.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.129.0)): Monaco and the VS Code extension highlight, complete and explain keys, `by` blocks, production routes and filters. The event model board shows key fields, composite lookups, per-production routes and what each reaction or reducer observes
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.129.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.129.0)): The Invoicing sample routes payments through a `Payment` event source with a production override, filtered reactions and reducers, and a composite-keyed view. The TimeTracking sample reads an engagement month row by its composite key
- **AI** ([Release v7.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.23.0)): The `$identity` caller root, and the `identity` block that describes identity details as authoring metadata, with diagnostics PLAY0633 to PLAY0646. (#571) (#572)
- **AI** ([Release v7.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.23.0)): The personal-data and secret markers, their qualifiers and scopes, purpose declarations with their checks and the processing report, as released in Screenplay 4.127.0. (#548)
- **AI** ([Release v7.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.23.0)): PLAY0653 for a repeated compliance marker, and that `pii personal` is one marker. (#577)
- **AI** ([Release v7.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.23.0)): The event data-subject mark with diagnostics PLAY0590 to PLAY0595, and that it is reported, not yet acted on. (#554)
- **AI** ([Release v7.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.23.0)): Reaction `runs as system` diagnostics PLAY0647 to PLAY0652, the trusted-actor privilege check, and the corrected premise about Arc's system identity. (#574)
- **AI** ([Release v7.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.23.0)): Composite and personal or secret stream ids (PLAY0515), PLAY0504 as an error for mismatched routed destinations, stream-id literal checks and canonical comparison. (#539) (#545)
- **AI** ([Release v7.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.23.0)): Self-describing identity-migration refusals (`identityMigrationIssues`) and PLAY0345 to PLAY0348 in the editors. (#540)
- **cli** ([Release v3.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.42.0)): `cratis screenplay conform <MODEL_ROOT>` checks that hand-written code still matches an authored Screenplay model: it extracts a model from the code with the same source adapters as `cratis screenplay generate`, without writing anything, and compares it with the model using Screenplay's own comparison (#259)
