- **cli** ([Release v3.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.42.0)): Code elements missing from the model and event or member shape mismatches are reported as blocking drift; model elements not yet realized in code are reported as informational, since a model may legitimately be ahead of the code (#259)
- **cli** ([Release v3.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.42.0)): `conform` exits 0 when the code conforms, 1 on blocking drift and 2 when the check could not run or could not compare everything, and `-o json` gives machine-readable findings for CI (#259)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.56.0)): Exposures (`exposure for`) and instance values (`instance`) are carried on the Scene application as `ExposureDeclaration`/`ExposedProperty` and `InstanceContribution`/`ContributedItem`, including collection operations, editable fields, re-exposures and item values. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.56.0)): A `contribute to` block written inside a screen now contributes its directives to the named contribution point, after what the screen's module and feature contribute. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.56.0)): Screen and dialog templates carry their slot `content`, `display` name, `description` and picker `scopes`; `scopes none` stays distinct from no scopes. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.56.0)): Arrangements carry `grid` columns and rows, fractional `grow` and `span` on containers as well as slots. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.56.0)): A `navigate` directive's route, `outlet` and parameters become a typed Scene `DestinationReference` on the element's `destination` property. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **AI** ([Release v7.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.22.0)): Model authoring teaches the MCP dependency graph and board dependency map, named-rule and handler intent views, specification route rows, the evidence-pinned repair loop (including the PLAY0516 timeline repair), semantic diff, completeness `checks`, scoped diagnostics and worktree-aware state roots. (#532)
- **AI** ([Release v7.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.22.0)): The language skills teach `depends on`, exact numbers, event-source and stream routes in commands and specifications, systems and operations, typed specification examples, reaction refusals and redelivery, `runs as system role` on reactions, policy negation and guarded screen actions. (#532)
- **AI** ([Release v7.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.22.0)): The toolchain skill lists PLAY0514 to PLAY0517 and the completeness diagnostics, explains `--scope` and `--check`, and says which verdicts editors show and which need the `screenplay` tool or the MCP `diagnostics` tool. (#532)
- **AI** ([Release v7.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.22.0)): Legacy extraction covers `--authoring-only-constructs` and what the Arc generator now recovers, including SP0039 for scenarios whose event sources cannot be stated. (#528)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.65.0)): The Screenplay generator writes event sources, streams, command routes and specification routes into its default output wherever Screenplay's executable model (ESM v8) can state them. This covers property, literal and single-placeholder `[EventStreamId("{Name}")]` stream ids, and `no stream` on unrouted expected events. (#3090)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.65.0)): Routes Screenplay still refuses are left out with a diagnostic naming why, and stay available behind `ScreenplayOptions.AuthoringOnlyConstructs`. That covers property-path and handler-command mappings, generated-property mappings, colliding source or stream names, and a source named `Default`. (#3090)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.55.0)): Screenplay specifications for models with named event sources and streams now run in the Stage Host and with `--engine semantic`. Givens and appended events are written with their event source type, stream type and stream id. Expected events are compared by route, including `unrouted`. Expectations in any order are matched one to one, so an earlier match can no longer consume the event a later expectation needs. The run report shows each event's route. Structural SpecRunner runs keep refusing routes with `STAGE-ESM-030`. (part of #177)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.127.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.127.0)): `ModelComparison.Compare` in `Cratis.Screenplay.Comparison` compares two Screenplay models and returns a typed structural difference: declarations added, removed, renamed or moved, changed members, event contract changes and generation coverage, specification expectation changes, direct dependants, identity changes, and what was not compared (#555)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.127.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.127.0)): Models without persisted identities, such as one freshly generated from code, are compared by matching declarations on kind and address; renames and moves then show as a removal and an addition rather than being guessed (#555)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.127.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.127.0)): Guide to comparing models from .NET: https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/model-comparison.md (#555)
- **AI** ([Release v7.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.21.0)): The `cratis-fundamentals-concept` skill teaches concepts that create their own value: implement `IGeneratable<TSelf>` and generate with `GenerateValue.Uuid()`, or `GenerateValue.UuidV7()` when index locality matters, from Cratis.Fundamentals 7.23.0. It covers the v7 trade-offs (reveals creation time, not monotonic within a millisecond) and when not to generate: client-supplied ids, secrets and tokens, and idempotency. (part of #576)
- **AI** ([Release v7.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.21.0)): Evaluations for `cratis-fundamentals-concept`: a self-generating identity, choosing UUID v7, and refusing generation where it does not fit. (part of #576)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.126.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.126.0)): A concept that repeats a compliance marker, such as `pii personal`, `pii pii` or `secret secret`, now gets warning `PLAY0653`. Both compilers keep the first marker, attach the concept's `pii` and `secret` settings to it and print it once. A quick fix in the editors and the MCP `propose-repair` tool removes the repeat (#584)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.54.0)): The Stage Host now runs Screenplay models that declare named event sources and streams and route commands to them. Each appended event carries its event source type, stream type and stream id. The Host registers the model's event sources with Chronicle each time it connects. Routed events are read back with their route when the Host rebuilds its state after a restart. A route the model could not have produced is refused, not ignored. (part of #177)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.125.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.125.0)): ESM v10 (language and semantic `10.0`, `schemaVersion: 10`) admits the reaction system identity as the `SemanticReaction` member `runsAs` (`{"kind":"system","roles":[...]}`, roles sorted). Only models that declare `runs as` select it; every other model keeps its version and bytes, and strict readers for schema 9 and earlier reject the member (#383)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.125.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.125.0)): Specifications run a reaction's invoked and returned commands as an authenticated system identity holding exactly the declared roles, so a role-gated invocation succeeds with `runs as` and is refused without it. Claim conditions against this identity are unknown and deny, and a specification's `given caller` is never passed to invocations
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.23.0)): `IGeneratable<TSelf>` in `Cratis.Concepts`: a concept implements `static TSelf New()`, so constrained generic code can create it without reflection and with Native AOT (part of #1176)
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.23.0)): `GenerateValue.Uuid()` returns an RFC 9562 version 4 UUID from `RandomNumberGenerator` (part of #1176)
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.23.0)): `GenerateValue.UuidV7()` returns an RFC 9562 version 7 UUID: `Guid.CreateVersion7()` on .NET 9 and later, and an equivalent implementation on .NET 8 (part of #1176)
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.23.0)): Documentation page on generating values: https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/blob/main/Documentation/csharp/generating-values.md (part of #1176)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): `Cratis.Scene.Engine.Bindings`: `BindingResolver`, `BindingValidation`, `BindingScope` and `QueryBindingState` resolve data context, query, component output and literal bindings with the same null behavior, diagnostics and cycle detection as the TypeScript engine. See [Binding resolution](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/architecture/binding-resolution.md). (part of #69)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): `nestBindingScope`, `removeComponentOutputs` and `QueryBindingState` in `@cratis/scene.engine` give nested templates their own data context, drop the outputs of elements that leave the tree, and accept a bound query's result only for its latest run. (part of #69)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): `useBoundQuery` in `@cratis/scene.react` reruns a bound query when its arguments change, aborts the previous run, ignores its late answer and clears the result when the selection is cleared or the rebind fails. (part of #69)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): `stackCommandFormLayout` and the command form's `widthSizeClass` property: at `Compact` a manual form stacks its fields in one column in reading order. See [Command form geometry](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/command-form-geometry.md). (part of #71)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): `applyCommandFormLayoutKey` and the `commandFormLayout` property editor move a form field with the arrow keys and resize it with Shift and an arrow key, announcing moves that would leave the declared columns. (part of #71)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): `resolvePackageHost` resolves the UI profile's `layout` and `theme` from the approved packages as `host.layout` and `host.theme`. See [Package hosting](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/package-hosting.md). (part of #75)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): `isNetworkAsset` decides which package assets reach beyond the host. (part of #75)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.124.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.124.0)): A reaction can declare the system identity its invoked commands run under: `runs as system`, optionally followed by `role "<Role>"` repeated with `and`. It prints after the description and before the first trigger and round-trips in both compilers, editors and MCP `declaration-details`. Until the identity is admitted into the executable model, binding a reaction that declares it reports PLAY0268; models without it are unchanged (part of #383)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.124.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.124.0)): New diagnostics: PLAY0647 for a malformed, repeated or misplaced `runs as` or empty or duplicate roles, and warnings for a reaction invoking a gated command without an identity (PLAY0648), an identity on a reaction that neither invokes a command nor has an implementation body (PLAY0649), a declared role no invoked command needs (PLAY0650) and an identity that definitely cannot satisfy an invoked command's gate (PLAY0651) (part of #383)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.124.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.124.0)): An opt-in `privilege` completeness check (PLAY0652) reports each producer of a reaction's trigger event that can cause an elevated reaction without holding the reaction's roles itself (part of #383)
- **cli** ([Release v3.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.41.0)): `cratis render` resolves every `ui profile` against the packages the bundled `cratis` target renders (`core`, `scene.web` and `Cratis.Components`) before it publishes anything. A profile it cannot honor stops the render: nothing is published, and managed artifacts from an earlier render stay as they were. The new diagnostics are `CLI-RENDER-007` (unknown package), `CLI-RENDER-008` (missing layout), `CLI-RENDER-009` (packages that cannot be combined, such as an incompatible theme or a missing dependency) and `CLI-RENDER-010` (undeclared theme). See [UI profile resolution](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/blob/main/Documentation/reference/screenplay.md#ui-profile-resolution). (part of #284)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.52.0)): `cratis render` emits the Stage frontend runtime into a generated application under `.frontend/stage`, with `src/stage-scene.json` (the composed Scene with native command form metadata) and `src/stage.ts` (routes read from the generated Arc proxies, plus localized strings). The generated frontend renders the full composed application instead of a single screen. (part of #236)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.52.0)): `CommandFormSchemas` in `Cratis.Stage.Contracts` gives the presentation schema and form fields for a command's properties, so the same command shows the same form fields in a live Stage and in a generated application. (part of #236)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.52.0)): The Stage frontend reads its Scene, routes and strings through a `StageSource`, which serves either the `/stage` endpoints or content built into an application. `App` also accepts extra components for its registry. (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.52.0)): `Verification/generated-react-browser` runs the browser scenarios used against a live Stage against a generated application served on its own backend: two-item scoped selection, deep link, delayed-response discard, clear, native create, rename and add-comment forms, theme, and fail-closed guarded actions. (part of #237)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.38.0)): Unique constraints can now keep every value an event source appends instead of one value per event source. Set `Mode = UniqueConstraintMode.PerValue` on `[Unique]`, or call `WithMode(UniqueConstraintMode.PerValue)` on the unique constraint builder. A value then stays reserved until a removal event releases it. A removal event can name the properties that carry the value to release (`[RemoveConstraint(..., Properties = [...])]`, `RemovedWith<TEvent>(e => e.Id)`); without properties it releases every value the event source holds. The existing behaviour stays the default (#4641)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.122.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.122.0)): A top-level `identity` block declares typed identity details beyond the token built-ins, each from exactly one source: `from claim "<name>"`, `from query <Name> by <expression>`, or an inline code or `file` implementation. It is authoring metadata, like `authentication`: it parses, validates, prints, merges across a folder and is navigable in the editors and MCP, but adds nothing to the executable model. Reading a declared detail with `$identity.<detail>` where it would execute reports PLAY0268 until the block is admitted (#119)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.122.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.122.0)): New diagnostics PLAY0633–PLAY0646 for invalid or duplicate identity blocks and details, missing or unknown sources, bodies under a sourced detail, redeclared built-ins, and query sources that are not keyed single results, use other details in `by`, return a mismatched type or are guarded by authorization that depends on identity details
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.121.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.121.0)): `$identity.<property>` reads the caller wherever an expression is accepted, as a shorter root for `$context.identity.<property>`. It names the same properties (`id`, `name`, `userName`, `isAuthenticated`, `roles`, `claims.<name>`), warns with PLAY0155 on an unknown property, and compiles to exactly the same executable model as the `$context.identity` spelling. `$context.identity` keeps working unchanged. Both editors highlight, complete and check the new root (part of #119)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.64.0)): `IIdentitySource` gives commands, queries and Arc's own event source id allocation a single, replaceable source of new identities; a handler takes it as a `Handle()` parameter (#3070)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.64.0)): `CommandScenario` and `QueryScenario` gain `Generate(...)`, which queues the identities a spec expects so generated ids can be asserted exactly (#3070)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.120.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.120.0)): `exposure for <Owner>` declares what a layout, screen template or dialog template lets an instance configure: component properties by identifier or exact quoted stable id, with an optional label, collection operations (`add`, `remove`, `reorder`, `edit-fields`), editable item fields and `reexposes <Owner>`. Maps to Scene's `ExposureDeclaration` and `ExposedProperty`. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.120.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.120.0)): `instance <Screen or Template>` stores typed literal values (`set <component>.<path> = <value>`) and collection items (`items` / `item <id>`) for what was exposed. Maps to Scene's `InstanceContribution` and `ContributedItem`. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.120.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.120.0)): `contribute to <Point> [order <n>]` inside a `screen` contributes content to a contribution point, mapping to Scene's `Screen.Contributions`. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.120.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.120.0)): Screen and dialog templates can provide their own slot content with `content <slot>`, and picker metadata with `display`, `description` and `scopes` (`application`, `module`, `feature`, `subfeature`, `slice` or `none`); layouts accept `scopes`. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.120.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.120.0)): Flow arrangements accept `grid ... columns <n> rows <n>`, `grow [<number>]` and `span <n>` on containers, and a weighted `grow <number>` on slots. Navigation accepts `outlet <name>`. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.120.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.120.0)): New diagnostics `PLAY0621`–`PLAY0632` reject malformed composition, unknown exposure owners and instances, unexposed or mistyped instance values, re-exposure cycles and broken re-exposures, unknown template content slots, unknown navigation outlets, template scope mismatches and components from packages no `ui profile` declares. The source stays in the syntax tree. See [diagnostics](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/diagnostics.md). (part of #168)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.120.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.120.0)): The canonical screen composition corpus covers every new construct in its typed positive vector and adds six rejection vectors with exact diagnostics and no artifacts. (part of #168)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.14.0)): `SceneNavigationHost` records every navigation as a history entry. With `createBrowserSceneHistory(window, basePath)`, route overrides and their parameters reach the address bar, deep links open the matching screen with its parameters, a refresh restores the entry, and the browser's back and forward buttons step through screens. `createMemorySceneHistory` gives embedded hosts the same behavior. See [Navigation diagnostics](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/blueprints/navigation-diagnostics.md#history-deep-links-and-dialogs-in-the-navigation-host). (part of #74)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.14.0)): Dialog destinations open as their own history entry. `closeDialog(result)` returns to the screen that opened the dialog and exposes the result as `dialogResult`, and the browser's back button also closes the dialog. (part of #74)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.14.0)): `matchSceneRoute` and `sceneRoutesFrom` match a URL to a screen route, capturing `{name}` path segments and query parameters; a URL that matches nothing is reported as `unresolvedUrl`. (part of #74)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.14.0)): A `MasterDetail` screen template in `@cratis/scene.blueprint.default`: a list beside the selected record, with a detail outlet that accepts `Detail` screens, stacking at a compact width. (part of #73)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.14.0)): `GalleryScreenPreview` takes a `state` (`populated`, `empty`, `loading` or `error`), applied by `applyTemplatePreviewState` to data-bearing elements only, so a template can be previewed in each data state. (part of #73)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.14.0)): The PrimeReact `dataTable` accepts `loading` and `error`: it announces a loading status or an error alert in place of its rows and keeps its column headers. (part of #73)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.14.0)): A pattern-to-template coverage matrix compares the MUI Store, Creative Tim, React Themes and ThemeForest business patterns with the templates that implement each, with priorities and deferred patterns: [Business pattern coverage](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/blueprints/pattern-coverage.md). (part of #73)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.63.0)): `[EventStreamId("{ReportingScopeId}:{Period}")]` builds a command's stream id from its own properties, so owner-qualified ids no longer need `ICanProvideEventStreamId`. Concepts contribute their underlying value, dates and times use fixed invariant formats, and the id is resolved after validation, so a validator can reject a missing part. `EventStreamIdTemplate.ResolveFor(command)` returns the same id for reads and seeding. See [Concurrency](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/chronicle/commands/concurrency.md). (#3102)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.63.0)): `EventRoute` carries the source type, stream type and stream id of a route. Resolve it from an event source definition with `IEventRoutes.For<TSource>(stream)`, read the command's own route with `CommandContext.GetEventRoute()`, and stamp returned events with `route.Route(id, events)`, so command metadata, reads and appends use one value. (part of #2987)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.63.0)): `IStreamReads` reads one stream, or every stream of one stream type, for an event source, tail first. The returned `StreamDecision.Append(...)` appends routed events guarded at that tail. An empty stream is checked as a first append, and a concurrent append into the same stream comes back as a concurrency validation failure. See [Stream decisions](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/chronicle/commands/stream-decisions.md). (#3101)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.63.0)): Return `CompleteStream` from a command handler, alone or next to its events, to close a stream after the command's events commit. Reactors can return it too. An already completed stream counts as success, and a later append to the closed stream is a validation failure. Closing happens after the commit, not atomically with it. See [Completing streams](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/chronicle/commands/completing-streams.md). (#3091)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.63.0)): Commands can tag the events they append with structured named tags: `[EventTag("projectId", nameof(ProjectId))]`, `ICanProvideEventTags`, a returned `EventTags` value, or application-wide `ICanProvideCommandEventTags` providers. Tags on returned `EventForEventSourceId` wrappers are kept on every append path. See [Event tags](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/chronicle/commands/event-tags.md). (#2570)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.63.0)): `CommandScenario` can seed events on a route (`Given.ForEventSource(id).OnRoute(route).Events(...)`), append competing events on a route (`AppendConcurrently(id, route, events)`), and assert closed streams with `ShouldHaveCompletedStream(...)`.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.63.0)): Analyzer ARCCHR0016 warns when a command declares `concurrency: true` but `Handle` returns `EventsWithConcurrencyScopes`, whose exact scopes replace the attribute's. A code fix removes the flag. (#3100)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.63.0)): Analyzer ARCCHR0017 reports an `[EventStreamId]` template that names a missing, non
- **AI** ([Release v7.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.19.0)): `LanguageModelResult.IsQuotaExhausted`, `PoolAttemptOutcome.QuotaExhausted`, and `TriedProviders` and `QuotaExhaustedProviders` on `PoolDispatchResult` (#423)
- **AI** ([Release v7.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.19.0)): The worker `failed` callback carries `reason: "quotaExhausted"` with `provider`, plus `model` and `vendorCode` when known, when the agent session ran out of quota. Other failures send the same fields as before (#423)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.62.0)): `[EventTag("name", nameof(Property))]` on a command tags every event it appends with a property value, or with a constant through `Value` (#2570)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.62.0)): `ICanProvideEventTags` on a command, and application-wide `ICanProvideCommandEventTags` services resolved per command from its scope, supply tags computed at runtime (#2570)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.62.0)): Returning an `EventTags` value from `Handle()` adds tags known only once the handler has run (#2570)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.13.0)): `diagnoseNavigation` (`@cratis/scene.engine`) and `NavigationDiagnostics.Diagnose` (`Cratis.Scene.Engine`) report duplicate routes, duplicate, missing and incompatible outlets, navigation cycles through nested outlets and unavailable screen or dialog targets, with the same codes and messages in both engines. `diagnoseRenderedNavigation` in `@cratis/scene.react` compares the routes the renderer actually builds. See [Navigation diagnostics](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/blueprints/navigation-diagnostics.md). (part of #74)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.13.0)): An outlet can list the template types it `accepts`; a destination placing any other screen there is diagnosed as an incompatible outlet. (part of #74)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.13.0)): `TemplateMetadata` carries `compatibility` (Scene and package version ranges), `attribution` and an SPDX `license` with `licenseUrl`, in both the TypeScript and C# models. `validateTemplateMetadata` / `TemplateMetadataValidation.Validate` reject missing or invalid values, and `describeTemplateCatalog` / `TemplateCatalog.Describe` list every template with whether it can be used and why not. See [Template compatibility, attribution and license](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/blueprints/template-provenance.md). (part of #73)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.13.0)): `resolvePackageHost` exposes the approved bundles' template catalog as `templates`; set `sceneVersion` on the host configuration to check each template's Scene range. (part of #73)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.13.0)): `resolveDesignTimeHost` loads package previews, designers, property editors, property displays and design-time actions through generic lookups, with a generic fallback and a diagnostic for every unknown, undeclared, unloaded or incompatible extension. `PackageDesignTimeMetadata.contractVersion` and `DesignTimeContractVersion` version the contract. See [Package design-time extensions](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/editing/design-time-extensions.md). (part of #70)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.13.0)): `runDesignTimeAction` submits an action's canonical edit batch whole or not at all, so host validation and undo stay authoritative. (part of #70)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.13.0)): A design record compares the WinForms and ASP.NET designer patterns with Scene's extension points: [Designer patterns from WinForms and ASP.NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/editing/designer-patterns.md). (part of #70)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.51.0)): `CratisRendering.PlanFrom(PlaySources, PlanSelection, CratisPlanOptions)` compiles `.play` files and folders and plans the selected modules, features, sub-features and slices, named by their authored path. Several entries combine into one plan. Files that are not selected are still compiled, so concepts, application-level declarations and sibling features can be passed in as context without being rendered. The result is a `CratisPlanResult` with the artifacts, typed diagnostics and a digest. An overload accepts an already loaded model.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.51.0)): `CratisRendering.PlanScaffold(CratisPlanOptions)` plans only the application scaffold, at the application root, without any Screenplay sources. Plans for a module, feature or slice never contain the scaffold.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.51.0)): The `Domain` option on `CratisPlanOptions` places slice code under `{Domain}/{Module}/{Feature}/…` and concepts and types under `{Domain}/Common`, with namespaces that follow the folders. Without a domain, the output is unchanged.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.51.0)): `ArtifactRenderPlan` now carries a digest of the planned output. The same sources and selection produce the same artifacts and digest, whatever order the files are listed or found in.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.51.0)): Typed `STAGE-PLAN-*` diagnostics report unknown modules, features or slices (listing the names that exist), an empty selection, an invalid or reserved domain, sources outside the root and sources that do not compile. A source plan never comes back silently empty. Symbolic links and other reparse points below the source root are refused rather than followed. See https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/plan-from-sources.md. (#210)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.119.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.119.0)): Added a render audit to the screens acceptance harnesses. It renders the canonical screens vector with the installed CLI and checks that two renders are byte-identical and that no artifact contains a timestamp, a GUID absent from the source, an absolute path, or host and user details. It also checks that a changed UI profile and a modified managed artifact are refused, and that the rendered application can be recovered into the same commands, events, read models and queries. `run-public-acceptance.sh` runs it after its self-test. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **AI** ([Release v7.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.18.0)): A bundle-diff run mode for the agent harness images (`DIRECT_RUN_MODE=bundle-diff`). The worker fetches a git bundle of the baseline commit from `DIRECT_BUNDLE_URL`, never clones or pushes, and refuses to start when repository credentials or clone inputs are present. It posts `{ diff, sha256, structured: { outcome, touchedFiles, gaps, summary, selfChecks }, usage, correlation }` to `DIRECT_RESULT_URL` before the completion callback. The outcome is `Completed`, `NoChanges`, `Refused` or `Cancelled` (#566)
- **AI** ([Release v7.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.18.0)): Phase progress events (`started`, `cloned`, `agentRunning`, `diffReady`) posted to `DIRECT_PROGRESS_URL` by every harness, carrying the caller's `DIRECT_CORRELATION` fields verbatim (#566)
- **AI** ([Release v7.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.18.0)): On SIGTERM, a bundle-diff worker posts its partial diff marked `Cancelled` within the termination grace period (#566)
- **AI** ([Release v7.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.18.0)): `WorkerRuntimeOptions.WorkerDeadline` and an optional `WorkerJob.Deadline` set the Kubernetes Job's `ActiveDeadlineSeconds`, which was fixed at 48 hours (the default is unchanged) (#566)
- **AI** ([Release v7.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.18.0)): `WorkerLaunchOutcome.ConfigurationTooLarge`: the Kubernetes runtime refuses a job whose mounted files would overflow the 1 MiB Secret limit, before creating anything, and logs the total and the largest files (#566)
- **Chronicle.Mcp** ([Release v2.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Mcp/releases/tag/v2.0.0)): A `Mutation` deployment profile (`Cratis__Chronicle__Mcp__Profile=Mutation`). It enables the job-control tools only for a configured principal, with an expiry date and grants scoped by event store, namespace, operation and job. (#21)
- **Chronicle.Mcp** ([Release v2.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Mcp/releases/tag/v2.0.0)): Denied tool calls return a stable, machine-readable error (`{"error":{"code":"…","operation":"…"}}`) with the codes `not_authenticated`, `not_authorized`, `principal_revoked`, `policy_expired`, `policy_invalid`, `bad_request` and `chronicle_denied`. The error never includes store, namespace, target or credential details.
- **Chronicle.Mcp** ([Release v2.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Mcp/releases/tag/v2.0.0)): Grants and revocations are checked again on every call, so revoking a principal or letting the policy expire takes effect without a restart.
- **Chronicle.Mcp** ([Release v2.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Mcp/releases/tag/v2.0.0)): Deployment profiles documentation covering the trust boundary, credential handling, the grant format and confirmation expectations for `delete_job`: https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Mcp/blob/main/Documentation/deployment-profiles.md
- **Chronicle.Mcp** ([Release v2.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Mcp/releases/tag/v2.0.0)): Chronicle MCP releases are listed in the official MCP Registry as `io.github.Cratis/chronicle-mcp`, using the versioned Docker image. The listing starts the server in the read-only profile. (part of #5)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.50.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.50.1)): The Stage event model carries each read model's modeled queries (`Queries`, with name, `by` parameter and cardinality), and `/stage/routes` resolves a `by` query by its modeled name. See [Query endpoints](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/urls.md#query-endpoints). (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.118.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.118.0)): Added a screens release acceptance matrix to the Screenplay documentation. It maps each criterion of the screens-release issues to merged source, released versions and executed proof, and lists the browser runtime blockers that remain open. See [Screens release acceptance](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/screens-release-acceptance.md). (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.118.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.118.0)): Added `run-public-acceptance.sh`, which runs the browser, MCP transcript and Studio acceptance harnesses with one command against the installed CLI and the Stage image that CLI defaults to. It fails when the CLI or Stage version differs from the expected one. (part of #173)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.50.0)): Scoped render plans now include every concept and composite type their selected slices reference, transitively, including through projections over other modules' events and through scoped projection children, joins and removals. The rendered files are byte-identical to the ones an application-scope plan produces. Concepts and types that the selection does not reference are left out.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.117.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.117.0)): ESM v9 admits `public event`, `event X from "store"`, `direction inbound|outbound` on Translate slices, projections and reducers that target a slice's public event, and `source events` captures; models using them bind and run instead of being refused with `PLAY0268`. Public and foreign imports stay refused. (part of #480) (part of #481) (part of #482) (part of #483) (part of #484)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.117.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.117.0)): The reference evaluator publishes the slice's public event from private events, one instance per event source id, so specifications can say `when append <private event>` then `<PublicEvent>`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.117.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.117.0)): Canonical serialization and the `full-esm-v9.json` golden carry event visibility and origin, slice direction, event targets and the events source of a capture, with `PublicEventsCorpus.V9` as the source-backed vector.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.117.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.117.0)): Samples: Invoicing gains an outbound translation and Commerce an inbound one.
- **AI** ([Release v7.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.17.0)): Screenplay MCP verification now includes a real stdio JSON-RPC transcript harness for scratch-corpus proposal, revision, stale-apply, and comment-preservation checks. (part of #536)
- **AI** ([Release v7.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.16.0)): Claude subscription conversations through the official Claude Code CLI, with streaming responses, native caller functions, structured output, and usage reporting. Requires Claude Code on the service PATH and Unix owner-only temporary files. See [subscription transport](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/blob/main/Documentation/anthropic-credentials.md).
- **AI** ([Release v7.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.16.0)): Default Claude Code model aliases for subscription providers without a model catalog: Fast uses Haiku, Balanced uses Sonnet, and Powerful/Premier use Opus. Explicit model mappings and discovered catalogs take precedence.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.114.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.114.0)): Shared Screenplay syntax, AST, printer and transport support for typed component literals, literal bindings and exact component stable ids, with matching TypeScript parser/model coverage. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.114.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.114.0)): Scene 4.12 command-form geometry authoring with `generation`, `layout`, columns, placements, gaps and width units preserved through the shared Screenplay ABI. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.114.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.114.0)): Rich screen corpus coverage for typed literals, exact stable ids, form geometry, package component bindings, templates, outlets and multiple screens. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.37.0)): `events` source for captures: declare `source events` with a `sequence` and one or more `from` event types, and the capture translates matching inbox events into local events. See https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/captures/capture-declaration-language/index.md (part of #4640)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.37.0)): `Events` source type, with `Sequence` and `Events` on the capture source definition. (part of #4640)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.112.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.112.0)): `public event` declarations, and an opaque quoted store origin on events and imports (`event X from "store"`, `import Qualified.X from "store"`). Events stay private unless declared public. (part of #481)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.112.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.112.0)): `direction inbound` or `direction outbound` on `Translate` slices; a Translate slice without a direction stays inbound. (part of #480)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.112.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.112.0)): Outbound translations can produce their public event with a `projection` or `reducer` whose `=>` target is the event. (part of #482)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.112.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.112.0)): `source events` captures with `from <Event>` lines, which turn another application's public events into private events in an inbound translation. (part of #483)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.112.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.112.0)): Diagnostics `PLAY0607` to `PLAY0620` that keep public and private events apart, and a repair for `PLAY0614` that declares the one consistent direction. (part of #484)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.112.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.112.0)): Support for the new forms in the TypeScript compiler, the Monaco language service, the VS Code extension, the event model board, the MCP tools and the published contract.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.110.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.110.0)): Screen composition corpus now includes a browser-native controls harness that starts the released Stage runtime, drives Chromium, and records the exact missing Stage endpoints blocking full browser assertions. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.36.0)): `[Public]` attribute marks an event type as public so it can be published outside the event log. (part of #4634)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.36.0)): `[PublishTo]` attribute and event sequence targets let a projection or reducer append its output as events to a named event sequence. (part of #4635)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.36.0)): Event type visibility and origin are stored with registered event types in the in-memory, MongoDB and SQL providers. (part of #4636)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.36.0)): Publications are recorded idempotently, and registering two publishers of the same event type to one sequence is rejected. (part of #4639)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.36.0)): Documentation for public events and event sequence targets. (part of #927)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.109.0)): Screen composition released-vector results now record the CLI 3.40.1 authored Scene render, including authored screens, templates, dialog, navigation, profile, command/query bindings, and remaining guarded-action warnings. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.108.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.108.0)): Screen composition corpus now records exact released-vector results for CLI 3.40.0, Stage 4.49.1, bundled Screenplay 4.105.0, Scene 4.12.0, and Studio 0.136.3. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.108.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.108.0)): Released-vector assertions now distinguish generated backend command/query forms from the authored full-app screens contract, including missing master/detail, navigation, dialog, profile, and package/icon surfaces. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **AI** ([Release v7.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.15.0)): Screenplay screen authoring guidance now covers component data-context bindings, exposed templates, manual form columns, packages/icons, hierarchical outlets, routes, toolbar dialogs, and support-level reporting. (part of #536)
- **AI** ([Release v7.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.15.0)): MCP screen authoring workflow now connects revision-checked proposal/apply editing with same-source validation, render, and runtime parity checks. (part of #536)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.107.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.107.0)): `purpose <Name>` declarations with `basis` (GDPR Art. 6(1)), `interest`, `condition` (Art. 9(2)), `authorization` (Art. 10), `subjects`, `retention`, `recipient`, `transfer … safeguard` and `erasure exception` (Art. 17(3)), referenced with `purpose <Name>` from modules, features and slices; they are report-only and do not change the executable model (part of #557)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.107.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.107.0)): The opt-in check `--check purposes` asks about personal data with no purpose in scope, special-category data without an Art. 9(2) condition, criminal-offence data without an authorization, purposes without a lawful basis and purposes nothing references (part of #557)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.107.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.107.0)): A record of processing activities generated from the model, through the MCP `processing-record` tool and `screenplay report processing --format json|markdown|csv`, with a DPIA prompt and a finding when erasing a data subject would also destroy data kept under a legal obligation; it is generated from declarations and is not legal advice (#557)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.107.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.107.0)): `description "…"` on concepts, policies, constraints, projections, screens and forms (#535)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.107.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.107.0)): The concepts guide explains each GDPR special category (`pii special …`) and criminal-offence data (`pii criminal`), with links to the regulation, and the editors show the same explanation on hover (part of #557)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.106.0)): Screen composition corpus now records working-branch harnesses with exact Stage, Studio, Scene, and Screenplay branch heads, commands, statuses, and release-switch rules for full-app screens parity. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.106.0)): Working-branch assertions now cover Stage screen planning, Studio Screenplay transport, Studio native Play metadata, Scene form geometry, browser native controls, and MCP revision-edit transcript expectations. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.12.0)): Platform-neutral command form geometry metadata for columns, field placement, spans, gaps and typed width units, with JavaScript and .NET model parity. (part of #73) (part of #75)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.12.0)): Command form layout validation, defaults, legacy width-token migration and resize/placement draft helpers that keep invalid draft geometry separate from committed metadata. (part of #74) (part of #75)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.12.0)): Dirty command value preservation helper for metadata edits and query refreshes. (part of #75)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.12.0)): Native command form rendering for typed geometry, including valid submit behavior and invalid geometry blocking before the native submit boundary. (part of #73) (part of #75)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.12.0)): Command form geometry reference documentation for downstream Screenplay, Stage and Studio consumption. (part of #73) (part of #74) (part of #75)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.48.0)): A `complianceDetails` render option renders Screenplay concept compliance metadata: personal data gets `[ComplianceDetails]` with its GDPR Art. 9(1) special category or Art. 10 criminal-offence prefix followed by the authored reason, and secrets get `[Encrypted]` with their encryption scope and reason. The option is off by default, so existing models render unchanged until it is settled whether a details change counts as an event schema change (https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/issues/4660). (#250)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.105.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.105.0)): Executable semantic model support for observable collection queries without a key and observable collection queries keyed by one caller-supplied `by` argument, including canonical JSON for Stage consumers. (part of #536)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.105.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.105.0)): Documentation and contract examples for Stage consumers showing unkeyed list query routes, keyed list query routes, and the unsupported filtered, scoped, performer-backed and source-filled variants. (part of #536)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.104.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.104.0)): `given caller as <Persona>` runs a specification as an authenticated caller built from the persona's policies. When a policy has alternatives, the leftmost one that can be built is used, and hover and MCP show which. The opt-in `personas` completeness check reports ambiguous personas, personas that gate nothing and gates no persona reaches (#382)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.104.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.104.0)): A specification can run over named `case` blocks with typed `parameter` lines, reading values as `case.<name>`. Each case runs and reports as its own specification, `<Specification>_<Case>`, in both compilers, the editors, the board, MCP `run-specifications` and `screenplay test --filter` (#449)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.104.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.104.0)): The machine-readable Screenplay contract lists persona callers, case tables and their diagnostics
- **Scene** ([Release v4.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.11.0)): Executable business gallery grouping for scope-aware Scene templates, with recursive package-host rendering for Components blueprint gallery screens. (part of #73)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.11.0)): Runtime package host surfaces for standalone, embedded and webview rendering, including approved asset and font emission through the real Scene React renderer. (part of #75)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.11.0)): Blocking package-host behavior so profiles with dependency, policy, asset or design-time diagnostics expose no partial runtime registry or rejected assets. (part of #75)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.11.0)): Runtime-only and design-time package separation so hosts can render safe runtime bundles without loading optional design-time contributions. (part of #75)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.11.0)): Scene navigation host for hierarchical screen, outlet, deep-link and dialog composition through rendered Scene trees. (part of #74)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.103.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.103.0)): MCP `propose-move` moves a slice to another feature, or a feature to another module or feature, as a reviewable proposal that keeps every semantic and event-contract identity, repairs references (including screen actions and `depends on`), and refuses moves that would change inherited authorization or interaction behaviour, collide with an existing name, or lose an identity (#386)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.103.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.103.0)): `screenplay contract` writes a versioned, machine-readable contract of the language: keywords and constructs, every diagnostic with its severity and title, every MCP tool and CLI command with their parameters, and which constructs each executable model version admits. Every fact is derived from the compiler, and the contract ships as a release asset and inside the `Cratis.Screenplay.Tool` package (#500)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.102.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.102.0)): Screen composition corpus now carries authored folder-source behavior assertions for master/detail selection, query rebinding, native form validation, dialog routing, package profile fallback counts, and browser control behavior. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.102.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.102.0)): Folder-source MCP and harness expectations record the import/edit/export, browser, CLI/Stage, and Studio surfaces without marking unexecuted harnesses as passing. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.102.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.102.0)): RegisterProject legacy corpus now includes a pinned Stage execution vector with the released render manifest, scene plan, typed bindings, and artifact hashes as a compatibility vector. (part of #173)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.61.0)): The generator now uses `Cratis.Screenplay` and `@cratis/screenplay-language` 4.98.0, so the embedded viewer picks up the newer language (#3078)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.61.0)): A concept marked both `[Encrypted]` and `[NotAudited]` is generated as `@sensitive`, and as `@pii @sensitive` when it is also `[PII]`. Markings on a property or its positional constructor parameter are read as well (#3079)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.61.0)): A concept with only `[Encrypted]` or only `[NotAudited]` is not marked `@sensitive`; the partial marking is reported as `SP0058` (Information) (#3079)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.61.0)): With `ScreenplayOptions.AuthoringOnlyConstructs` enabled, specifications state where events land: `stream <Source>.<Stream>` and `streamId` on given and appended events, and `no stream` on expected events. Source and stream declarations are added for routes the specifications state (#3060)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.61.0)): Expected events can carry the issued command's route when every production uses command context and the stream id is a concrete value (#3060)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.101.0)): ESM v8 makes event sources, streams and command routes executable. A model that declares a source, routes a command or states a specification route selects v8; every other model keeps its version and bytes.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.101.0)): The model lists `eventSources`, a command carries a `route`, and every fact the command produces carries a `route` with its source kind, stream kind and encoded stream id. (part of #407)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.101.0)): The stream id is resolved after validation and requirements, and before generated values are produced. If it can't be formatted (empty text, non-NFC text, or an integer outside ±(2^53−1) in the default numeric mode), the command is rejected as `Contract`. Nothing is appended for it, and earlier facts in the same cascade are kept. (part of #407)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.101.0)): Specification routes run at v8:
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.101.0)): `given` and `when append` events with a route are placed on that stream.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.101.0)): `then` compares the route, and `no stream` requires an unrouted fact.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.101.0)): A routed fixture or example is typed by its source's identifier, even when no command produces the event.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.101.0)): Composite stream ids run at v8. Parts are encoded in declaration order with the escaped `|` separator, and specifications compare them part by part. (part of #462)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.101.0)): Route lines in typed specification examples now run as well. Redelivery locators keep their route lines refused until reaction redelivery is admitted. (part of #491)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.101.0)): MCP `propose-rename` now moves a source's identity entries together with its streams. Readiness reports routes as admitted, and the semantic diff marks a reordered, retyped or added stream id part as contract-breaking. (part of #407)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.100.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.100.0)): `pii special <category>` marks GDPR Art. 9(1) special-category data (health, genetic, biometric, racial or ethnic origin, political opinions, religious or philosophical beliefs, trade union membership, sex life or sexual orientation), and `pii criminal` marks Art. 10 criminal-offence data (part of #557)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.100.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.100.0)): `secret scope subject|namespace|global` states the Chronicle encryption scope for an operational secret (part of #557)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.46.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.46.0)): Generated specification classes carry the Screenplay specification's `description` as their escaped `<summary>`, on both render paths. Specifications without a description render as before. (#249)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.99.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.99.0)): `on click`, `on double click` and `on select` interactions can choose a whole action list from the item's state, with ordered `when` branches and an optional `otherwise` that use the guarded-action condition language. This works in screens, named behaviors and component outlets, in both compilers, the editors and the Invoicing sample (#447)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.99.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.99.0)): Decision record 0048 specifies guarded interactions and how the old `where` guard migrates (#447)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.60.0)): The embedded event model board shows each slice's specifications as cards for the given events, the action, the expected events and the expected errors. Everything else a specification states, such as the caller, the clock, generated values, `then returns` responses, read models given or expected absent, query results, operations and stream routes, appears in the card header. (#3080)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.60.0)): The generated Screenplay derives a screen's `title`, `table` and `column` directives from `DataPage`, `DataTableForQuery`, `DataTableForObservableQuery` and `Column` imported from `@cratis/components`, and an `action` from `CommandDialog`. A table or action is written only when it names a query or command the slice declares. (part of #3049)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.45.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.45.0)): Scoped projections can set literal values in `every` blocks at levels without a `join`. Stage writes each literal onto every `from` at that level, where Chronicle applies every-mappings. Levels that combine an `every` literal with a `join`, literals that overlap a parent or child path of a `from` mapping, and literals in `all` blocks stay refused with a typed reason. (part of #117)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.59.0)): Generated queries carry the method's XML summary as their `description`. (#3048)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.59.0)): Query parameters with a default value are emitted as optional filters, and nullable returns, including through `Task<T>` and `ValueTask<T>`, are emitted as optional returns. Default values and nullable collection elements, which Screenplay cannot state, are reported as `SP0019`. (#3048)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.59.0)): With `ScreenplayOptions.AuthoringOnlyConstructs`, a query with a body gets a `performer` naming the file that holds its read logic. By default the performer is left out and reported as `SP0019`, because performer-backed queries are not executable. (#3048)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.59.0)): A query that receives Arc's `QueryContext` is reported as `SP0041`: it is a host dependency, so no `from $context` source is inferred. (#3048)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.59.0)): Policies written with `RequireAssertion` that compare a claim to a property of the command are emitted as claim conditions matching the artifact property, keeping `&&`, `||` and grouping. A comparison against the identifier becomes `matches subject`. (#3050)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.59.0)): Policies on a keyed snapshot query that compare a claim to the key supplied by the caller are emitted as `matches subject`. (#3050)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.44.0)): A Stage host running the semantic engine can run the model's specifications on request with `POST /stage/semantic/specifications/run`, optionally limited to a list of semantic scopes. Each run uses fresh, isolated state in a separate SpecRunner process, which the Stage image now ships, so it never touches the live session's world or event store. Specifications Stage cannot run exactly come back as typed `Unsupported` results with the capability and the reason, never as passes. (part of #23)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.98.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.98.0)): A `when redelivered <Event> to <Reaction>` locator can now state the redelivered event's route, with `stream Source.Stream` (plus a `streamId` or composite part block) or `no stream`. Two given events that differ only in their stream can then be told apart. A locator matches only when exactly one given event definitely fits. (part of #490)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.98.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.98.0)): Typed event examples can now carry a route. A step that uses the example can replace that route as a whole, and the route's origin (authored, example or override) appears in the effective specification API and in MCP `find-fixtures`. (part of #491)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.96.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.96.0)): Positive typed screen source case for component bindings, master/detail templates, command forms, dialog outlets, navigation, package profiles, icons and presentation hints. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.96.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.96.0)): Browser, MCP, CLI, Studio and Stage harness expectation contracts with machine-checkable assertions and explicit pending version vectors for final released-package parity. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): Rendered commands and read models carry their Screenplay `documentation` block as escaped `<remarks>` in the generated C#, like events already do. (#243)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.95.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.95.0)): `screenplay test [path] [--filter <address>] [--format text|json]` runs a model's specifications with the reference runner and reports each as passed, failed, unsupported or unbound, with expected and actual events, read-model state, command responses and query results for failures; it exits 1 when any specification fails and 3 when nothing failed but something could not run (#377)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.95.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.95.0)): MCP `run-specifications` runs specifications selected by address, semantic id or scope, with the same outcomes and failure details, in revision-pinned pages (#377)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.94.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.94.0)): Typed screen bindings for data context, query results and component outputs, including null behavior, update mode, expected value metadata and invalid raw-text preservation. (part of #536)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.94.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.94.0)): Screen package component, toolbar, navigation route, form column, template metadata and profile icon authoring across the C# and TypeScript compilers, Monaco keywords, VS Code grammar, samples and documentation. (part of #536)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.94.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.94.0)): MCP authoring coverage for creating the screen release fixture and editing typed component bindings and dialog/navigation actions with revision-checked proposals. (part of #537)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.41.0)): Scoped projections that clear a nested object and later re-create it render again, now that the pinned Chronicle kernel stores this shape correctly. Composite-key lookups, `FromAll`, literals in `every`/`all` blocks and root join removals stay refused. (part of #117)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.10.0)): Shared rendered binding scope for Scene React trees, with typed component-output property resolution, preserved cleared values and stale-query abort support. (part of #69)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.10.0)): Generic design-time package context with canonical Scene state, diagnostics, permissions, capabilities and edit/action submission for package designers and property editors. (part of #70)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.10.0)): Command form package-side designer and property editor registrations that execute through generic design-time boundaries and preserve the native command form runtime contract. (part of #71)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.10.0)): Renderer-side destination resolution for stable navigation identity, bound deep-link parameters, outlets and dialog actions. (part of #74)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.10.0)): Package host resolution diagnostics for duplicate bundles, dependency/version conflicts, forbidden design-time imports, network assets and duplicate runtime singletons, with resolved layout, template, theme, icon and asset outputs. (part of #75)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.10.0)): Category and scope metadata on business list, live list, master/detail and command form blueprint templates for package-backed galleries. (part of #73)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.93.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.93.0)): The compiler packages now include a shared stream id formatter: `SemanticStreamIdFormatter` in .NET and the exported stream id functions in TypeScript. It formats single-value ids and encodes and decodes composite ids, using the same rules in both languages. A shared set of vectors checks both. (part of #407)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.92.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.92.0)): `ScreenCompositionCorpus.V1` with single-file, folder, reordered and relocated source forms, screen behavior probes, and pinned backend ESM admission diagnostics for the screens-release authoring fixture. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.9.0)): Typed binding contracts for data context, query result, component output and literal sources, with resolver diagnostics for cycles, missing components and type mismatches. (part of #69)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.9.0)): React component output scope for element-to-element bindings, including lifecycle cleanup for cleared selections. (part of #69)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.9.0)): Package design-time metadata and optional React design-time bundles for previews, designers, property editors, display renderers and canonical edit actions. (part of #70)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.9.0)): Command form descriptors for auto/manual mode, responsive columns, field widths and deterministic field generation through the native Arc form runtime. (part of #71)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.9.0)): Stable destination, outlet and navigation metadata for hierarchical screens, dialogs, authored routes and toolbar/navigation chrome. (part of #74)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.9.0)): Frontend package host configuration for approved bundles, profile resolution, assets, design-time loading policy and duplicate runtime singleton diagnostics. (part of #75)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.9.0)): Business blueprint pattern and package-hosting documentation for embedded and standalone rendering hosts. (part of #73)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.91.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.91.0)): Specifications accept a `description` line that says which rule or behavior they witness (#392)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.91.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.91.0)): Modules, features, slices, commands, read models and reactions accept a fenced Markdown `documentation` block, like events. It is report-only authoring metadata: it never changes the executable model, folder merge keeps the first file's block and reports conflicting blocks, and the MCP `declaration-details` summary returns `description` and `documentation` for every declaration that has them (#392)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.91.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.91.0)): New diagnostics PLAY0558 (malformed documentation block) and PLAY0559 (conflicting module or feature documentation across files) (#392)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.90.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.90.0)): MCP `semantic-diff` compares two exported model revisions (`export-workspace` snapshots) with the same paged structural, identity and dependency output as a proposal's semantic diff, and refuses unreadable, incompatible or stale revisions (#456)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.90.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.90.0)): MCP `find-specification-obligations` lists, for every command, read model, policy and constraint in a module, feature or slice, the specifications its structure calls for (success, each rule and authorization denial, constraints, removals and more) and whether a matching specification exists (#390)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.90.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.90.0)): MCP `find-modeling-smells` asks advisory questions about generic update or CRUD names, events that repeat another command's event shape, and commands or read-model properties with many events; it never changes compilation results (part of #393)
- **cli** ([Release v3.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.37.0)): `cratis render` writes authored Screenplay `description` text as XML doc comment summaries on generated commands, events, read models, queries and types, and event `documentation` as remarks. Metadata the renderer cannot use, such as text with characters invalid in XML, blocks rendering with `CLI-RENDER-005`. When the documents cannot be read as a Screenplay workspace, for example a file with an upper-case `.PLAY` extension, the model renders without the comments and `CLI-RENDER-006` says why. (#278)
- **cli** ([Release v3.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.36.0)): `cratis screenplay validate <path> --scope <Module>[.<Feature>[.<Slice>]]` checks one module, feature or slice and the declarations that directly reference it, with the same selection and exit-code rules as the `screenplay` tool; the whole application's error and warning counts are always reported alongside, in the console and in JSON, and `--scope` combines with `--check` (#274)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.88.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.88.0)): Event streams can declare a composite id: a `streamId` block with two or more named parts. Command routes and specification routes then map each part by name, as `part = value`. Both compilers parse, check and print these, and so do the typed syntax, MCP, the web editor, VS Code and the event model board. Single-value stream ids are unchanged. Composite routes are not executable yet, like every route today (PLAY0268). (#462)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.8.0)): Component packages can declare a checked mapping from their independent Scene catalog to a Components renderer, with diagnostics for incompatible identity, ABI, profile, slots and capabilities. See [manifest authority](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/components-package/manifest-authority.md). (#27)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.8.0)): Layouts, screen templates and dialog templates support optional semantic types, custom browse categories and scope restrictions, with shared C#/TypeScript validation for application hierarchy and recursive slot compatibility. Application shells remain layouts, and templates remain optional for outlet-only composition. (#72)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.38.0)): A converted `ReadModelDefinition` names the projection its read model was built from (`SelectedProjectionName`) and lists the other projections the slice declares (`IgnoredProjectionNames`), so an importer can tell people which projection was used. Which projection is chosen is unchanged. (#218)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.37.0)): Screenplay `csharp` and `file` policies render as Arc authorization when their body passes Stage's pure-code checks. The body sees the caller (authentication, roles and claims, with roles kept separate from claims), the command identifier or query key, and the time Arc received the command or query as `PolicyContext.Occurred`. Opaque and portable policies compose in authored order, and an authorization that reaches an opaque policy without a receipt time or subject denies. Bodies that read the tenant, the untyped artifact or identity names are refused with `STAGE-ESM-015`. (part of #57)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.35.0)): `eventSequence.Prepare(event)` serializes an event once with the store's real event serializer, including polymorphic types and additional-information providers, and returns a `PreparedEvent` snapshot that append and verification both reuse. (#4510)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.35.0)): `AppendPrepared(eventSourceId, preparedEvent)` appends a prepared event, and `AppendPrepared<TEventSource>(...)` does the same through a registered event source, resolving routing and concurrency scope the way `Append<TEventSource>` does. (#4510)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.35.0)): `eventSequence.VerifyContent(sequenceNumber, preparedEvent, eventSourceId)` reports whether the complete content of a stored event is `Equal`, `Different` or `Unavailable` compared with the prepared event, without returning the stored content to the caller. Erased, unreadable and revised content is `Unavailable`, as is content on SQL storage, where revision state is not yet tracked. (#4510)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.35.0)): Verification compares content across every stored generation of the event type and reports `Unavailable` when any generation cannot be compared without losing precision. (#4510)
