- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.35.0)): The Workbench exposes a `VerifyContent` command and generated proxies for content verification. (#4510)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.35.0)): Documentation for preparing, appending and verifying events, including the limits of verification: https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/events/appending.mdx (#4510)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.87.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.87.0)): A public .NET API for scoped validation: `ScopedDiagnostics.TryValidate` in `Cratis.Screenplay.Mcp` takes a file or folder path (or the sources in memory), a module, feature or slice address and the completeness checks to run, and returns the scope's diagnostics, its direct dependents, the affected scopes, unresolved event consumers and the whole application's error and warning counts. An unknown or ambiguous scope and an unreadable path are reported as errors. The `screenplay` tool and the MCP `diagnostics` tool use the same implementation (#533)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.86.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.86.0)): The `propose-source` MCP tool proposes whole `.play` documents as text (create, replace, remove, move and rename a document key), validated as authoring source with identity continuity and reviewed and applied like any other proposal. Text that does not parse returns `SourceParseFailed` with located diagnostics and no proposal (#385)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.85.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.85.0)): Warning PLAY0557 when a reaction's `on refused by authorization` branch invokes an authorization-gated command: reactions have no invoking identity yet, so the reference runner always refuses the command and the branch always fires, while Arc runs reactor commands as the system. Reported by both compilers, in the Monaco editor and in VS Code workspace files (#489)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.36.0)): `claim matches subject` and claim comparisons with a property work against Date and DateTime values. The claim must equal the value's canonical text exactly as Screenplay writes it (`yyyy-MM-dd` for a Date; the round-trip format with `Z` for UTC for a DateTime), independent of culture; any other spelling never matches. This applies to generated applications and to Stage's own specification runner. (part of #57)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.84.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.84.0)): The MCP `propose-rename` tool now renames event sources and event streams. Renaming a source updates the source in every command route and specification route that names it. Renaming a stream updates only the routes to that source's stream. Existing `id` pins are kept exactly as written, and none are added. A rename is refused when the new name is already a source, when it is already a sibling stream, or when the source is declared in more than one place. (#467)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.83.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.83.0)): The web editor and VS Code now complete `depends on` targets in module and feature bodies. They list the sibling features, the siblings of each enclosing feature, the root modules, and qualified paths to features in other modules, nearest first. After a typed module name and a dot, they list that module's features. The container itself, its ancestors and descendants, and targets it already declares are left out. (#494)
- **cli** ([Release v3.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.35.0)): `cratis screenplay validate --check <names>` runs Screenplay's opt-in structural completeness checks (`data-bindings`, `input-surfaces`, `field-origins`, `query-keys`, `event-consumers`, `navigation`, the codes `PLAY0530`–`PLAY0537`, or `all`). The option can be repeated, its findings are warnings that fail only with `--warnings-as-errors`, and the checks are skipped with a note when the model has errors. It combines with `--executable`. (part of #274)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.35.0)): Specifications can use Screenplay `example` declarations, including step overrides and inline assignments, on every Stage path: rendered specifications, the in-memory runner and the event-model runtime all see the same values as when they are written out. (part of #209)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.82.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.82.4)): An editor diagnostic support reference lists the diagnostics the TypeScript compiler and the editors share, and the checks that still need the `screenplay` tool or the MCP `diagnostics` tool (part of #495)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.58.0)): The Screenplay generator writes a reactor as a declarative reaction, with `produces` or `invokes` and mappings from the triggering event, when its handler unconditionally returns an event, a command, or a literal collection of either, and Chronicle runs it for every such event. Reactor scenarios of such reactors become `when append` specifications. Everything else keeps its `file` reference. (#3040)
- **cli** ([Release v3.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.34.0)): `cratis screenplay validate` and the embedded MCP server accept policy negation (`not role`, `not claim`, `not authenticated` and `not (...)`), which selects ESM v7, and warn with `PLAY0546` when `not` applies to a claim whose target can be absent or is not text. `cratis render` rejects such a model with the blocking `CLI-RENDER-004` diagnostic, because the bundled renderer does not render it yet. (#271)
- **cli** ([Release v3.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.34.0)): Screenplay models can declare `depends on` for modules and features, define reusable `example` values for specifications, give a screen `action` conditional alternatives, and route specification events with `stream`. `cratis screenplay validate` checks them; specification event routes and reaction `on refused`, `when redelivered` and `then no events` are not executable yet, so `cratis screenplay validate --executable` and `cratis render` report `PLAY0268` for them.
- **cli** ([Release v3.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.34.0)): The embedded MCP server's `diagnostics` tool takes `scope` (one module, feature or slice) and `checks` (opt-in completeness checks); `cratis screenplay validate` does not offer these yet. The server also adds a `semantic-diff` view to `read-proposal`, a dependency map on the event model board, dependency declarations in `dependency-graph` and `declaration-details`, routing data and effective example values with their origin in `find-fixtures`, examples in search, references and rename, routing data in `find-references` and `dependencies`, and opens other registered Git worktrees of the same repository.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): Policies can use `not`, with Screenplay's fail-closed meaning: a claim comparison that cannot be decided (a missing, null or non-text target) stays undecided under `not`, and an undecided policy denies. This applies to generated applications and to Stage's own specification runner. (part of #209)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): Rendered C# carries Screenplay `description` as each declaration's `<summary>` and event `documentation` Markdown as `<remarks>`, copied as escaped text, for commands, events, read models, queries and types. Declarations without a description keep their generated summary unchanged. (#178)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): `CratisRendering.WithAuthoringMetadata(profile, compilation)` adds a model's descriptions and documentation to a render profile, so a render host that has the Screenplay compilation can include them when planning from the executable model. (#178)
- **cli** ([Release v3.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.33.0)): `cratis screenplay desktop install|status|update|uninstall` manage the Screenplay MCP server in Claude Desktop and ChatGPT Desktop as regular subcommands, with their own help, shell completion and `llm-context` entries. `--model-root` is offered only by `install` and `update`. (#240)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.81.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.81.0)): Policy conditions accept `not <condition>`, including `not role "…"`, `not claim … matches …` and grouped conditions; `not` binds tighter than `and`, which binds tighter than `or`. A claim comparison whose target is missing, null, an absent subject or not text is unknown, `not` keeps it unknown, and a policy whose result is unknown denies access. Warning PLAY0546 flags `not` over a claim whose target can be absent or is not text. Negating a policy reference or an opaque predicate is refused. (#431)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.81.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.81.0)): Models that use `not` select ESM v7, which gains a `not` policy condition in this release; models without it keep their version, bytes and revision. Consumers that read v7 must admit the new condition kind, and older strict v7 readers reject it. (#431)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.81.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.81.0)): Declare `example <Name> : <Event|Command|ReadModel>` once and use its name wherever that type goes in `given`, `given readmodel`, `when`, `when append`, `then` and `then readmodel`, overriding values with the step's indented lines or one inline assignment such as `given AcmeInvoice total = 5000`. Examples expand before binding, so the executable model is the same as writing the values out; a property missing from both the example and the step is reported at the step. Examples cannot state event source or stream lines; state those on the step. (#427)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.81.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.81.0)): MCP `find-fixtures` shows effective fixture values with their origin (authored, example or override), examples appear in search, references and dependencies and can be renamed safely, and .NET embedders get expanded specifications with the same origins. Monaco and the VS Code extension complete examples and show effective values on hover; the Invoicing, Commerce and TimeTracking samples use them. (#427)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.81.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.81.0)): A screen can offer one action that runs one of several commands depending on the selected item: `action "<label>"` with ordered `when <condition> execute <Command>` alternatives and an optional `otherwise hidden` or `otherwise execute <Command>`. The first alternative whose condition holds decides, a condition on a missing or null field does not match, and when none holds the fallback runs or the action is hidden, as it is when no item is selected. Running the selection needs renderer support. (#432)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.81.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.81.0)): A reaction's `invokes` can declare what happens when the command is refused, with ordered `on refused` branches (optionally `by validation`, `by constraint [Name]` or `by authorization`) that `acknowledge` the refusal or produce events using `$refusal.reason`, `$refusal.constraint` and `$refusal.message`; specifications can use `when redelivered <Event> to
- **Screenplay.CritterStack** ([Release v0.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay.CritterStack/releases/tag/v0.26.0)): Generated models link commands to the events their handlers append through Marten `Events.Append` and `StartStream`, and through Wolverine aggregate handlers (#80)
- **Screenplay.CritterStack** ([Release v0.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay.CritterStack/releases/tag/v0.26.0)): Public diagnostic codes `WolverineDiagnosticCodes.ProductionLinkOmitted` and `CritterStackDiagnosticCodes.MixedSliceKindsSplit` identify omitted production links and split feature slices
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.34.0)): Publish open alert incident gauges by event store, namespace, observer, event sequence, condition and severity, with periodic sampling, stale-snapshot suppression and owner failover. (#4533)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): Policies that check only a role or only a claim render as Arc policies. Signed-in callers with the role or claim are allowed; everyone else, including callers who are not signed in, is denied. Roles are read through the identity's role claim type. (part of #57)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): `claim matches subject` works against Uuid subjects. The claim must equal the Uuid's canonical text exactly (lower-case, hyphenated), as in Screenplay's reference semantics, in generated applications and in Stage's own specification runner; a missing claim or any other spelling denies. (part of #57)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): Reducer bodies can read `context.Tenant` and compare it with `TenantId.Default` and `TenantId.NotSet`. (#185)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): When a code body uses the authored camelCase name of a generated member, the compile error now names the generated PascalCase member and points at the `.play` or attachment line and column. (#185)
- **Screenplay.CritterStack** ([Release v0.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay.CritterStack/releases/tag/v0.25.0)): Authored enums are declared as Screenplay enumeration concepts (#76)
- **Screenplay.CritterStack** ([Release v0.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay.CritterStack/releases/tag/v0.25.0)): Wolverine `SlicePattern` handlers and Marten `FetchManyForWriting` streams are recognized
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.51.0)): Transactional appends retain routing, subject, occurrence time, tags and concurrency scopes through commit in enrollment order, and reject conflicting scopes for one event source before enrollment. (#204)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.57.0)): Generated Screenplay specifications state `then returns` when an Arc command scenario asserts the command's response against a literal, for scalar responses and for asserted fields of record responses. Commands without generated values keep their `returns` beside successful scenarios. Generated values still cannot be pinned from Arc specs, so commands with them keep their previous form. (part of #2887)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.56.0)): `ScreenplayOptions.MaximumExecutableModelVersion` (MSBuild `CratisEmbeddedScreenplayMaximumExecutableModelVersion`, for example `6.0`) keeps generated Screenplay documents below a given executable model version. Below 7.0, generated identifiers, generated values and command responses are left out with `SP0052`, so tools that do not admit ESM v7 yet, such as `cratis render`, accept the document. (#3056)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.55.0)): The Screenplay generator binds every document it generates, not only compiles it. Binding errors that are not known limits of the executable model are reported as `SP0056`: a warning when you generate yourself, information inside an application's build so it never fails the build. Embedded generation binds the document that holds the whole application. (#3039)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.54.0)): Generated Screenplay documents declare each read model as a `readmodel`, with its properties, its XML summary as `description`, and its source `file`. Projections and keyed queries therefore resolve the read model they name. Each read model is placed once per document, in the slice that owns it. Scoped embedded documents import it from the document that declares it. A read model that cannot be declared faithfully is left out and reported with `SP0057`. (#3047)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.80.0)): MCP `find-fixtures` lists the event source and stream, stream id or `no stream` stated on specification events. `find-references` and `dependencies` on an event source or stream now include the specifications that route events to it. (part of #457)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.80.0)): The web editor completes `stream`, `streamId` and `no stream` under specification events and their stream targets, shows hover help, and lists `PLAY0547`–`PLAY0551`. VS Code highlights the route lines and lists the same findings. (part of #457)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.80.0)): The event model board shows the routes a specification states in its header. (part of #457)
- **cli** ([Release v3.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.32.0)): `cratis screenplay validate --executable` also binds the model into an executable semantic model and fails with its binding diagnostics, such as `PLAY0268` for event sources and streams, when it does not bind. It never renders, writes files or needs a container runtime, and its JSON summary reports `executable`. A document a root file imports from above its folder reports `CLI-VALIDATE-001`, and a document the binder cannot represent reports `CLI-VALIDATE-002`. (#243)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.79.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.79.0)): Modules and features that state `depends on` are now checked against the dependencies Screenplay infers from their slices. Containers that declare nothing get no new findings. (part of #416)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.79.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.79.0)): A dependency the model has but the container doesn't declare is a warning (`PLAY0552`), one per container and producer module, naming what to declare.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.79.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.79.0)): A declaration nothing uses is information (`PLAY0553`), and two containers that declare each other get information (`PLAY0556`).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.79.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.79.0)): Each container is held to its own declarations, including the slices of its sub-features. References whose owner is ambiguous never raise a warning.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.79.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.79.0)): The MCP `dependency-graph` tool has a `declarations` view: each declaration with its status, and each counted edge as declared, provisional or undeclared, with evidence capped by `evidenceLimit`. `declaration-details` gains a dependencies view for modules and features. (part of #416)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.79.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.79.0)): VS Code and the web editor list the declared-dependency findings and offer `depends on` in module and feature bodies. (part of #416)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.53.0)): Analyzer `ARCCHR0015` warns when a command's `Handle()` returns a nullable event, directly or inside `Task`, `ValueTask`, `Result` or `OneOf`. A `null` event either succeeds without appending anything or fails with an unexplained error. A code fix rewrites the handler to `Result<TEvent, ValidationResult>` with a rejection. See [ARCCHR0015](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/chronicle/code-analysis/ARCCHR0015.md). (#3036)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.78.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.78.0)): Specification `given`, `when append` and `then` events can state their event source and stream with the child lines `stream <Source>.<Stream>` and, for a keyed stream, `streamId = <value>`. A `then` event can assert that it is unrouted with `no stream`. A routed `given` or `when append` states `for`, checked against the source's identifier type. The executable model refuses these lines with `PLAY0268` until a version admits event routes, so they can be authored, printed and checked now. (part of #457)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.78.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.78.0)): New diagnostics: `PLAY0547` for a malformed or misplaced route line, `PLAY0548` for a route under `when <Command>`, `PLAY0549` for a stream that doesn't resolve or a stream id that doesn't fit it, `PLAY0550` for a missing or mistyped `for`, and `PLAY0551` for a route that contradicts the command under test. (part of #457)
- **cli** ([Release v3.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.31.0)): `cratis screenplay generate --authoring-only-constructs` also writes Arc constructs that are not executable yet: operations and the systems they use, event sources and streams with command routes, and `reads` and `require` recovered from `Provide()`. A document using them has no executable model. Providers other than Arc warn with `CLI0014`. (#262)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.77.0)): The MCP App and the VS Code event model board can switch from the board to a dependency map. The map shows modules as columns in suggested story order and features inside them, with other bounded contexts as outer nodes. Each edge is labelled with its kinds and the number of slice pairs behind it. By default it shows the dependencies that shape the story, and toggles add the others. Selecting an edge lists its slice pairs and the references behind them; in VS Code each reference opens its source. The map can be used with the keyboard and a screen reader. In the MCP App it follows the current or proposed model. (part of #415)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.76.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.76.1)): Completion offers only what the scope you are in can hold: the root, a module, a feature, each slice type, and the bodies of read models, reducers, forms, contributions, behaviors, personas, authentication, seeds, themes, UI profiles and layouts.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.76.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.76.1)): An empty `command` expands into its identifier, the properties of the event it most likely produces, and the `produces` block, as ghost text and as a completion.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.76.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.76.1)): Pressing Enter after a block header indents to the body, in the Monaco and VS Code editors.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.76.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.76.0)): Modules and features can state what they depend on with `depends on <Module>`, `depends on <Module>.<Feature>` or `depends on <SiblingFeature>`, one target per line, after `description`. The statement is authoring metadata and does not change the executable model. (part of #416)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.76.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.76.0)): Targets resolve inside out: sibling features first, then the siblings of each enclosing feature, then modules. A target that is the container itself, one of its ancestors or descendants, or that names nothing raises a warning (`PLAY0554`). A repeated target is kept once with a warning (`PLAY0555`).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.76.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.76.0)): Declarations from several files accumulate. Layout expansion writes them only in the owning file. Renaming a module or feature through the workspace rename updates the targets that name it.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.75.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.75.0)): Structure completion in the Monaco and VS Code editors: on an empty line inside a command, specification or form, ghost text suggests the `produces` block, the `given`/`when`/`then` skeleton or a `field` per command property, built from your own declarations.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.75.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.75.0)): The VS Code extension registers the language keywords with the Code Spell Checker extension, so `readmodel` and the other constructs are no longer flagged as unknown words.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.52.0)): Commands mark their event source `identifier` and route productions with `for`, using the same rules as Arc's runtime. The generator reports ambiguous or unreadable identities and productions routed to another event source. (part of #2887)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.52.0)): An event produced by exactly one unconditional, complete command production anywhere in the application is written inline as `produces event`, and the embedded event model board shows inline events. (part of #2887)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.52.0)): Events carry their XML summary as `description`, their remarks as `documentation`, and a rename pin for generation-1 events whose stored name differs from the type name. (part of #2887)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.52.0)): Unconditional `Must(Method)` validator rules become named rules that point to their implementation file, where no specification exercises the command. (part of #2887)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.52.0)): Event scenarios become `when append` specifications, and specifications state the event source of their events with `for`. A scenario whose event sources cannot be stated faithfully is left out and reported. (part of #2887)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.52.0)): Identifiers and values a handler creates with `Guid.NewGuid()`, and scalar and record command responses, are written as `generated` values and `returns` where the executable model admits them and specifications do not depend on them. (part of #2887)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.52.0)): `ScreenplayOptions.AuthoringOnlyConstructs` (MSBuild `CratisEmbeddedScreenplayAuthoringOnlyConstructs`) also writes constructs that are not executable yet: operations and the systems they use, event sources and streams with command routes, and `reads` and `require` recovered from `Provide()`. (part of #2887)
- **AI** ([Release v7.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.13.0)): `IAIChatClients.For(purpose, target)` gives a pooled chat client for an agent the application defines itself, dispatched through the same pool selection, concurrency limit, cooldown and usage recording. Applications that keep their agents in their own read models no longer need to resolve providers themselves.
- **AI** ([Release v7.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.12.0)): `IAIChatClients.For(purpose)` hands out an `IChatClient` for an agent that goes through the same provider-pool dispatch, least-burnt member selection, per-provider concurrency limit, rate-limit cooldown and usage recording as `ILanguageModel`. It supports multi-turn, streaming and tool calls (wrap it with `UseFunctionInvocation()`). (#439)
- **AI** ([Release v7.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.12.0)): `IProviderChatClientFactory` opens the vendor chat client for a configured provider, so the vendor SDK stays behind one seam.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.74.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.74.0)): The TypeScript compiler package infers the same dependency graph as the .NET compiler and the MCP `dependency-graph` tool, so the boards can show it: slice edges by kind, implied module and feature edges, groups that depend on each other, unresolved references and a suggested story order. (part of #414)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.73.0)): New MCP tool `dependency-graph` shows how slices, features, modules and other bounded contexts depend on each other, inferred from the model with no new language.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.73.0)): Each slice edge has a kind: uses facts from, reacts to, decides from, asks, shows, used in specifications (hidden by default), or outside the model (contract imports).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.73.0)): The tool implies edges between modules and features, including mixed levels, with counts and the slice references behind each edge.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.73.0)): It reports groups that depend on each other.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.73.0)): It suggests a story order that keeps the authored order unless a dependency forces a change. The suggestion is never applied. (part of #414)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.71.0)): VS Code and the web editor now list `PLAY0516` (a slice uses an event from a slice drawn after it) and `PLAY0517` (modules or features that use each other's events) as information in the problem list. Neither finding fails a build. (part of #413)
- **Components** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): `SchemaEditor` has a `layout` prop. `'table'` is the existing drill-down table and stays the default; `'tree'` shows nested properties inline and applies every edit through `onChange`, with `readOnly` for display.
- **Components** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): Concept-typed properties (`concepts`, saved as `x-concept`), a required toggle (`allowRequired`) and a key toggle (`allowKeyProperty`, saved as `x-key`, one key per object). The required and key toggles work in both layouts.
- **Components** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): Extension points for products that attach their own features to a schema: `header`, `footer`, `isPropertyProtected`, `validatePropertyName`, `onPropertyRenamed` and `onPropertyRemoved` in both layouts; and, in the tree layout, `renderPropertyLeading`, `renderPropertyAccessory`, `renderPropertyDetails`, `renderPropertyTypeBadge`, `getPropertyRowState`, selection through `selectedPropertyId` and `onPropertyClick`, and a controlled mode with a callback for every edit.
- **Components** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): New optional `SchemaEditorLabels` entries for the added controls. Existing labels are unchanged.
- **Components** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): The property model and the schema conversion helpers (`jsonSchemaToProperties`, `propertiesToJsonSchema`, `setKeyProperty`, `setRequiredProperty` and the rest) are exported from `@cratis/components/SchemaEditor`.
- **Components** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): Documentation for choosing a layout and for the extension points: https://github.com/Cratis/Components/blob/main/Documentation/SchemaEditor/index.md
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.70.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.70.0)): MCP `read-workspace view=repairs` and `propose-repair` now offer a typed repair for `PLAY0516`, a slice that uses an event from a slice drawn after it. The repair moves the producing feature, slice or module, or an import line, ahead of the consumer. When the order comes from a glob, it adds an explicit import of the producer's file before the glob, which keeps importing new files. A repair is offered only when it fixes the finding without introducing new ones and leaves the model, identities and file placements unchanged; on models that do not compile to an executable model yet, that is proven on the source. (part of #413)
- **cli** ([Release v3.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.29.0)): `cratis direct login`, `logout`, `status` and `use <tenant>` provide browser-based Direct sign-in, server-side token revocation, live identity status and tenant reauthorization. Sign-in uses the public `cratis-cli` client with authorization code and PKCE, and discovers the issuer from Direct or accepts an explicit `--issuer`. (#205)
- **cli** ([Release v3.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.29.0)): Direct credentials are isolated from Chronicle contexts and stored in the operating system credential manager. Unix users can explicitly choose owner-only plaintext storage with `--insecure-file-store`; status and logout honor each credential's recorded store without exposing tokens. (#205)
- **cli** ([Release v3.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.29.0)): Direct authentication validates callback state and issuer, binds stored credentials to their issuer, serializes token refresh across processes and bounds HTTP requests. Issuers require HTTPS except for loopback development servers. (#205)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.69.0)): Report when a slice uses an event declared in a slice drawn after it on the event model board: `PLAY0516` for a projection or named reaction trigger whose event comes from later in the authored order, and `PLAY0517` once for a group of modules or features that use each other's events. Both are information diagnostics, so `--warnaserror` still passes, and the C# and TypeScript compilers report the same findings. (part of #413)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.8.0)): Projections can build children of a derived-type family: a `children` collection of an interface with exactly one registered derivative uses that concrete type for its fields, key, parent key and mapping tags, and writes `_derivedTypeId` on every child `From`, so the family codec reads the child back even when the kernel adds it from a keyed update after removal or before creation; the C# client stamps only the creating event. The fluent `projections.Children` accepts the same family with the derivative's builder, and a derivative's `key`, `no-auto` and `not-projected` tags do not turn another read model that holds the family as a whole property into a projection. See [project derived children](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/blob/main/Documentation/derived-codecs.md#project-derived-children). (#27)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.51.0)): Add `Cratis:Arc:Introspection:IdentityDiscovery` to disable user, tenant and identity-schema discovery independently of the catalogs. Set it and `Enabled` to `false` to remove all discovery endpoints without discovery authentication checks or exposure warnings, while keeping `/.cratis/me` available. (#3037)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.50.0)): Events can carry structured named tags (`NamedTag`, a validated name and value) with the `namedTags` append option, per entry in `appendMany`, and inside units of work. Read them on `EventContext.namedTags` for reads and reactor and reducer deliveries. Appends without named tags are unchanged. (#206)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.50.0)): Single appends support named tags with registered event sources. Batches and unit-of-work commits reject non-empty named tags combined with any registered-source entry with `NamedTagsWithRegisteredEventSourceNotSupported`, preventing routing loss until the [kernel limitation](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/issues/4603) is resolved. Empty named-tag arrays remain supported.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.50.0)): `EventScenario` and `ReadModelScenario` reject non-empty named tags; `ReactorScenario` validates and records named tags on returned side effects.
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.7.0)): Generated `contracts/alerts` and `contracts/eventsources` packages, plus the `EventSource` fields on append requests and event contexts, from the Chronicle 19.32.3 protocol. The SDK does not set `EventSource` or expose alerts and event-source registration yet.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.68.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.68.2)): The canonical corpus includes a source rejection vector for a generated value supplied as specification input. (#421)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.68.0)): Generated command values are admitted as ESM v7, selected only by generated properties, responses, generation fixtures or return expectations; unchanged models retain their ESM version, bytes and revision. Reference execution uses deterministic fixtures and reports `Unsupported(IdentityAllocation)` when reached generation has no fixture. Consumers must explicitly admit or reject v7 separately for reading, reference execution, rendering and reverse recovery; a package upgrade does not grant admission. (part of #300)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.68.0)): Scalar and authored-order record command responses are available only on acceptance, with `then returns` assertions, paged MCP export, editor guidance and Invoicing examples. Operations, streams, handlers and exact mode remain independently unadmitted; UI response-name continuations and end-to-end navigation require downstream support. (part of #303)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.68.0)): Generated values do not exist yet when a command is authorized or validated: policies, the implicit subject, rules and requirements that reference one report `PLAY0273`, and a generated property on a concept with validation rules reports `PLAY0268`. (part of #300)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.68.0)): `SemanticExecutionRequest.GeneratedValues` supplies deterministic generated fixtures, and `SemanticAccepted.Response` exposes the accepted command response. `RegisterProjectCorpus.V7` provides source-backed v7 conformance vectors, with expected responses in `CanonicalCorpusSpecificationExpectation.Response`. (part of #309)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.6.0)): Binary values: `[]byte` fields (and named byte-slice types, with one optional pointer) are written as base64 strings that match the C# client, including `+` escaped as `\u002B`, `""` for empty values and omission of nil values. Supported shapes are fields in event payloads, including fields inside nested objects, top-level read-model fields, and `From` AutoMap copies between binary fields (part of #64)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.5.0)): Configure trusted gRPC stats handlers on SDK-owned connections, including reconnects, while keeping handler lifetimes application-owned. (#53)
- **AI** ([Release v7.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.11.0)): A rule that `.cratis/` holds configuration and the shared AI corpus only: Screenplay models, source, generated code, specifications and documentation are never produced there.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.67.0)): Without a project, the Screenplay MCP server now works in `Documents/Screenplay` in your home folder, creating it on demand, so Claude and ChatGPT desktop need no model folder. The `.play` files are ordinary files there.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.67.0)): When the host offers a project root, the server serves the folder already holding the project's `.play` files, else its `Source` or `src` folder, else a new `Screenplay` folder in the project.
- **cli** ([Release v3.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.28.0)): `cratis screenplay mcp` now finds the model itself. It serves the folder that already holds the project's `.play` files, otherwise `Source/` or `src/`, otherwise a new `Screenplay/` folder at the project root. It also works in a project with no `.cratis/ai.json`.
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.3.0)): Capture webhook sources accept inbound authorization: `captures.Webhook(path, captures.WithBasicAuth(...))`, `WithBearerToken` or `WithOAuth`, readable through `Source.Authorization()` and `Definition.Authorization()`. Authorization never appears in the capture declaration text, and is redacted from `fmt` and `log/slog` output, including when held in private fields (part of #37)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.2.0)): Connection lifecycle hooks: `WithOnConnected` and `WithOnDisconnected` register `ConnectionHook` callbacks that receive a `ConnectionEvent` (generation, connection ID, selected address and cause) on every connect, reconnect and close. Hooks run on a client-owned dispatcher in transition order, never delay reconnects, contain panics and always pair a delivered Connected with exactly one Disconnected, including on `Close` (part of #62)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.2.0)): Custom load balancing: `WithLoadBalancer` accepts your own `LoadBalancer` that picks the endpoint for every connection attempt, including SRV-discovered candidates. A failed selection returns `*LoadBalancerError` and is retried with the normal reconnect backoff. The client borrows the balancer and never closes it, and rejects it when combined with a `loadBalancer=` connection-string option or `WithGRPCConnection` (part of #62)
- **Arc.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Command and query pipelines with execution contexts, service scopes, command filters, response value handlers and Arc-shaped results, usable without a dependency-injection container or a Chronicle connection (#3) (#6) (#10) (#11) (#16)
- **Arc.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Flat command operations with commit-aware compensation, plus traditional-handler envelopes and request-merge adaptation (#30) (#35)
- **Arc.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Application builder and net/http hosting with endpoint discovery, following Arc's snapshot command and query HTTP contracts; static files and an SPA fallback are served through `Builder.Handle` (#17) (#36)
- **Arc.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Observable queries over Server-Sent Events and WebSocket, the query hub, emission guards and ownership verified across two application instances (#21) (#27)
- **Arc.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Validation, authentication, authorization, identity and tenancy, including scoped policy and validator factories (#7) (#11)
- **Arc.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Validation severity filtering, query paging and sorting metadata, and paging metadata that is cleared when HTTP publication fails (#28)
- **Arc.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Test scenarios and a task-board HTTP conformance host for specifying command and query behavior (#18)
- **Arc.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Source preview: Chronicle integration (`github.com/cratis/arc.go/integrations/chronicle`) with a shared provider for Chronicle deliveries and Arc operations, command operations with compensation, reactor-issued commands, and optimistic decision reads (#14) (#19) (#29) (#31)
- **Arc.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Source preview: MongoDB integration (`github.com/cratis/arc.go/integrations/mongodb`) with read-model rendering, concept codecs, snapshot queries and bounded change-stream observation (#22)
- **Arc.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Source preview: `arc-gen` generates typed Handle, Provide, query, validator and policy adapters and TypeScript proxies for the Cratis frontend packages, including Int32 enum parsers and validator and policy registration directives (#13) (#20) (#32)
- **Arc.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Source preview: `arc-gen` writes an OpenAPI 3.1 document and an opt-in Screenplay `.play` metadata file; both are files only, with no HTTP route and no embedded viewer (#24) (#37)
- **Arc.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Source preview: `arc-vet` diagnostics that report Go authoring mistakes beyond generation-time signature checks (#34)
- **Arc.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Backend operation diagnostics and protected query health (#33)
- **Arc.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Source preview: recipes for mounting Arc under Chi, Gin and Echo, golang-jwt authentication, browser-origin CORS and CSRF protection, OpenTelemetry tracing and
- **AI** ([Release v7.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.9.0)): A completeness self-check ("do not cut corners to save tokens or effort"), a per-turn protocol and fuller phase-transition and reasoning-note guidance in `cratis-screenplay-modeling-lifecycle` (#497)
- **AI** ([Release v7.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.9.0)): The business-question lens and an automation-chain audit as review checks in `cratis-screenplay-model-review`, which now has 89 checks (#497)
- **AI** ([Release v7.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.9.0)): Build prompts and conformance checklists for hand-written slices in `cratis-application-slice-conformance`, including a check that the running application really subscribes each reactor (#497)
- **AI** ([Release v7.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.9.0)): A candidate inventory and UI-walk progress reporting in `cratis-screenplay-legacy-extraction` (#497)
- **AI** ([Release v7.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.9.0)): Good and bad scenario forms, with a runnable invoicing example and a design-mode companion that declares its stored-state rules, in `cratis-screenplay-scenario-coverage` (#497)
- **AI** ([Release v7.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.9.0)): An Acknowledgments section in the README crediting Martin Dilger, Nebulit GmbH and TrogonStack (#497)
- **AI** ([Release v7.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.8.0)): `IAIProviderCapacities` reports an `AIProviderCapacity` for any provider: its usage windows (5-hour, daily, weekly or monthly, each with the share used and when it resets), one `Headroom` figure from 0 to 1, `CanStartWork`, and `AvailableAgainAt`. Claude subscriptions, ChatGPT/Codex subscriptions, GitHub Copilot and the Z.AI coding plan report their real windows. API-key providers fall back to the configured usage capacity, or count as unmetered.
- **AI** ([Release v7.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.8.0)): The `CapacityFor(providerId)` query and its `@cratis/ai` proxy (`Providers/Capacity`) let a frontend show a provider's windows and headroom.
- **AI** ([Release v7.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.8.0)): `ICanReportAIProviderCapacity` lets an application add a capacity source for another vendor.
- **AI** ([Release v7.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.8.0)): New options `AIProviderOptions.MinimumHeadroomToStartWork` (default 0.02) and `CapacityFreshness` (default 5 minutes). Observations are cached, and the last good reading is kept when a vendor fails to answer.
- **AI** ([Release v7.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.8.0)): `ProviderRateLimit.ResetIndicatedBy(reason, now)` reads the reset time out of a limit message, such as "resets Oct 9, 2am (UTC)" or Claude Code's `|<epoch>` suffix.
- **AI** ([Release v7.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.8.0)): `PoolSelectionData.HeadroomByProvider` ranks pool members by remaining headroom.
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): Ten Screenplay method skills, which you install with the `cratis/screenplay` profile:
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): `cratis-screenplay-modeling-lifecycle`: phases, gates, verdicts and the model-first decision.
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): `cratis-screenplay-toolchain`: versions, tools and diagnostics.
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): `cratis-screenplay-discovery`
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): `cratis-screenplay-slice-design`
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): `cratis-screenplay-streams-and-consistency`
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): `cratis-screenplay-automations-and-translations`
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): `cratis-screenplay-scenario-coverage`
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): `cratis-screenplay-model-review`
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): `cratis-screenplay-legacy-extraction`
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): `cratis-screenplay-render-and-gap-fill`
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): `cratis-application-slice-conformance` checks hand-written or gap-filled slice code against its contract, element by element and spec by spec. It installs with `cratis/specifications/dotnet`.
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): Three agents for model-level work: Screenplay Modeler, Screenplay Reviewer (read-only) and Screenplay Renderer. They use your session's model.
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): `cratis-arc-react-page` maps Screenplay screens, forms and queries to Arc React pages, and traces every field on screen to its source.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.66.0)): An experimental, opt-in VS Code bridge offers the C# `PLAY0166` repair (declare a missing produced event) and the `PLAY0478` repair (explicitly route to the command identifier) through a configured, trusted Screenplay executable. Each repair shows read-only previews of every source and identity change and is applied only after an explicit final confirmation. (part of #341)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.66.0)): Repairs are refused while affected files have unsaved or untitled changes, and a stale review is refused at Apply time when source files, identity state or referenced attachments changed on disk, even if no file notification arrived. (part of #341)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.66.0)): When an Apply outcome is uncertain, the extension preserves your typing and never retries; a definite refusal after dispatch is treated as uncertain too. Read-only inspection of the retained root and explicit consent through **Screenplay: Resume repairs after inspection** allow the next repair to start a fresh, revision-checked session without reusing old tokens or reviews. (part of #341)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.66.0)): The VS Code guide documents how to enable and use the repair bridge, inspect recovery state and resume repairs safely. (part of #341)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.65.0)): A named validation rule on a command can carry an `implementation` block with ordered `hint` lines and, optionally, the rule's `file` or tagged code fence. A rule with only hints, or an empty block, records pending implementation intent. (part of #307)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.65.0)): Pending rules are valid source but report `PLAY0268` when bound. A rule whose implementation is attached binds exactly as before, and adding, editing or reordering its hints does not change the executable model, requirement identity or content hash. (part of #307)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.65.0)): Both compilers, the Monaco and VS Code editors, MCP inventories and typed authoring understand the new block, and workspace edits and refactorings keep each pending rule's obligation with its rule or refuse the edit. (part of #307)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Client foundation with pinned Chronicle contracts, connection supervision and registration lifecycle (part of #3)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Event-store cache eviction with `(*Client).EvictEventStores()`, borrowed scoped facades, client-wide slog routing and guarded two-phase client preparation (part of #62)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Fundamentals.Go concepts and shared scalars in event serialization (part of #13)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Strict ordered unit of work with participant and owner (part of #14)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Unique and event-type constraints with removers (part of #15)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Event-level model-bound declarations for unique, subject, index and descriptor metadata (part of #28)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Default JSON naming aligned across serialization, schemas and projection paths (part of #29)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Registered serialization codecs, including declared Int32 enum codecs and derived schema parity (part of #64)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Typed decoding of read-model paths through registered serialization plans (part of #50)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Read-model catalog registration, one-shot reads, collections and bounded reducer reads (part of #20)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Projections with model-bound and fluent declarations: mappings, arithmetic, global mappings, children, joins, nested objects, removals, context tags and typed nullable clears (part of #26)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Projection variants, global handlers, recursive children, source inference and preview (part of #27)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Typed projection initial values and artifact labels (part of #60)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Runtime artifact registration with atomic catalog publication and reconnect retention (part of #63)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Convention-driven reactors with replay policies, returned effects, rich effects and reliable delivery (part of #30)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Convention-driven reducers with nullable state, passive reads and replay lifecycle callbacks (part of #31)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Historical event generations with bidirectional migrations (part of #32)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Read-model watches, materialized windows and read-model reactors (part of #33)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Admitted decision guards with owned commits for protected decision reads (part of #34)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): PII and encrypted metadata with the complete subject-key lifecycle (part of #35)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Event seeding after observers start without duplicate startup appends (part of #36)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Event store subscriptions, webhooks, external services and captures (part of #37)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Public scenario test kit for events, reactors and read models, with strict projection subscriptions (part of #38)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Audited revise and redact operations for event history (part of #40)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Observer and jobs administration with explicit completion outcomes (part of #59)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Typed contextual and usual-action pattern queries (part of #61)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Ordered event enrichment, audit providers and a stable identity rename (part of #65)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Append notifications with origin identity and typed callback-based origin resolution (part of #47)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Checked sequence-number helpers (part of #18)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.64.0)): A top-level `numbers exact` preamble opts a file into exact numbers; its numbers are read, printed and transported as bounded decimals without rounding, in both the C# and TypeScript compilers. (part of #285)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.64.0)): New diagnostics for exact numbers: unknown, repeated, misplaced or nested numeric directives, numbers outside the exact decimal range, and files or import barrels that disagree on numeric mode (`PLAY0508`–`PLAY0513`). (part of #285)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.64.0)): Proposed decision 0024 describes the exact numeric source mode and its path to ESM v7. (part of #285)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.32.0)): Open alert incidents are materialized from the recorded alert transitions into incident rows stored next to the System event sequence, for MongoDB, SQL (SQLite, PostgreSQL, SQL Server) and in-memory storage. Redelivered or out-of-order transitions never overwrite newer state, and a cleared incident cannot be resurrected by a delayed raise (part of #4424)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.32.0)): `GetOpenIncidents`, `GetOpenIncident` and `GetOpenIncidentCounts` queries on the new Alerts service, scoped to an affected event store and optionally a namespace, with filtering by observer, condition and minimum severity, bounded keyset paging and counts grouped by namespace, condition and severity (part of #4424)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.32.0)): Every incident query reports whether the incident view is `Ready`, `CatchingUp` or `Degraded`, so an empty result is never mistaken for "no incidents" while the view is still catching up or has failed partitions (part of #4424)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.32.0)): Documentation for querying open alert incidents: https://www.cratis.io/chronicle/hosting/alert-incident-queries/ (part of #4424)

## Changed

- **Stage** ([Release v4.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.77.0)): The compliance rendering reference explains the mapping, the identity rule and its surrogate-identifier fix, and why concepts should be marked before their events are first published. Models without compliance render exactly as before.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.77.0)): Play and the Stage Host do not encrypt or redact values of personal-data concepts: the Host's own event store keeps them in plain text (see https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/issues/346).
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): Synced with Arc 22.76.0, Chronicle 19.51.0, Fundamentals 7.23.0, Specifications 4.1.1, Orleans 1.16.3, Components 4.26.4, Chronicle.TypeScript 6.51.0, Arc.TypeScript 0.57.2, Chronicle.Kotlin 6.13.0, Arc.Kotlin 7.7.0, Chronicle.Elixir 3.10.2, cratis CLI 3.53.1 and Chronicle.Mcp 2.0.0; each skill's verified sources name the release read (#620)
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): The standalone Screenplay pin moves to 4.141.0 with its published contract; Stage 4.74.0 to 4.76.0 capabilities are taught as standalone Stage, since cratis CLI 3.53.1 still bundles Stage 4.73.1
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): The Chronicle MCP skill states the exact effect of each Chronicle.Mcp 2.0.0 tool, its read-only default and the mutation profile, and warns that it targets the active `cratis` context
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): The CLI operations guard treats `observers remove` and `observers clear-partition-quarantine` as mutating commands
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): Concurrency guidance teaches decision reads (`IStreamReads`, `[ProtectedDecision]` with `DecisionRead<T>`, `IDecisionReads`) instead of advisory read-model injection
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): Scene packages are named `@cratis/scene.*` in the Stage scaffold tables
- **Arc** ([Release v22.76.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.76.0)): Cratis.Screenplay and `@cratis/screenplay-language` are updated to 4.139.0. (#3175)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.141.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.141.0)): Models with protected concepts now bind and run their specifications instead of failing with PLAY0268; markers do not change reference-evaluator outcomes, and free-text reasons stay report-only (part of #141)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.141.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.141.0)): PLAY0268 now refuses compliance on enum concepts and on concepts used as event-source or stream identities, and refuses an event that holds a personal or subject-scoped secret value together with an explicit `subject` mark until subject lineage is admitted (part of #141)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.141.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.141.0)): The MCP readiness text and editor hover documentation describe concept compliance as executable in ESM v13 (part of #141)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.76.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.76.0)): The Host world rebuilder maps a stored enum number to the member that declares it, so events stay readable when numbered members are reordered. Unnumbered enums keep declaration-order numbers, and unknown numbers, fractions and undeclared names are still refused. (#296)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.76.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.76.0)): The rendering reference explains explicit enum member numbers and keeps the append-only rule for unnumbered enums; the Docker and renderer-target guides list Screenplay 4.139.0 and ESM v1–v12. (#296)
- **AI** ([Release v7.34.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.34.1)): The Screenplay, Stage and Cratis skills no longer restate the current Stage scaffold dependency pins (Arc, Chronicle, Components, Scene, `@cratis/arc`) or the CLI's bundled Cratis.Arc.Screenplay version; they point to the toolchain and rendered-application version tables, so the next CLI or Stage release only updates those tables. Version-qualified claims such as "refused at Stage 4.73.1" stay as they are
- **AI** ([Release v7.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.34.0)): Capability statements across the Screenplay skills match Screenplay 4.139.0, CLI 3.53.1 and Stage 4.73.1: list and observable queries bind and run, descriptions render as code comments, ownership claims on text, Uuid, Date and DateTime subjects render, `navigate to` is the destination after a successful command, and public events and contributions follow the released language (part of #611)
- **AI** ([Release v7.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.34.0)): A reaction that both appends events and runs commands stays one modeled reaction; the skills explain that Arc 22.72.0 runs it, that Stage 4.73.1 refuses it, and that a hand-written reactor needs Arc 22.72.0 or later (part of #611)
- **AI** ([Release v7.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.34.0)): Legacy extraction warns that `generate` and `conform` evaluate projects through MSBuild, so they run against a trusted clone rather than the live legacy checkout, and describes Prologue `interpret --no-llm` (part of #611)
- **AI** ([Release v7.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.34.0)): Attribution for the agentic-engineer material now records the explicit consent of Martin Dilger and Nebulit GmbH (part of #611)
- **AI** ([Release v7.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.33.0)): The Screenplay toolchain skill pins the standalone Screenplay compiler to 4.139.0 and refreshes the published contract it checks against
- **cli** ([Release v3.53.1](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.53.1)): Bundles Stage 4.73.1 and runs `cratis/stage:4.73.1` by default. The release fixes the running Stage serving the Scene document with the .NET spelling of each enumeration, which left a dialog destination opening nothing. (part of #284)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.70.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.70.0)): The Screenplay generator now targets Cratis.Screenplay 4.135.0 (NuGet and npm).
- **Arc** ([Release v22.70.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.70.0)): An encryption scope other than the default is kept. A concept marked `[Encrypted(EncryptionScope.Namespace)]` or `[Encrypted(EncryptionScope.Global)]` together with `[NotAudited]` is written as `secret scope namespace|global` when authoring-only constructs are enabled. In the default executable output it stays a bare `secret`, and the new `SP0061` diagnostic names the scope the document does not state. (#3089)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.70.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.70.0)): `[PII]` on a property whose type is not a concept (for example `[PII] string Email`) is now reported with `SP0062` instead of being dropped silently. Screenplay classifies concepts, so introduce a personal-data concept to carry the marking. (#3089)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.70.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.70.0)): When `[PII]` on one property promotes its concept to `pii` everywhere, the new `SP0063` diagnostic names the other uses of that concept that were not marked. (#3089)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.70.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.70.0)): The `SP0058` and `SP0059` messages and the compliance section of the generator documentation now use the current `pii` and `secret` terms. (#3089)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.70.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.70.0)): Generator verification tolerates a known Screenplay admission-limit diagnostic only when the loaded Screenplay compiler still lists its code as an active error, so a retired or downgraded code fails verification instead of being silently accepted. (#3099)
- **cli** ([Release v3.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.53.0)): Bundles Stage 4.73.0 (Scene 4.21.0, Screenplay 4.137.0) and runs `cratis/stage:4.73.0` by default. The release brings module-level and screen-level navigation contributions, a screen's place in the module and feature hierarchy, toolbar items that open their destination, presentation hints (`icon`, `text`, `both`), and the Scene host's outlets, dialogs and deep links. (part of #284)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.73.0)): The Stage no longer keeps navigation state of its own: the primary screen, nested outlets, the open dialog and the `#/Screen?parameter=value` address belong to Scene's `useSceneHistoryNavigation` over `createHashSceneHistory`. An address that stands for an outlet destination restores the whole composition, and closing a dialog goes back to where it was opened from. (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.73.0)): Navigation contributions carrying `id`, `icon`, `presentation` or a `destination` are no longer refused with `STAGE-SCENE-UI-001`; Scene 4.21.0 honours them.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.73.0)): The generated-React browser harness proves it against the generated application: the module-level entry, a screen no entry lists nested under its module and feature, the toolbar with an icon-only item and a placement hint, and a toolbar dialog that opens and closes (70 assertions, 0 blocked).
- **Stage** ([Release v4.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.73.0)): Moves to Scene 4.21.0 and Screenplay 4.137.0. The frozen default scene, the frontend shell digest and the screens vector ledger are re-pinned, and the canonical typed composition is translated aga
- **cli** ([Release v3.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.52.0)): The CLI bundles Stage 4.72.0 (Scene 4.20.0). `cratis run` starts `cratis/stage:4.72.0`, which, being a built frontend, renders only the three shipped packages; the documentation says so and points at `cratis render --package-set` for custom packages. (part of #284)
- **cli** ([Release v3.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.52.0)): An unknown package in a `ui profile` (`CLI-RENDER-007`) now says a custom runtime package has to be approved with `--package-set`. (part of #284)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.49.0)): Every-event mappings (`FromEvery`, `FromAll`, model-bound `[FromEvery]`, and PDL `every`) now also apply to events handled only by child or nested-child collections. The mapped values are written to the parent read model the child belongs to. Opt out with `ExcludeChildProjections()` in the fluent builder or `exclude children` in PDL; model-bound `[FromEvery]` always includes children (#4725)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.72.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.72.0)): Rendered applications and the live Stage use Scene 4.20.0 (`@cratis/scene.*` and `Cratis.Scene.*`), which publishes the approved package set loader. (part of #236)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.72.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.72.0)): `Verification/generated-react-browser/run.sh` runs another scenario script when `STAGE_GENERATED_REACT_SCENARIO` names one, so a rendered application with a custom package can be held to its own assertions. (part of #236)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.21.0)): `extractNavigationItem` reads the whole authored item - `id`, `icon`, `presentation`, `destination` and `hierarchy` - instead of only the label, target, order and group. (part of #74)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.136.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.136.0)): `SemanticExecutionPlan.Compile(model, selectedSlices)` accepts an empty selection. It returns a plan that blocks only on application-level admission issues. Every slice issue is reported in `UnselectedIssues`, and the reference evaluator refuses execution that reaches those declarations. A scoped render whose slices were all removed no longer fails on unrelated slices. An unknown slice id is still refused. (#661)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.71.0)): Stage now builds against Screenplay 4.133.3 and Chronicle 19.45.0, and the Stage Host image runs `cratis/chronicle:19.45.0-development`. (#327)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.71.0)): Generated applications pin Cratis and Arc 22.68.0, an explicit Cratis.Chronicle 19.45.0 client and a `cratis/chronicle:19.45.0-development` kernel image. Apart from those version strings the scaffold is unchanged. (#327)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.71.0)): Generated read-model and query specifications dispose their `ReadModelScenario`, which is disposable from Chronicle 19.45.0, so generated applications keep building warning-free. (#327)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.71.0)): A navigation contribution that sets `id`, `icon`, `presentation` or a `destination` is refused with `STAGE-SCENE-UI-001`, naming the field, because the pinned Scene runtime does not honor it yet. Rendering these fields is tracked in https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/issues/329. (#327)
- **AI** ([Release v7.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.30.0)): The toolchain skill states that ESM v10 is released and frozen (decision 0066), that Screenplay 4.135.0 selects ESM v11 for exact whole-number literals while `numbers exact` and enum member numbers stay unadmitted, and that concept generation strategies (decision 0054) are not implemented yet (#593)
- **AI** ([Release v7.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.30.0)): The pinned standalone Screenplay tool and published contract move to 4.135.0, and the pinned cratis CLI to 3.50.0, whose default renderer (Stage 4.67.0) applies case tables and whole numbers as `long` from ESM v8 (part of #593)
- **cli** ([Release v3.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.51.0)): `cratis render --scope` no longer stops on execution-plan problems (`PLAN-*`) in slices outside the scope: they are reported as warnings naming the declaration, and only problems in the selected slices or application-level declarations block. A scope whose slices were all removed still re-renders to clean up its files. The CLI uses Screenplay 4.134.0 (#247)
- **cli** ([Release v3.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.51.0)): `cratis render` refuses models whose navigation contributions declare `id`, `icon`, `presentation`, `group` or `destination` with `CLI-RENDER-015`, instead of rendering without them, until the bundled Stage renderer supports them (#247)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.48.0)): In an opted-in projection, an event type named in the projection declaration language without a generation means the latest generation registered when the projection is registered, and the projection stays on that generation. To use a specific generation, declare the projection with the .NET projection builders (part of #4710)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.48.0)): Registering an opted-in projection fails when it names two generations of the same event type, the same rule that applies to opted-in reactors and reducers (part of #4710)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.69.0)): `QueryScenario.Perform` returns the first result of a query that returns `ISubject<T>` or `ISubject<IEnumerable<T>>`, the same result a plain HTTP request gets, instead of throwing `StreamingQueryNotSupported`. (#3153)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.69.0)): Disposing a `QueryScenario` also ends its observations, after any startup and in-flight processing have settled. `QueryObservation.Dispose()` starts disposal without waiting; use `DisposeAsync()` or dispose the scenario to wait for cleanup. (#3153)
- **cli** ([Release v3.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.50.0)): `cratis render` admits Screenplay ESM v9 and v10 models. Constructs the bundled Stage renderer does not support yet are refused with their own diagnostic instead of a generic version error: public events and event-target projections and reducers (`STAGE-ESM-032`), foreign origins and event-source captures (`STAGE-ESM-033`), reaction system identity (`STAGE-ESM-034`), reaction observer filters (`STAGE-ESM-035`) and per-production route overrides (`STAGE-ESM-036`). Reducer observer filters render. ESM v11 and later are still refused with `CLI-RENDER-004` (#303)
- **AI** ([Release v7.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.29.0)): The backend application profiles (`cratis/application/csharp`, `typescript`, `elixir`, `kotlin`, `java`, `arc-chronicle`, `arc-only` and `chronicle-dotnet`) now include the Screenplay method and language skills and the Screenplay MCP server, so model-first work can be proposed and done in application repositories. Installing them does not opt a repository in: that still takes a committed `.play` model under the model root or a configured `mcpServers.screenplay.root`, and with Cratis CLI 3.46.1 or later the server creates no model folder until the first write (#495)
- **AI** ([Release v7.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.29.0)): In Pi, when Screenplay comes in only through an application profile and no model root is configured, a missing Cratis CLI, a CLI older than 3.46.1 or a server that fails to start leaves the Screenplay tools inactive with one notice instead of an error; selecting `cratis/screenplay` (or a profile that already included it) or configuring a root keeps startup errors visible (#495)
