- **AI** ([Release v7.28.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.28.1)): The reactor rules and the reactor, slice-specification and C# specification skills teach the Chronicle 19.46.0 `ReactorScenario` behavior: a reactor handler's read-model parameter must be seeded, or declared absent with `Given.ForEventSourceId(id).NoReadModel<T>()`, otherwise the scenario throws `ReadModelNotSeededForReactorScenario` naming the type and key; a handler that throws synchronously surfaces its own exception; and `EventContext.Occurred` defaults to UTC. They no longer say the read model arrives as `null` (#599)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.46.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.46.0)): `EventContext.Occurred` defaults to UTC (`DateTimeOffset.UtcNow`) when no time is given, matching the time the kernel stores. Events seeded into `ReadModelScenario` and `ReactorScenario`, and the event context returned when you append without an explicit time, now carry a `+00:00` offset instead of the machine's local offset. The instant is unchanged (#4571)
- **AI** ([Release v7.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.28.0)): The pinned standalone Screenplay tool and published contract move to 4.131.0, and version statements across the Screenplay skills separate the current pins (CLI 3.46.1 bundling Screenplay 4.130.0 and Stage 4.52.0) from historical probes (#592)
- **AI** ([Release v7.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.28.0)): A skill may carry three to five evaluation tasks instead of exactly three (part of #591)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.133.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.133.4)): The browser native-controls harness expects the authored navigation label and the Workspaces > Tracking hierarchy, and reaches the list screen through that navigation link. Against CLI 3.48.0 and `cratis/stage:4.67.0` it passes 52 assertions with no blockers. (part of #173)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.16.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.16.3)): The jobs documentation now explains what happens to a job step's state when the step succeeds, fails, is stopped or is removed, that `PerformStep` works on a snapshot of the state, and how to deliver outside input such as worker callbacks to a running job through the job's own grain. See https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/blob/main/Documentation/concepts/jobs.md (#66)
- **cli** ([Release v3.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.48.0)): The CLI bundles Stage 4.67.0 and Screenplay 4.131.0. (part of #284)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.45.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.45.0)): A reducer with no dispatchable methods is no longer registered with the kernel. The client logs a warning naming the reducer and why each reducer-like method was skipped, and completion waits on that event sequence no longer time out waiting for it (#4726)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.133.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.133.3)): Specification cells in the MCP app and the VS Code board show only the item cards. The values, the caller and the reason for an expected error appear in a hover card, instead of as loose text under each card.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.67.0)): A model that authors no navigation entries keeps the flat list of screens as the menu. (part of #237)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.44.0)): In `ReactorScenario`, a reactor that declares `[EventSourceType]`, `[EventStreamType]` or `[FromEventSource<TSource>(stream)]` now handles only the given events that match its filter, as it would against a running kernel. A filtered reactor no longer handles events given without `OnStream` unless the default route matches its filter. Give those events with `OnStream` on the route the reactor listens to. Tag filters are not applied in the scenario
- **AI** ([Release v7.27.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.27.2)): The screens guidance separates the executed vector (CLI 3.43.0, Stage 4.52.0, Scene 4.15.0) from packages published since (Scene 4.17.0, Screenplay 4.133.1, Stage 4.64.0, CLI 3.46.1, Studio 0.152.2), and no longer calls the newer ones the verified latest. (part of #536)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.133.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.133.2)): ESM v10 is now listed as released. It first shipped in Screenplay 4.125.0, and its contract includes reaction system identity, production route overrides and observer `from` filters. The interoperability version table, MCP readiness text, editor help and sample notes no longer call it claimed or unreleased. A consumer that admits v10 must admit all three features. See https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/interoperability.md (#647)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.133.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.133.2)): Decision 0066: an executable semantic model version is frozen as soon as any package exposing it is published. New features take the next version when they are admitted. See https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0066-published-esm-versions-are-frozen.md (part of #647)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.133.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.133.2)): Decision 0054 (concept generation strategies and freshness) and decision 0065 (explicit enum member numbers) are accepted. Neither is implemented yet. (part of #615) (part of #643)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.65.0)): `STAGE-SCENE-ACTION-001` and `STAGE-SCENE-INTERACTION-001` now refuse only guards Stage cannot evaluate: anything other than `item.<field>` compared with a literal (`==`, `!=`, `>`, `>=`, `<`, `<=`, `contains`, `startswith`) combined with `and` and `or`, and any fallback other than `hidden` or an `execute` that names a command. Such a guard is still refused in runnable rendering, in the Scene a running Stage serves, and by the frontend runtime, which shows the action disabled with the diagnostic. See [Guarded actions and interactions](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/stage-rendering.md#guarded-actions-and-interactions). (part of #236)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.65.0)): A navigation started by an interaction is a step the browser can go back from, and its arguments become the target screen's parameters. (part of #237)
- **cli** ([Release v3.47.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.47.0)): The CLI uses Stage 4.62.0, and `cratis run` starts the matching `cratis/stage:4.62.0` container. That container's default engine refuses event sources, streams and routes with `STAGE-ESM-030`, so render routed models with `cratis render` (#290)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): `validateValue` checks an `Object` value that has `properties` field by field: it must be a record, every described field must fit its descriptor, required fields must be present and a field the descriptor does not describe is refused. An `Object` without `properties` still accepts any record or array. (part of #70)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): `validatePackageBundle` checks nested descriptors - duplicate fields, collections without item descriptors and enums without choices - at every depth, including inside collection items. (part of #70)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): Icon diagnostics also check icon fields inside a described `Object` value. (part of #70)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.133.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.133.1)): The dependency map now shows the same Cratis logo and rounded toolbar as the board, in the same place, with its content kept clear of both.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.133.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.133.1)): The map's edge level and dependency kinds moved from a bar above the map into the View button, so the board and the map each have their own view settings.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.133.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.133.1)): The toolbar buttons are fully rounded to match the toolbar, and property tooltips stay next to the card they describe, from `@cratis/event-models` 0.152.0.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.64.0)): Stage builds against Scene 4.16.0: the `Cratis.Scene.Engine` and `Cratis.Scene.Model` packages, the Stage frontend's `@cratis/scene.*` packages, and the `@cratis/scene.*` version a generated application pins all move from 4.12.0 together. (part of #236)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.133.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.133.0)): The canonical screens corpus now records the public CLI 3.43.0 with Stage 4.52.0 vector, the first whose render includes the generated React application on the Stage runtime. Every browser assertion passes, and the render audit passes on all 57 artifacts: they are deterministic and portable, profile rejection publishes nothing, and the rendered application recovers to every declaration in its source. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.43.0)): In `ReadModelScenario`, a reducer that declares `[EventSourceType]`, `[EventStreamType]` or `[FromEventSource<TSource>(stream)]` now receives only the seeded events that match its filter, as it would against a running kernel. A filtered reducer no longer receives events seeded without `OnStream` unless the default route matches its filter. Seed those events with `OnStream` on the route the reducer listens to. See [Seed routed events](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/testing/read-models/scenario.mdx#seed-routed-events)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.63.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.63.2)): Stage now gives each reason it refuses a reaction its own code, in the renderer, the Host and the semantic executor. Reactions are still not rendered or run (part of #207).
- **Stage** ([Release v4.63.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.63.2)): `STAGE-ESM-024` names the trigger kind: clock (interval or schedule), application trigger, startup, shutdown, or an opaque reaction body.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.63.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.63.2)): The new `STAGE-ESM-037` marks a trigger that both produces an event and invokes a command.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.63.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.63.2)): `STAGE-ESM-013` names the `$context.causedBy.*` member a reaction uses.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.63.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.63.2)): Event-triggered reactions report `STAGE-ESM-024`, saying that reaction rendering is not yet admitted.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.63.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.63.1)): The rendering reference now documents how generated enums are stored. Their numbers follow declaration order, so until Screenplay can give members fixed numbers (https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/issues/643), only append new members, and never reorder or remove existing ones.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.63.0)): Stage now uses Screenplay 4.131.0 and accepts ESM v10 models. Slices that use no v10 construct render and run as before. Each v10 construct is refused with its own code wherever Stage loads a model:
- **Stage** ([Release v4.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.63.0)): `STAGE-ESM-034`: a reaction's `runs as` identity.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.63.0)): `STAGE-ESM-035`: observer filters, `from` on reactions and reducers.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.63.0)): `STAGE-ESM-036`: route overrides on a single production.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.63.0)): `matches` rules now treat `$` as the end of the input, as Screenplay 4.130 does. The semantic executor, generated validators and legacy validators all match it, so a value with a trailing newline no longer satisfies a pattern ending in `$`. (#292)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.41.0)): Registering a pinned reactor or reducer fails when it handles a generation that is not registered for the event type, or two generations of the same event type (part of #4710)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.41.0)): With SQL storage, revising an event now records the revised generation, so pinned consumers receive the revision. Revisions made before this release cannot be told apart from the original content (part of #4710)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.62.0)): Stage now accepts Screenplay ESM v9 models. Slices that use no v9 construct render, run and specify exactly as they would at v8.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.62.0)): Each v9 construct is refused with its own code wherever Stage loads a model: the renderer, the Host, the semantic executor, rendered specifications, the legacy renderer, the eventmodel engine and the structural SpecRunner. This includes references from projections, reducers, constraints, reactions, captures, command productions and specifications.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.62.0)): `STAGE-ESM-032`: public events, and projections or reducers that target an event.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.62.0)): `STAGE-ESM-033`: events that come from another store, and `source events` captures.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.62.0)): `STAGE-ESM-024`: Translate slices, with or without a direction. This is unchanged. (#290)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.66.0)): Arc depends on Cratis.Fundamentals 7.23.0. (part of #3128)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.40.0)): Testing scenarios share one in-memory event store per scenario. The event log and any named event sequence you append to see the same data, and kernel services that used to throw `NotSupportedException` in a scenario now answer from that store. For example, the admin password status now reports that no admin password is required (#3434)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.40.0)): An event appended in an older generation is migrated through your registered event type migrations (`EventTypeMigration<TUpgrade, TPrevious>`) and stored in every generation, in `EventScenario` as well as in the events you seed into `ReadModelScenario`. Read models and reducers now see the migrated values (part of #3941)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.40.0)): `EventStoreForTesting` enforces closes-stream constraints, as `EventScenario` already did
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.40.0)): Concurrent appends to one event sequence in a scenario run one at a time, the way the kernel processes them. Two concurrent appends of the same `[Unique]` value can no longer both succeed
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.40.0)): An event type or generation your scenario did not discover still fails with `EventSchemaNotDiscovered`. A constraint type the kernel does not know now fails explicitly instead of being ignored
- **Stage** ([Release v4.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.61.0)): Whole-number properties, command inputs, events, read models, concepts, stream ids, sentinels and literals render as `long` for models at ESM v8 and later. Generated TypeScript stays `number`. (#263)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.131.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.131.0)): `semantic-diff` reloads both sides on every page, so a continuation after either side changed is refused as stale. `beforeWorkspaceJson` and `afterWorkspaceJson` keep working; combining one with the matching source object is refused (#558)
- **cli** ([Release v3.46.1](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.46.1)): The CLI uses Screenplay 4.130.0 (#311)
- **cli** ([Release v3.45.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.45.0)): The ownership manifest `.cratis-render.json` stores the semantic ids of each managed file and uses schema `2`. A destination rendered by an earlier CLI is upgraded on its next render (`--check` reports that one manifest rewrite with exit `6`), and an earlier CLI refuses to publish over a schema `2` destination. Generated files are unchanged (#246)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.130.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.130.0)): The Screenplay MCP server accepts a model root that does not exist yet, as long as its parent folder exists. Reading tools treat it as an empty model and create nothing; the folder and its `.screenplay` state are created by the first apply or recovery write. Hosts such as `cratis screenplay mcp --project-root` no longer have to create an empty model folder just to start the server (#633)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.130.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.130.0)): When the server discovers its root from the client's project or falls back to `Documents/Screenplay`, it no longer creates that folder on open; it is created on the first write (#633)
- **AI** ([Release v7.27.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.27.1)): The `cratis/codescene` profile docs now state that neither the Cratis CLI nor any harness registers the CodeScene MCP server: each user registers `cs-mcp` (or `npx -y @codescene/codehealth-mcp`) in their own user-level MCP configuration, never in committed project files (#589)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.58.0)): Stage and the applications it renders now use Chronicle 19.39.1 and Arc 22.65.3. In this release Chronicle registers event source definitions in the application's services, which routed commands need when they run.
- **AI** ([Release v7.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.27.0)): Pi rule delivery now honours `cratis/<profile>`-scoped rules: in a repository with `.cratis/ai.json`, such a rule loads only when it selects that profile or one nested under it. `application` and `framework` rules behave as before
- **Ante** ([Release v1.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Ante/releases/tag/v1.3.0)): The shell is served by Ante itself for `/`, `/index.html` and every application route, with `Cache-Control: no-cache`, instead of the static file middleware's default-files handling.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.17.0)): The Cratis Components toolbar button runs its attached interactions and opens its authored `destination` - a dialog or a slice screen - through the enclosing navigation host, or announces it with `cratis.scene.navigate` outside one. (part of #74)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.17.0)): The business pattern coverage matrix now states its prioritized decisions and the specifications that hold each one. See [Business pattern coverage](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/blueprints/pattern-coverage.md#decisions). (part of #73)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): The core `action` component resolves its argument mapping when it is invoked and adds the values as `arguments` to the `cratis.scene.command` event, so a host submits what the user typed. (part of #69)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): A property that declares `acceptedBindingKinds` accepts a binding expression of one of those kinds in place of its own value type, and refuses other kinds with the accepted ones named. A command form's `inputs` can now be bound to a table's `selectedItem`. (part of #69)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): The Generate fields action on a command form is disabled, and refuses to run, when the form's fields come from a binding, not only when they are authored. (part of #71)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): The `Cratis.Components` manifest no longer declares `commandFields` and `fieldWidths` as its own property editors. They are host editor kinds, so design-time hosts no longer report them as missing. (part of #70)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): Exposed template configuration leaves collection items past the owner's `maximumItems` out of the effective result and reports them as `contributionTypeMismatch`; they stay in the saved data. See [Exposing a template to its screens](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/blob/main/Documentation/editing/exposure-and-configuration.md
- **cli** ([Release v3.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.43.0)): `cratis chronicle login` no longer accepts a root certificate (PEM or DER) as `certificatePath`; the setting names a client certificate, as it already did for every other command (#198)
- **AI** ([Release v7.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.24.0)): Across the Screenplay skills, `description` holds a one-line summary with short status markers such as NOT enforced in the model today, and reasoning, assumptions and rejected alternatives go in `documentation`. (#538)
- **AI** ([Release v7.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.24.0)): The model-first opt-in reference names the same model root as the corpus rules: where the project's `.play` files live, else `Source/` or `src/`, else `Screenplay/`. (#510)
- **AI** ([Release v7.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.24.0)): The Stage skill states the Arc and Chronicle versions Stage 4.51.3 renders, and separates the protected-decision API from protection Stage emits. (#530)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.65.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.65.2)): Arc depends on Cratis.Screenplay 4.128.0 and `@cratis/screenplay-language` 4.128.0. (#3134)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.38.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.38.2)): Register a new generation only after every Chronicle server in a cluster has been upgraded to this release. A server on an older release can still overwrite stored content during the upgrade. See the [migration guide](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/migrations/index.md) (part of #4189)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.129.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.129.0)): A single explicit `key` decides a read model's identifier instead of inference from its queries, and binds to the same executable bytes as the inferred identifier. Composite keys and query `by` blocks are checked when compiling, and executable binding refuses them with PLAY0268 until https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/issues/599 admits them (#459)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.129.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.129.0)): The identifier-type check for a command route skips productions that carry their own route, and specification route expectations use each production's effective route (part of #302)
- **AI** ([Release v7.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.23.0)): The standalone Screenplay pin moves to 4.127.0; the `cratis` CLI stays at 3.41.0, and the skills say where its bundled Screenplay 4.114.0 differs.
- **cli** ([Release v3.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.42.0)): The CLI bundles Screenplay 4.127.0 and reads executable models up to ESM v9; `validate` and the Screenplay MCP server admit v9 models (#302)
- **cli** ([Release v3.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.42.0)): `cratis render` refuses ESM v9 models with `CLI-RENDER-004` until Stage can render public events (#302)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.128.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.128.0)): The canonical screens corpus now records the public CLI 3.41.0 with Stage 4.51.3 vector. Every browser assertion passes, and the render audit passes in full: renders are deterministic and portable, a UI profile naming an unknown package or a missing layout is refused with nothing published, and the rendered application recovers to the declarations its source makes. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.128.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.128.0)): The render audit no longer counts the `file` source reference a recovered read model carries as a member when it compares a recovered model with its source. (part of #168)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.128.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.128.0)): The screens release acceptance matrix now reflects this vector and the latest Scene, Stage, Studio, Screenplay and AI deliveries. See [Screens release acceptance](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/screens-release-acceptance.md). (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.56.0)): Stage builds against `Cratis.Screenplay` and `Cratis.Screenplay.CanonicalCorpus` 4.122.0. Models that use ESM v9 constructs (public events, event origin, translation direction, event-target projections and reducers, events-source captures) are refused with `STAGE-ESM-016` and plan no artifacts. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.56.0)): A `navigate` route or parameter is no longer refused with `STAGE-SCENE-UI-001`. An `outlet` on a dialog template still is, because a Scene dialog template declares no outlets. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.16.0)): Job grains use resource-optimized placement (CPU, memory and activation count) among the silos that host the job type. Cratis/StudioIssues#345
- **AI** ([Release v7.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.22.0)): Version pins move to standalone Screenplay 4.125.0 and cratis CLI 3.41.0, which bundles Screenplay 4.114.0, Stage 4.51.3 and `Cratis.Arc.Screenplay` 22.54.0. (#528)
- **AI** ([Release v7.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.22.0)): Event sources, streams and routes are described as binding and running in the reference evaluator (ESM v8); `cratis render` refuses models above ESM v7 with `CLI-RENDER-004`, and Stage refuses generated values and responses with `STAGE-ESM-028`/`STAGE-ESM-029`. (#528)
- **AI** ([Release v7.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.22.0)): A reaction that declares `runs as system role` with a role the gate admits is a trusted path, not a review defect. (#532)
- **AI** ([Release v7.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.22.0)): Discovery describes authored board order instead of name-sorted slices, `description` no longer has to be the first body line, `@pii` on an identifier is the PLAY0515 error, and skills that point to the Invoicing sample name its preview-construct exceptions. (#532)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.65.0)): A routed command states its route instead of the legacy `concurrency` block. An information diagnostic names each concurrency dimension that is dropped. (#3090)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.65.0)): Setting `ScreenplayOptions.MaximumExecutableModelVersion` below v8 keeps the previous output, with routes left out and reported. (#3090)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.65.0)): Arc depends on Cratis.Screenplay 4.124.1 and `@cratis/screenplay-language` 4.124.1. Generated documents use the current compliance keywords: `pii` for personal data and `secret` for operational secrets. (#3090)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.65.0)): When the generator leaves out a read model, it also leaves out the queries, screen data, productions and scenarios that depend on it, and says so, so the document still binds. A slice that becomes empty this way is reported. (#3090)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.127.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.127.0)): The MCP `semantic-diff` view and revision comparison now use the same comparison as the library API; their output is unchanged (#555)
- **AI** ([Release v7.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.21.0)): The `AuthorId` examples in the concepts and vertical-slices rules use `IGeneratable<AuthorId>` with `GenerateValue.Uuid()` instead of `Guid.NewGuid()`. (part of #576)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.54.0)): A command whose stream id cannot be formatted is rejected as a contract failure after its authorization and validation have run, without echoing the value. Specification runs that use routed fixtures are still refused with `STAGE-ESM-030`. The eventmodel engine and the structural SpecRunner keep refusing routes.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.125.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.125.0)): Binding no longer refuses `runs as` with PLAY0268, and MCP readiness no longer lists it as unsupported
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.125.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.125.0)): The TimeTracking sample grants its approved-week automation a dedicated `PayrollAutomation` role as the alternative to the Finance department claim, which human payroll callers still need
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.23.0)): `CorrelationId` implements `IGeneratable<CorrelationId>`, and `CorrelationId.New()` uses `GenerateValue.Uuid()` (part of #1176)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): Stage and the applications it renders now use Chronicle 19.37.8: the Chronicle client packages, the Host's bundled `cratis/chronicle:19.37.8-development` kernel, and the scaffold's pinned client packages and development image. This release keeps the event source on batch appends, formats event source ids the same way in every culture, and ships the routing conformance that event-source routing builds on. (part of #177)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): A `RenderBindingScopeProvider` rendered inside another now nests its data context, query results and component outputs over the inherited scope; previously it ignored them. (part of #69)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): Binding paths read only own properties and array indexes, so inherited members such as `constructor` and `length` resolve as absent, and a `literal` binding with a null value counts as absent, matching the C# engine. (part of #69)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): A package host is blocked with a diagnostic when the UI profile names a layout or theme no approved package provides, or a theme incompatible with the profile's packages. (part of #75)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): With network assets forbidden, a host now blocks any absolute URL other than `data:` and `blob:`, and protocol-relative `//host/...` references, not only `http` and `https`. The diagnostic names the assets. (part of #75)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.124.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.124.0)): PLAY0557 no longer fires when the reaction declares `runs as`, and its message now names `runs as system role` as the remedy. The reaction documentation is corrected: Arc runs a reactor's commands as the system only when the reactor carries `[ExecuteCommandsAsSystem]` (part of #383)
- **AI** ([Release v7.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.20.0)): Screenplay screen authoring, UI composition, render and Stage guidance now name the public vector that passes the browser acceptance run (CLI 3.40.7 with `cratis/stage:4.51.1` and bundled Screenplay 4.114.0) and the latest packages (Scene 4.14.0, Screenplay 4.122.0, Stage 4.51.3, Studio 0.141). (part of #536)
- **AI** ([Release v7.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.20.0)): Screen guidance no longer warns about Studio deploy failures or older-vector gaps that pass on the public vector; it keeps the fail-closed guarded Close and double-click refusals (`STAGE-SCENE-ACTION-001`, `STAGE-SCENE-INTERACTION-001`), the pending UI-profile and render-recovery work in Cratis/cli#301, and the pending Studio production proof. (part of #536)
- **cli** ([Release v3.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.41.0)): A profile naming an unknown package or a missing layout used to publish every artifact and exit 0. It now fails. Fix the profile if a render that used to succeed now reports `CLI-RENDER-007` through `CLI-RENDER-010`. (part of #284)
- **cli** ([Release v3.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.41.0)): `CLI0017` now names the reason a source path was refused and, when one path is responsible, that path, relative to the solution or project you read.
- **cli** ([Release v3.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.41.0)): The bundled Arc source adapter is now 22.54.0.
- **cli** ([Release v3.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.41.0)): The CLI now uses Stage 4.51.3, and `cratis run` starts `cratis/stage:4.51.3` by default.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.52.0)): Generated applications pin their frontend packages to the versions the Stage frontend builds with: `@cratis/arc` 22.58.2, `@cratis/components` 4.26.2, `@cratis/fundamentals` 7.22.8 and `@cratis/scene.*` 4.12.0. Re-rendering an existing application updates its `package.json` to these versions. (part of #236)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.52.0)): A generated application loads the PrimeIcons font, applies the Stage theme and serves styled PrimeReact components. It reads the PrimeUI license from `PRIMEUI_LICENSE` or `STAGE_PRIMEUI_LICENSE` at build time. (part of #236)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.38.0)): `Cratis.Screenplay` is updated from 4.16.0 to 4.111.0. Projection declarations that use syntax Chronicle cannot lower, such as refusal, list or object expressions, now fail with a compiler error instead of an exception (#4118)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.38.0)): The `WaitForRegistration` documentation now states what a successful registration guarantees: the kernel has registered the artifacts and subscribed their observers. To read your own writes from a materialized read model, wait for the append with `WaitForCompletion`, or use a passive read model (#4202)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.123.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.123.0)): Records `browser-native-controls-runtime-3.40.7-4.51.1`: CLI 3.40.7 (public Homebrew formula) + `cratis/stage:4.51.1` (the CLI's default) + bundled Screenplay 4.114.0. 51 browser assertions passed, none blocked or pending — the first exact public-release combination where the browser acceptance harness fully passes. Stage [#286](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/pull/286) (released as Stage v4.51.1) fixed the `AllWorkItems` route-resolution regression that blocked every earlier public-vector run.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.123.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.123.0)): Updates the MCP typed transcript vector to the CLI 3.40.7 rerun (same shape as the 3.40.4 run it replaces: 9 requests/responses, 37 tools, `executableReady` true).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.120.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.120.0)): A standalone `navigate to` directive in a screen now prints its `route`, `outlet` and `parameter` lines instead of dropping them. (part of #173)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.51.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.51.3)): When a query or command name matches no endpoint, or several distinct ones, Stage attaches no route instead of guessing. The one exception is an unkeyed collection binding whose query has no endpoint of its own: it reads its read model's `All<ReadModels>` route, which answers the same rows; a binding with a `by` parameter is never widened to the unfiltered collection. The element carries `routeStatus` (`unresolved` or `ambiguous`) and a `routeDiagnostic`, and `/stage/routes` lists ambiguous names under `diagnostics`. See [Which route a screen calls](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/urls.md#which-route-a-screen-calls). (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.51.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.51.3)): `/stage/routes` keys queries and commands by their own name only; full type names and endpoint display names are no longer included as keys. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **cli** ([Release v3.40.7](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.40.7)): The bundled Stage is now 4.51.1, so `cratis run` starts `cratis/stage:4.51.1`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.14.0)): The PrimeReact text, textarea, password, number, date, dropdown and checkbox components use `ariaLabel` as the control's accessible name, and every form field in the default blueprint's templates is named by its label. (part of #73)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.14.0)): The default blueprint's tables now pass their records as `rows` with an `emptyMessage`, so they show their data when rendered with PrimeReact. (part of #73)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.51.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.51.2)): The eventmodel Host engine now refuses a model that declares named event sources, streams or event-source routes with `STAGE-ESM-030` before it runs anything. The Host reports the model as unsupported and answers `/api` requests with 501, instead of running the model without its routes.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.51.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.51.2)): Structural SpecRunner runs of a model with named event sources, streams or routes now exit with code 1 and `STAGE-ESM-030`, and write no results, instead of reporting specifications that ignored the routes. Routes run only on the semantic engine. (#257)
- **AI** ([Release v7.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.19.0)): Quota and spend-limit errors (Anthropic `enforced_spend_limit_reached` and "credit balance is too low", OpenAI and Azure `insufficient_quota` and `billing_hard_limit_reached`, HTTP 402) are no longer retried against the same provider. Pool dispatch and pooled conversations park that provider and resend the same request to the next member with capacity. Ordinary 429 throttling still retries after `Retry-After` (#423)
- **AI** ([Release v7.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.19.0)): When every pool member is exhausted, the failure names each provider tried (#423)
- **AI** ([Release v7.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.19.0)): A Claude session that exits normally but ends with a quota-exhausted error result is now reported as failed instead of completed (#423)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.13.0)): Every built-in layout, screen template and dialog template in `@cratis/scene.blueprint.default` and `@cratis/scene.blueprint.components` declares compatibility with Scene `^4.13.0` and its package dependencies, Cratis attribution and the MIT license. The default shell and dashboard credit the Sakai arrangement they follow. (part of #73)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.13.0)): `validateNavigationDestinations` now uses the shared navigation diagnostic
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.119.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.119.0)): The screens release acceptance matrix now includes the CLI 3.40.5 and 3.40.6 browser runs, a regression in the work item list on Stage 4.50.1, the render audit results, Studio 0.139.0, and the access the production Studio automation needs. See [Screens release acceptance](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/screens-release-acceptance.md). (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **cli** ([Release v3.40.6](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.40.6)): The bundled Stage is now 4.50.1, so `cratis run` starts `cratis/stage:4.50.1`.
- **AI** ([Release v7.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.18.0)): Language model failures from Anthropic, Z.ai and OpenAI-compatible providers name the vendor, model, HTTP status, vendor error type and code, provider id and `Retry-After`. They also include the vendor's response body, capped at 2 KiB with credentials removed. The log entry carries the same fields as structured values. (#346)
- **Chronicle.Mcp** ([Release v2.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Mcp/releases/tag/v2.0.0)): **Breaking:** the server now runs in the `ReadOnly` profile by default and no longer registers `stop_job`, `resume_job` or `delete_job`. To keep using them, set `Cratis__Chronicle__Mcp__Profile=Mutation` and configure an authorization policy as described in the deployment profiles documentation.
- **Chronicle.Mcp** ([Release v2.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Mcp/releases/tag/v2.0.0)): In the `Mutation` profile the server refuses to start when the authorization policy is missing, expired or malformed. That includes wrong-shaped list settings, unknown keys, and a list split across configuration sources. Error messages name the setting, never its value.
- **cli** ([Release v3.40.5](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.40.5)): The bundled Stage is now 4.50.0, so `cratis run` starts `cratis/stage:4.50.0` and `cratis render` serves by-parameter collection queries filtered by their argument.
- **Components** ([Release v4.26.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.26.3)): Horizontal `CommandStepper` and `StepperCommandDialog` now show each step name below its step number, with names wrapping instead of being cut off, so every step stays readable in narrow dialogs (#411)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.118.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.118.0)): The browser acceptance harness now runs the CLI's default Stage image instead of a pinned 4.49.2, records the CLI and Stage versions it actually ran, and removes its Stage container afterward. (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.118.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.118.0)): The browser acceptance harness reaches the work item list through the authored navigation before checking native create, finds a renamed row by its current title, and matches field labels such as "Comment Id". The full native create, rename and add-comment flow now runs end to end. A `--self-test` mode checks these helpers, and `run-public-acceptance.sh` runs it first. (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.118.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.118.0)): The canonical corpus now pins the AddComment identifier expectation to the authored model: `commentId` is a client-supplied, non-generated identifier, so a native form must collect it. (part of #168)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.50.0)): Generated authorization policies are now rendered one file per protected operation, as `GeneratedPolicies/StagePolicy_{operation}.cs`. Opaque policy bodies and their typed contexts are rendered one file per use site. `GeneratedPolicies/Policies.cs`, `PolicyBodies.cs` and `TypedContexts/PolicyContext.cs` keep their paths, and their content no longer depends on which slices are rendered. Each policy registers itself, and `RegisterGenerated` applies the registrations in name order.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.50.0)): After upgrading, render a generated application at application scope once before rendering a single module, feature or slice, so that every policy moves to the new layout. See https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/guides/build-renderer-target.md. (#211)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.117.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.117.0)): Specifications that use `when capture` over an events source report a typed unsupported result in the reference evaluator.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.117.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.117.0)): Documentation, the MCP syntax-schema description, the contract golden, the Monaco hover and completion wording and decision 0051 describe the v9 admission. Consumers must reject v9 documents until they support it.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.49.11](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.49.11)): The Stage surface ledger now records exactly which query shapes render and which are refused with `STAGE-ESM-010`: snapshot lists and observable optional lookups are refused. It also records that the default Scene composition leaves list queries out.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.37.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.37.3)): Typed event source ids over `DateTime`, `DateTimeOffset`, `DateOnly` and `TimeOnly` are now written in their round-trip formats (for example `2026-10-23T14:15:16.0000000Z` for a UTC `DateTime`) on every culture. Streams written earlier under a date or time identity, or under a `decimal`, `double` or `float` identity on a culture that uses a decimal comma, now have different ids: append to or read from them through the old string id if you still need them
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.37.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.37.2)): `RegisterRequest` has a new optional `Registrant` field. Existing clients keep working; they simply no longer trigger retirement until updated.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.49.9](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.49.9)): Stage consumes the Screenplay 4.114 command-form and binding ABI for stable component ids, literal bindings, form generation mode, and form layout metadata. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.116.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.116.0)): Recorded the exact public CLI 3.40.4 and Stage 4.49.7 browser acceptance rerun as a new partial released vector while preserving earlier Stage 4.49.5 red evidence. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.116.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.116.0)): Tightened native dialog submission targeting so the browser harness submits inside the active dialog instead of clicking a background command button. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.116.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.116.0)): Updated the MCP released-vector record with the real AI #563 stdio transcript rerun on public CLI 3.40.4. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Ante** ([Release v1.2.11](https://github.com/Cratis/Ante/releases/tag/v1.2.11)): When a host reports onboarding outcomes (`Ante:HostOutcomeUrl`), the invitation, organization setup and self-service registration wizards no longer stop on a "check again / continue" screen once setup has published. They keep showing the spinner while Ante asks the host again every two seconds, and take the person to the application as soon as the host reports success. The manual screen is shown only when the host reports a failure, or has reported nothing after a minute.
- **cli** ([Release v3.40.4](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.40.4)): `cratis render` and `cratis run` now use Stage 4.49.7 and Screenplay 4.113, so generated screen applications use the released rich screen ABI and default to `cratis/stage:4.49.7`. (part of #284)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.115.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.115.0)): Recorded the exact public CLI 3.40.3 and Stage 4.49.5 browser acceptance rerun as a new partial released vector while preserving the older Stage 4.49.2 partial receipt. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.115.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.115.0)): Hardened the browser harness to continue after missing native form fields and record form-control descriptors when Stage exposes command controls without fillable inputs. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.115.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.115.0)): Captured the remaining Stage 4.49.5 runtime blockers for scoped comments, selected-row query identity, deep links, stale response discard, invalid native submit suppression, native form fields, and theme application. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.49.6](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.49.6)): Stage now maps the rich Screenplay screen ABI into typed Scene plans, including stable component ids, literal bindings, command-form generation and layout metadata, component presentation and exposed-value metadata, and literal-null preservation. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.49.6](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.49.6)): Stage now builds against Screenplay 4.113 and Scene Engine 4.12 so generated plans use the current public screen and keyed-query ABI. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **AI** ([Release v7.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.17.0)): Screenplay screen authoring and Stage render guidance now document the current Scene 4.12, Screenplay 4.114, Stage 4.49.5, CLI 3.40.3, and Studio 0.136.4 vector while keeping runtime, Studio deploy, and CLI Screenplay-package pairing caveats separate. (part of #536)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.114.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.114.0)): Screen and form documentation now describes the shared literal, stable-id and Scene geometry contracts that Studio, Stage and Conformance should consume instead of guessing syntax. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **cli** ([Release v3.40.3](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.40.3)): `cratis render` and `cratis run` now use Stage 4.49.5, so generated screen applications run with stable native command-form descriptors and authored command action routes exposed for native command forms. (part of #284)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.113.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.113.0)): Expanded the canonical screen browser harness into a two-item acceptance check that records selected-row identity, scoped comment query, stale-response discard, clear-selection, native command payload, projection persistence, dialog/deep-link, and theme/package rendering blockers instead of treating enabled controls as success. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.113.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.113.0)): Replaced the Studio production harness blocker stub with real new/import/save/reopen/export/Play operations that run when a Studio URL is provided and report operation-specific blockers otherwise. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.113.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.113.0)): Replaced the MCP folder-source blocker stub with a JSON-RPC transcript client for initialize, tool discovery, scratch workspace open, PreserveTrivia proposal, stale revision refusal, dropped-comments check, apply, reopen, and revision-change verification. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.113.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.113.0)): Refreshed the released screen vector matrix to keep Stage 4.49.2 partial evidence labeled partial and prepare a Stage 4.49.4 rerun once the compatible CLI release is available. (part of #173)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.37.0)): The capture engine now subscribes events captures to their inbox event sequences per namespace, alongside `api` sources. (part of #4640)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.112.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.112.0)): Decision 0049 is accepted and records the origin as a quoted store name and when `PLAY0614` applies.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.112.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.112.0)): `repair-capabilities` now lists five repairable codes, including `PLAY0614`.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.49.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.49.3)): Stage frontend now consumes the Scene 4.12 command-form geometry contract for generation mode, field placement, row and column spans, gaps, and width constraints, with Scene frontend packages pinned to 4.12.0. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.111.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.111.0)): Browser-native controls harness now records released Stage endpoint success, seeded list/detail/comment rendering, selection clearing, theme/menu rendering, and disabled native command blockers. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.111.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.111.0)): Released-vector results now move the browser status from missing Stage endpoints to partial browser pass with native command actions pending. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.111.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.111.0)): Screenplay interoperability documentation now describes the Stage 4.49.2 browser progress and remaining native action blockers. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.110.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.110.0)): Released-vector results now preserve the browser blocker as missing `/stage/routes`, `/stage/scene`, and `/stage/locales` responses instead of treating the running shell as a browser pass. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.110.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.110.0)): Screenplay interoperability documentation now names the browser harness and the Stage endpoint blocker for native-control conformance. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.36.0)): Appending a private event type to the outbox is now rejected, and the event log cannot be used as a publication target.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.36.0)): The event type registration contract carries visibility and origin; clients that omit them keep working.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.109.0)): Stage plan expectations now move from backend-only red evidence to authored Scene composition evidence while keeping browser-native controls, Studio production Play, and folder-source MCP revision editing pending. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.109.0)): Screenplay interoperability documentation now describes the authored-render released vector and its remaining guarded/browser/Studio blockers. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.108.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.108.0)): Stage plan expectations now preserve the released CLI/Stage red evidence instead of the previous refusal vector. (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.108.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.108.0)): Screenplay interoperability documentation now records the exact released-vector pass, red, and pending matrix for screen composition conformance. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **AI** ([Release v7.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.15.0)): Stage and render guidance now flags authored UI omissions and silent default composition as unsupported output rather than partial delivery. (part of #536)
- **cli** ([Release v3.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.40.0)): `cratis render` now bundles Stage 4.49.1 with Screenplay 4.105.0, so the canonical screen-composition corpus observable query surface and query-only specifications plan through the released renderer. (part of #284)
- **cli** ([Release v3.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.40.0)): `cratis run` now defaults to the matching `cratis/stage:4.49.1` sandbox image instead of the previous Stage 4.43 image. (part of #284)
- **cli** ([Release v3.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.40.0)): Screenplay executable validation now follows the bundled 4.105 binding behavior for imported root files and explicit event streams. (part of #284)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.49.0)): Stage now consumes Screenplay 4.105.0 and the matching canonical corpus release that includes executable observable collection query shapes for the screen-composition corpus. (part of #236)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.49.0)): The Cratis renderer now admits and emits the screen corpus read-model query surface for no-argument observable collections and by-parameter observable collections, including `AllWorkItems` and `CommentsForWorkItem`, while preserving protected event-source route refusals. (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.49.0)): The screen corpus backend surface now preserves command literal mappings, partial projection update events and non-colliding scoped projection implementation names, and now generates executable query-only specifications for the corpus listing, comments and details scenarios. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.106.0)): Screenplay interoperability documentation now distinguishes working-branch evidence from final released-vector execution for the screens corpus. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.48.0)): Event-source routes from Screenplay 4.101 are refused with `STAGE-ESM-030` on every rendering and admission path, including the legacy renderer and specification runs, instead of being dropped. (part of #250)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.48.0)): Screen navigation routes and parameters and dialog-template outlets are refused with `STAGE-SCENE-UI-001` instead of being silently dropped, and a semantic or legacy Stage host reports these scene refusals as unsupported instead of failing at startup. (part of #250)
- **AI** ([Release v7.14.5](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.14.5)): Questions about API keys and other credentials passed to an Arc command now reach the command skill, which explains `[NotAudited]` and, for retained secrets, `[Encrypted]` (#549)
- **AI** ([Release v7.14.5](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.14.5)): Questions about an observable query that Arc does not discover because it is declared as `Subject` or `BehaviorSubject` now reach the read-model skill (#549)
- **AI** ([Release v7.14.5](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.14.5)): The README explains that Claude Code and Codex limit how many skill descriptions they list, and how to install fewer profiles or raise Claude Code's budget (#549)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.47.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.47.0)): Stage now consumes Screenplay 4.101.0 and the matching canonical corpus, allowing the published screen-composition corpus to plan its Scene application surface instead of relying on the legacy RegisterProject byte fixture. (part of #236)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.47.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.47.0)): Screen conversion now preserves typed screen-language constructs for generated and live Scene parity: package-backed components, toolbars, icons, presentation values, nested component bindings, component outlets, template metadata and guarded actions. (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.47.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.47.0)): Unsupported backend event-source route semantics from the newer Screenplay compiler are explicitly rejected with `STAGE-ESM-030` until the Cratis renderer can generate them, preserving protected backend semantic refusals while admitting the screen surface. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.102.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.102.0)): CLI/Stage screen parity now preserves the released CLI 3.39.0 and Stage 4.43.0 refusal diagnostics as a red vector instead of substituting the legacy render for screens acceptance. (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.102.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.102.0)): Screenplay interoperability documentation now distinguishes the real screens corpus contract from the legacy RegisterProject compatibility evidence. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.61.0)): A concept used as a command identifier, event-source identifier, destination or stream id has its `@pii` and `@sensitive` annotations left off, because Screenplay rejects protected identities there; `SP0059` (Information) reports it and the identity and route stay in the document (#3079)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.61.0)): A scenario whose routes conflict with the declared source and stream types is left out with `SP0039` instead of emitting a guessed route (#3060)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.61.0)): Without `AuthoringOnlyConstructs`, a scenario that needs an event route is left out with `SP0060` (Information) rather than dropping the route silently. An unrouted occurrence in authoring-only output is reported with the same code (#3060)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.61.0)): A command's source and stream route is left out when one of its productions is routed explicitly or cannot be proven, and the source's identifier type is not a `Uuid`, so the document compiles under the stricter checks (#3060)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.101.0)): `then events in any order` now finds an assignment instead of matching greedily, at v8 only. Specifications at earlier versions keep their outcomes. (part of #407)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.101.0)): Some route mappings are still refused:
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.101.0)): A mapping that reads a property path, or a route on a handler command, keeps PLAY0268.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.101.0)): A mapping from a generated property, a stored source or stream name that collides, and a source stored as `Default` now report PLAY0273.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.100.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.100.0)): Personal data is marked with the keyword `pii` after the concept type (`concept Email : String pii`); `personal` is accepted as the same marker. Operational secrets are marked with `secret`, because "sensitive" reads as GDPR special categories (part of #557)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.100.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.100.0)): An unknown compliance marker is an error instead of being ignored (part of #557)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.60.0)): `SP0028` is now reported for each part of a screen that cannot be read, instead of once per screen. A column with a custom `body` or selection renderer, a column without a `field`, a computed title or header, a `DataPage` menu item, a `detailsComponent`, a table or dialog bound to another slice's query or command, and a component with spread properties are each left out and reported. A screen using none of the known components still reports it once. (part of #3049)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.45.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.45.0)): The refusal for root-level `remove via join` now points to the open Chronicle and Screenplay issues that block it (https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/issues/4263, https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/issues/563) instead of a closed one. (part of #117)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.44.0)): The semantic host's `/stage/semantic/admission` report lists specifications the run endpoint can execute as supported, instead of reporting every specification as unavailable. (part of #23)
- **cli** ([Release v3.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.39.0)): `cratis render` and `cratis run` now consume the released Stage 4.43.0 renderer package and default the run container to `cratis/stage:4.43.0`, carrying the native Arc CommandForm shell era and the shared Scene 4.10 typed-binding contracts. (part of #284)
- **cli** ([Release v3.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.39.0)): The bundled Screenplay compiler, MCP server and canonical corpus move to 4.93.0, the release Stage 4.43.0 is compiled against; render planning against the older compiler release failed while loading the renderer. (part of #284)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.98.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.98.0)): PLAY0526 now refuses route lines only in command and read-model examples, and its message says so. PLAY0548 no longer fires for a route under `when redelivered`. (part of #491)
- **AI** ([Release v7.14.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.14.3)): Ten skills that exceeded 500 lines or about 5,000 tokens now keep their decision rules, traps and version pins in `SKILL.md` and move secondary detail into reference files: the .NET, TypeScript, Elixir and Kotlin Chronicle clients, reactors, Arc commands in TypeScript, and the Screenplay capture, command-surface, event-modeling and specifications skills (#542)
- **AI** ([Release v7.14.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.14.3)): Reference files longer than 100 lines open with a contents list, so an agent that previews the top of a file still sees everything it covers (#542)
- **AI** ([Release v7.14.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.14.3)): Links to reference files say when to read them, and traps an agent must know before acting now sit in `SKILL.md` ahead of the material that depends on them (#542)
- **AI** ([Release v7.14.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.14.3)): Capability limits are stated against the version each skill was verified on instead of "today" or "yet" (#542)
- **AI** ([Release v7.14.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.14.3)): Screenplay skills name the `screenplay` MCP server together with each tool they call (#542)
- **AI** ([Release v7.14.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.14.3)): The read-model skill is pinned to Arc 22.16.0 and the performance review to Components 4.26.2, the versions their APIs were verified against (#542)
- **AI** ([Release v7.14.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.14.3)): Six skill descriptions no longer contain generic type brackets such as `IProjectionFor<T>`, which the Claude Skills API rejects (#542)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.97.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.97.0)): The positive typed screen source case parses with zero authoring diagnostics and asserts its typed screen shape: template assignments, toolbar items, component bindings with null-clear behavior, dialog template references and manual form columns. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.97.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.97.0)): The MCP authoring harness is executable on the released version vector; only browser, CLI/Stage and Studio harnesses stay pending on their later releases. (part of #168) (part of #173)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.43.0)): The live Stage command form renders through the native Arc `CommandForm`: command fields built from the modeled form schema with required-field validation at the field boundary, native server-side validation and exception feedback, automatic layout or manually placed unequal columns, and query refresh after a successful submit. (part of #236)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.43.0)): Typed bindings resolve through the shared Scene engine resolvers instead of a local path reader, adding nested component property paths, `preserve`/`clear` null behavior and mode/value-type diagnostics for synthesized screens. (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.43.0)): The Stage runtime frontend pins exact Scene 4.10.0 with Arc 22.58.2, Components 4.26.2 and Fundamentals 7.22.8, and the .NET renderer contracts pin Scene.Engine and Scene.Model 4.10.0 so the emitted shell and the runtime share the same binding contracts. (part of #237)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.58.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.58.3)): Command scenarios whose only failure assertion is `ShouldNotBeAuthorized`, `ShouldHaveExceptions`, or `ShouldNotBeSuccessful` are no longer generated as rejections, because they do not prove a validation or constraint rejection. They are omitted and reported as unreadable specifications. (#3046)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): Stage now compiles models with Screenplay 4.93.0. Models therefore get that version's checks: PLAY0504 is an error when a routed command's identifier type, or an event's destination type, differs from the target event source's identifier type, and stream id literals on command and specification routes are validated. (part of #243)
- **cli** ([Release v3.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.38.0)): `cratis render` and `cratis run` now use Stage 4.40.0 packages and the matching `cratis/stage:4.40.0` runtime image by default. (part of #284)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): Live Stage now renders authored dialog templates instead of reporting every dialog as unsupported. (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): Stage screen composition now carries template chrome, screen content and authored command forms into the same rendered application surface. (part of #236)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): Stage command forms now use modeled field metadata, command route lookup, validation feedback and query refresh at the submit boundary. (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): Stage bindings now accept Scene 4.9 typed component-property paths in addition to the legacy binding path adapter. (part of #237)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.93.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.93.0)): PLAY0504 is now an error, not a warning, when a routed command's identifier type, or an event's destination type, differs from the identifier type of the event source it routes to. Unrouted commands are unaffected. (part of #407)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.93.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.93.0)): Stream id literals on command routes and specification routes are now checked like any stream id. Text must be non-empty, well-formed and in Unicode NFC form. In the default numeric mode, an integer must lie between -9007199254740991 and 9007199254740991. Specification route contradictions compare canonical values, so a UUID written in another case is the same id. (part of #407)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): Live Stage screen rendering now keeps table query state, row selection and binding data scoped to each table instance, including keyboard selection and selection clearing. (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): Stage query bindings now support scoped argument rebinding and stale request cancellation before updating rendered data. (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): Generated and live Stage frontends render through the Scene 4.9 default blueprint and PrimeReact package composition instead of flattening screen slots. (part of #236)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.90.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.90.0)): The commands guide notes that its running-total event example is a counter, not a generic update event (part of #393)
- **cli** ([Release v3.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.37.0)): The CLI bundles Stage 4.36.0, and `cratis run` starts the Stage 4.36.0 container, which compiles models with Screenplay 4.81.5.
- **cli** ([Release v3.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.37.0)): `cratis render` admits ESM v5 to v7 and leaves refusals to the renderer, which names the construct it cannot render: reactions, captures, triggers and Automation or Translate slices (`STAGE-ESM-024`), read-model absence assertions (`STAGE-ESM-027`), generated command values (`STAGE-ESM-028`) and command responses (`STAGE-ESM-029`). Policy negation now renders. `CLI-RENDER-004` is reported only for a model newer than ESM v7.
- **cli** ([Release v3.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.37.0)): Rendered policies no longer refuse number or Boolean claim comparisons, which never match, and compare Uuid, Date and DateTime claims only against Screenplay's canonical text, so generated policy code changes for those claims.
- **cli** ([Release v3.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.37.0)): `cratis run` refuses a screen `action` with `when … execute` alternatives (`STAGE-SCENE-ACTION-001`).
- **cli** ([Release v3.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.36.0)): The bundled Screenplay compiler and MCP server are now 4.87.0, which adds the `propose-source` authoring tool to `cratis screenplay mcp`; the MCP reference lists it (#285)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.89.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.89.0)): When a proposal is refused because an identity migration is missing or stale, the refusal names each address and which argument it belongs in (`semanticRenames`, `eventRenames`, `retiredSemanticAddresses` or `retiredEventAddresses`), and MCP returns them as `identityMigrationIssues` (part of #386)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.89.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.89.0)): The TypeScript compiler, VS Code and Monaco report the guarded screen action diagnostics PLAY0345 to PLAY0348, matching the C# compiler (part of #527)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.88.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.88.0)): PLAY0515 now also refuses a `@pii` or `@sensitive` concept used as a stream id type, as a composite stream id part, or as the source of a route's stream id mapping. Before, it only covered identifiers and event source destinations. The message suggests a surrogate identifier and never shows the value. (#525)
- **AI** ([Release v7.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.14.2)): A secret concept that the system keeps, such as a third-party API key, now takes Chronicle's `[Encrypted]` together with Arc's `[NotAudited]`; personal data keeps `[PII]` alone, never combined with `[Encrypted]`, and neither marking belongs on an event source id (#531)
- **AI** ([Release v7.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.14.2)): Credentials used for authentication are described as never stored: a password or token given to a command is `[NotAudited]`, and events carry only a hash or a reference. The security review skill and both security-reviewer agents now agree on this (#531)
- **AI** ([Release v7.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.14.2)): Screenplay's `@sensitive` is described as an operational secret, rendered as `[Encrypted]` plus `[NotAudited]`, with the Screenplay versions that reject `@pii` (4.69.1) and `@sensitive` (4.84.1) on identifiers (#531)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.36.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.36.1)): Stage builds against Screenplay 4.84.1. (#201)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.84.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.84.1)): `@sensitive` now means an operational secret, not personal data: encrypted at rest without erasure and withheld from the causation chain. C# providers render it as `[Encrypted]` and `[NotAudited]`, and `@pii @sensitive` as `[PII]` only. The concepts guide, diagnostics reference and editor hovers describe this mapping, and decision record [0034](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0034-sensitive-means-operational-secret.md) records it (#384)
- **cli** ([Release v3.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.35.0)): The `cratis screenplay validate` JSON summary reports `valid`, `errors` and `warnings`, and with `--check` it also reports `checks`, `completenessStatus` and, when the checks were skipped, `completenessNote`.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.34.0)): A screen action with `when … execute` alternatives (a guarded action, Screenplay 4.81) is refused with `STAGE-SCENE-ACTION-001` instead of being rendered, because the Scene components Stage renders to cannot yet pick a command from the selected item's state. Screens without guarded actions are unaffected. (part of #209)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.58.0)): A command or append scenario whose facts set off a reactor that is now declarative is left out and reported with `SP0039`, because Arc's scenarios do not run reactors while the generated specification would include the reaction's facts. (#3040)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.82.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.82.3)): The samples policy names the preview constructs that stay out of the Invoicing sample while the executable model refuses them, each with its issue and fixture, and a specification fails when a language construct is neither in Invoicing, a preview construct, nor covered by another named sample (part of #495)
- **cli** ([Release v3.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.34.0)): The CLI bundles Screenplay 4.81.7 and Stage 4.30.0.
- **cli** ([Release v3.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.34.0)): `cratis screenplay validate` reports new diagnostics for `depends on`: `PLAY0554` and `PLAY0555` (warnings) for an invalid or repeated target, `PLAY0552` (warning) for a dependency the model uses but the container does not declare, and `PLAY0553` and `PLAY0556` (information) for an unused or mutual declaration. Warnings fail `--warnings-as-errors`.
