- **cli** ([Release v3.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.34.0)): `cratis screenplay validate` and `cratis render` reject a personal-data value typed directly in a reaction trigger as a reaction destination (`PLAY0515`), report `PLAY0524` once per missing required fixture property and `PLAY0519` for a property assigned twice in a step, and resolve a reaction's `for` to the event when an event and an application trigger share a name.
- **cli** ([Release v3.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.34.0)): Printed models keep large integers and small fractions as written instead of in scientific notation.
- **cli** ([Release v3.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.34.0)): `cratis render` produces applications for Arc 22.50.5 and Chronicle 19.32.0, renders role-only and claim-only policies as Arc policies, and refuses a specification whose caller is not signed in but has roles or claims (`STAGE-ESM-011`).
- **cli** ([Release v3.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.34.0)): `cratis run` starts the Stage 4.30.0 container. It still compiles models with the container's own Screenplay release, which does not parse policy negation, `depends on`, `example`, conditional screen actions or specification event routes, so a model that uses them can pass `cratis screenplay validate` and still fail `cratis run`.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): Stage builds against Screenplay 4.81.5 and admits models of executable model versions 5 to 7. A model that uses a construct Stage does not render yet is refused, for the slices being rendered, with a diagnostic naming that construct: `STAGE-ESM-024` for reactions, captures, triggers and Automation or Translate slices, `STAGE-ESM-027` for read-model absence assertions in specifications, `STAGE-ESM-028` for generated command values and `STAGE-ESM-029` for command responses. Models that use none of them render as before. (#201) (#206)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): The semantic runtime refuses the same constructs instead of running models it cannot execute. (#201)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.82.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.82.0)): The timeline-order check now also covers reducers and `reads`. A reducer's `on` rules count like projection events, and a command or reaction that `reads` a read model built by a slice drawn after it is reported as `PLAY0516`, naming the read model. Reading a view that is built from facts produced on the reader's own side is a feedback loop and is not reported. `PLAY0517` now says that its members depend on each other's events or read models. The typed repair covers the new findings. (part of #413)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): Policies that compare a claim with a number or a Boolean are no longer refused. As in Screenplay, such a comparison never matches, so the rest of the policy decides: an `or` with a role still allows callers with that role, and the comparison alone denies. (part of #57)
- **cli** ([Release v3.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.33.0)): `cratis screenplay mcp --help` uses the same help layout as every other command and writes it to standard output; protocol runs still keep standard output for JSON-RPC and start no update checks. `cratis screenplay mcp` now rejects a blank `--project-root` or `--project-root-env` value.
- **cli** ([Release v3.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.33.0)): `cratis screenplay mcp install|status|update|uninstall` is deprecated and runs the matching `cratis screenplay desktop` command after a notice on standard error.
- **cli** ([Release v3.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.33.0)): `cratis new --help` describes the reserved `list` template value and the wizard.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): The semantic runtime builds its scene from the Screenplay semantic model instead of the legacy event model; authored screens and layouts are still read from the model's syntax. (part of #23)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): A concept marked `@sensitive` renders as `[Encrypted]` and `[NotAudited]`: its values are encrypted at rest and kept out of the causation chain, but not enrolled in erasure as personal data. It previously rendered as `[PII]`. A concept marked both `@pii` and `@sensitive` renders as `[PII]` only. (#197)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): Stage refuses to render a model whose `@pii` or `@sensitive` concept is used as an event-source identity (a command identifier, a projection key or a `for` destination), with `STAGE-CRATIS-COMPLIANCE-001`, before writing any output. Chronicle does not allow personal data as an event source id. `@pii` on other concepts still renders as `[PII]`. (part of #197)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): The "Rendering N application(s)" message is printed after the model has been read, and is not printed when the event model is invalid. (#187)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): Specifications whose caller is not signed in but carries roles or claims are refused with `STAGE-ESM-011`, because Arc removes roles and claims from callers who are not signed in. (part of #57)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): Stage translates tenants the same way in its own runtime, in generated command handlers and in generated reducers: the default tenant, and an Arc request with no tenant, become the zero-GUID portable default (`TenantId.Default`). A named tenant keeps its exact name, and a tenant named as an empty string or as the zero GUID is refused rather than merged into the default. Generated command handlers previously wrote Arc's raw `Default` value. (#185)
- **Screenplay.CritterStack** ([Release v0.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay.CritterStack/releases/tag/v0.25.0)): Dependencies are updated and generation is verified against Screenplay 4.47.0 and Screenplay Generation 0.18.0
- **Stage** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): Rendered applications build against Arc 22.50.5 and the Chronicle 19.32.0 client and development image, the versions Stage itself runs. The frontend moves to `@cratis/arc` 22.50.5 and `@cratis/fundamentals` 7.19.8. (#216)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): Rendered backend projects turn off Arc's embedded Screenplay extraction (`CratisEmbeddedScreenplayEnabled=false`), because the `.play` model they were rendered from is already the source of truth. (#216)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.55.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.55.1)): Arc uses Cratis.Screenplay 4.80.0, and the embedded board uses the 4.80 language package. Generated documents still compile cleanly and bind. The embedded board shows only compiler warnings as warnings, not the new timeline-order notes. (#3066)
- **Components** ([Release v4.26.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.26.2)): The `SchemaEditor` tree layout now offers concepts in a Concepts submenu of the type menu, instead of listing them inline, so the menu stays short when a domain has many concepts. When none are defined the submenu shows the existing "no concepts" hint.
- **cli** ([Release v3.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.32.0)): `cratis screenplay validate` states which verdict it checked: the JSON summary reports `checked` (`source` or `executable`), and the text summary says that a plain pass checks the source only. The Screenplay reference explains the difference between a valid, an executable and a renderable model.
- **cli** ([Release v3.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.31.0)): `cratis screenplay generate` uses Cratis.Arc.Screenplay 22.52.0. Generated Arc models now carry command identifiers, `for` routing, inline events, event metadata, named rules and `when append` specifications. Generated identifiers and command responses produce ESM v7 models, which `cratis render` does not accept yet. (#262)
- **AI** ([Release v7.14.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.14.1)): The Arc command rules and skills now say a command `Handle()` never returns a nullable event or `null` to mean "nothing to do". Missing state or a failed rule rejects the command with `Result<TEvent, ValidationResult>` and `ValidationResult.Error(...)`, a validator, or `Provide()`. The `ARC0006` guidance says to handle a missing read model by rejecting. Optional command operations are unaffected. (#518)
- **AI** ([Release v7.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.14.0)): `IProviderChatClientFactory.Create` is asynchronous, so an application that keeps provider credentials protected can reveal one (a call to a key service) before the vendor client is opened. Applications register their own factory; the default one is unchanged in behavior.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.76.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.76.1)): The VS Code extension turns off word-based suggestions for `.play` files, so suggestions come from the language service alone.
- **cli** ([Release v3.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.30.0)): The CLI bundles Screenplay 4.74.0, whose compiler and embedded MCP server read executable semantic model (ESM) v7: generated command values and command responses.
- **cli** ([Release v3.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.30.0)): `cratis screenplay validate` and `cratis render` reject a `@pii` concept used as an event-source identifier, a command identifier or a command or reaction `for` destination (`PLAY0515`, an error; use a surrogate identifier). Models that validated before can fail.
- **cli** ([Release v3.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.30.0)): `cratis screenplay validate` warns about undeclared events in projection removals and capture appends (`PLAY0166`) and about projection mappings to read-model properties that are not declared (`PLAY0514`); these warnings fail `--warnings-as-errors`.
- **cli** ([Release v3.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.30.0)): `cratis screenplay validate` reports two information diagnostics, which never fail validation: `PLAY0516` for a slice that uses an event from a slice drawn after it, and `PLAY0517` for slices, features or modules that use each other's events.
- **cli** ([Release v3.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.30.0)): Validating a root file merges its imported files depth-first in import order, where it was breadth-first before. When parts of one module or feature are imported at different depths, this can change which of two conflicting declarations is kept and reported first.
- **cli** ([Release v3.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.30.0)): With no arguments and no `.cratis/ai.json`, `cratis screenplay mcp` now serves the model folder inside the single root the client offers rather than the root itself. It keeps the folder nearest that root which already holds `.screenplay` state, and otherwise uses the common folder of its `.play` files, else `Source` or `src`, else a new `Screenplay` folder it creates. It reports competing folders that hold state, and an interrupted write in one of them blocks the workspace until that folder is opened and recovered. With neither a client root nor a model in the current directory it creates and serves `Documents/Screenplay`, where it previously asked for a path.
- **cli** ([Release v3.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.30.0)): The embedded MCP server merges workspace files in file-path order. Its `expand-layout` tool writes import-based files, with one file per slice for the `slice` layout, and refuses a layout that would change the authored order. `read-workspace view=repairs` and `propose-repair` offer a repair for `PLAY0516`, and the new `dependency-graph` tool shows how slices, features and modules depend on each other.
- **cli** ([Release v3.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.30.0)): When the embedded MCP server rewrites module and feature files it keeps their `import` lines where they were written, and its execution readiness names command handlers and exact numbers for any model that uses them.
- **cli** ([Release v3.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.30.0)): The `visualize-model` event model board is drawn in authored declaration and import order, shows each specification's command once with its values, error messages and caller, and counts reactions in its added and removed summaries.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.75.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.75.0)): The VS Code extension turns GitHub Copilot inline suggestions off for `.play` files by default. Re-enable them with `"github.copilot.enable": { "screenplay": true }`.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.52.0)): Arc uses Cratis.Screenplay 4.68.2. (part of #2887)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.52.0)): Commands whose handler is empty or only returns a response no longer get a `handler` file reference, and `SP0044` is reported as information when `AuthoringOnlyConstructs` is off. (part of #2887)
- **AI** ([Release v7.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.12.0)): Rate-limit recording moved into the shared `ProviderRateLimitRecorder`, used by both completions and chat clients.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.73.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.73.2)): `expand-layout` refuses to propose a layout that would change the authored order of modules, features or slices. It now takes that order from the same root the compiler's timeline check uses, so an import-less `application.play` beside a single importing file no longer leads to path order. When there is no root to take the order from, the proposal's review text says that path order was used. (#412)
- **Components** ([Release v4.26.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.26.1)): The `SchemaEditor` tree layout now shows each property type as a colored chip, matching how types look on the event model board, and names a concept in a highlighted chip. The type menu uses the same chips, a deeper shadow and roomier items, and rows pick up a primary-colored border on hover.
- **Components** ([Release v4.26.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.26.1)): The chip colors are `--cratis-schema-editor-type-*` and `--cratis-schema-editor-concept-*` custom properties, so an application can retheme them.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.72.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.72.0)): `expand-layout` writes import barrels instead of repeating module and feature headers in every file: `application.play` imports module files; with `layout: "feature"` or `"slice"` a module file imports its feature files, which import nested features and, for `"slice"`, one file per slice holding only that slice, while `layout: "module"` keeps features and slices inline in the module file. Parameters and destination paths are unchanged, and authored order, comments and identities are preserved. (#434)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.72.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.72.0)): Compiling from a single root file now merges imported files depth-first in import order, where it was breadth-first before. Wildcard matches stay alphabetical and folder compilation keeps file-path order. This can change merged sibling order, and which of two conflicting declarations is kept and reported first, when parts of one module or feature are imported at different depths. Applies to the .NET compiler, the standalone tool and the TypeScript compiler and VS Code extension. (#434)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.72.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.72.0)): Canonical printing keeps `import` lines in module and feature files where they were written instead of moving them directly after the description, so the timeline-order repair for imports that follow declarations (PLAY0516) is now offered. (#434)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.9.0)): `Sequence.Revise` accepts replacements with PII or subject, namespace or global encryption instead of returning `ErrUnsupported`. The kernel protects the revision and its system request with the original event's subject, and erasing that subject removes the revised personal data. Revising with an unclassified generation of an event type that another generation classifies is still refused, because that plaintext could not be erased. (part of #87) (part of #40)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.9.0)): Classified read models work on projection replay routes: bounded and passive `GetAll`, `ReplayProjection`, `GetSnapshots`, hydration sessions and immediate projections release each value with the subject it was written under, for both `Id` and `id` keys, instead of returning `ErrUnsupported`. (part of #87) (part of #33)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.9.0)): Decision reads admit classified models, as in C#, and server agreement no longer compares server-side protection metadata. (part of #87) (part of #34)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.9.0)): Projections that subscribe to all events and also map explicit event types can be read through bounded collection replay, `ReplayProjection` and `GetSnapshots`. (part of #87) (part of #33)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.9.0)): Protection declared beneath an unprotected map, and on array elements that are themselves lists or maps, now registers instead of failing with `declarations.DeclarationError`. Beneath a map, the kernel protects each map value's classified members; a classified list- or map-valued array element is protected and erased as a whole element. (part of #87) (part of #35)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.69.0)): The C# compiler now computes the authored order of a folder model from the same root the boards use: the single root, otherwise an `application.play` that imports something, otherwise the only importing document. Order stays out of the executable model, its revisions and identities. (part of #413)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.8.0)): Compiling a projection now fails with a located declaration error when a derived `children` collection has no or several registered derivatives, when its discriminator cannot be written as a kernel literal, when a global mapping targets `_derivedTypeId`, or when a derivative declares projection mappings outside a `children` collection of its family, including `nested` interface fields. The C# client instead writes children without a discriminator or drops those declarations.
- **AI** ([Release v7.11.5](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.11.5)): Require session prompts and handovers to state their repository, branch, expected HEAD, named worktrees and date, and verify those preconditions before continuing; mismatches mean the prompt is stale, not that work is lost (#403)
- **AI** ([Release v7.11.5](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.11.5)): Make plain method calls the default specifications for pure command, reducer and reactor decisions, adding scenarios where pipelines, validation, constraints or projections contribute proof; distinguish application-assembly Debug wrappers from dedicated spec projects and allow message assertions when exact wording is specified behavior (#449)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.32.5](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.32.5)): The compliance documentation describes what erased `[PII]` values release as for each schema type: empty strings, `0` or `false` for non-nullable numbers and booleans, and `null` for nullable values. See [Erasing a subject](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/compliance/erasing-a-subject.mdx). (part of #4620)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.32.5](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.32.5)): The key lifecycle documentation explains how stored-event migration and event revision release protected values strictly. Operational failures abort the write. Migration stores erased values for an erased subject, while a revision still needs an authorized new key. A data key that cannot be unwrapped for a subject with a recorded erasure is treated as erased. See [Key lifecycle](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/compliance/key-lifecycle.mdx). (part of #4619)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.7.0)): Generated contracts are pinned to Chronicle 19.32.3 (`contracts.ProtocolVersion`), and the getting-started and contributing guides start `cratis/chronicle:19.32.3-development`.
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.7.0)): Decision guards are qualified on Chronicle 19.32.3. Upgrade the kernel to 19.32.3 before using decision reads; other kernel versions, including 19.29.4, return `chronicle.ErrUnsupported`. (#79)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.7.0)): Webhook Bearer, Basic and OAuth authorization is persisted in the selected event store on Chronicle 19.32.3, and the documentation no longer lists the missing authorization as a kernel limitation. (#48)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.7.0)): Global confidentiality (`encrypted(scope=global)`) and protected numeric scalars are documented as working from Chronicle 19.32.3. (part of #35)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.7.0)): `RemovedWithJoin` removes children identified by `id` or `Id` on Chronicle 19.32.3. (part of #26)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.7.0)): Projection queries without an explicit read-model target infer their schema on Chronicle 19.32.3. (part of #27)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.7.0)): The reactor guide no longer requires waiting for observers before the first append on Chronicle 19.32.3; the wait remains documented for older kernels.
- **AI** ([Release v7.11.4](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.11.4)): The Screenplay skills describe ESM v7 from Screenplay 4.68.0: generated command values and command responses bind and run in the standalone compiler, with fixtures for generated values and `then returns` for declared responses, while the cratis CLI's bundled 4.66.0 still refuses them and Stage does not render them, so they are gap-fill (#515)
- **AI** ([Release v7.11.4](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.11.4)): Features that no ESM version admits yet (exact numbers, operations, event sources and streams, reads) are named by feature instead of by a planned version number, following Screenplay's admission-time numbering (#515)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.68.0)): MCP execution readiness now names command handlers and exact numbers for any model that uses them, and reports generated properties on concepts with validation rules as not executable. (part of #309)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.32.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.32.3)): Upgrade every kernel silo in a cluster together rather than one at a time. While silos run different versions, an observer that finishes catching up may not be notified and may deliver some events again once it is recovered.
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.6.0)): Binary values are refused with `ErrUnsupported` before registration in every other use. That includes collections of binary values, which the pinned kernel corrupts when projecting them ([Cratis/Chronicle#4595](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/issues/4595)). It also includes maps, joins, variants, keys, identities, subjects, indexes, unique constraints, protection, event migrations and literals. Types that contain binary values must use simple ASCII property names that are unique regardless of case and are not reserved by the kernel (part of #64)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.6.0)): Decoding a binary value accepts only canonical, padded base64. Values with whitespace or null elements, which the C# client tolerates, are rejected (part of #64)
- **AI** ([Release v7.11.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.11.3)): The Stage and Screenplay skills describe Stage 4.24.2: ESM v4 models render when every event they use is at its first revision, an evolved event is refused with `STAGE-ESM-026` and is gap-fill with hand-written event-type migrations, and a reference to a historical revision is refused with `STAGE-ESM-025`
- **AI** ([Release v7.11.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.11.3)): The skills target the cratis CLI 3.28.3, which bundles Stage 4.24.2 and reports an evolved event with `STAGE-ESM-026` instead of `CLI-RENDER-003`, and the agent harness images install it
- **cli** ([Release v3.28.3](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.28.3)): The CLI bundles Stage 4.24.2, so `cratis render` renders ESM v4 models whose events are at their first revision, and reports an evolved event with Stage's `STAGE-ESM-026`, which names the event and its revision, instead of the CLI's own `CLI-RENDER-003`
- **Stage** ([Release v4.24.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.24.3)): The Stage host uses the shared Cratis OpenTelemetry setup. Every `Cratis.*` trace source and meter, including the Chronicle client, is now subscribed, and OTLP export follows the standard `OTEL_*` configuration. (#191)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.24.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.24.2)): Stage renders ESM v4 models, which Screenplay produces for models with evolved events, as long as every event they use is at its first revision; v1 to v3 models render exactly as before (#165)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.24.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.24.2)): An evolved event, or anything that depends on one, is refused with `STAGE-ESM-026` until Stage can render the Chronicle event-type migrations it needs, and a reference to a historical revision is refused with `STAGE-ESM-025` (#165)
- **AI** ([Release v7.11.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.11.2)): The Screenplay and Stage skills target the cratis CLI 3.28.2, which bundles Screenplay 4.66.0 and Stage 4.24.1: `cratis screenplay validate` now binds Automation and Translate slices, `cratis render` still refuses ESM v6 models with `STAGE-ESM-016`, and guidance for the older bundle applies before 3.28.2 (#500)
- **AI** ([Release v7.11.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.11.2)): The Screenplay skills describe 4.66.0's named-rule `implementation` blocks: accepted on handlers, operation phases and command named rules, refused on concept rules, built-in property rules and whole-command bodies (#500)
- **AI** ([Release v7.11.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.11.2)): The Stage skills describe Stage 4.24.1 and its `STAGE-ESM-024` ledger diagnostic, and the model-first reference describes where cratis 3.28.2 places and finds Screenplay models (#500)
- **AI** ([Release v7.11.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.11.2)): The agent harness images install the cratis CLI 3.28.2 (#500)
- **AI** ([Release v7.11.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.11.1)): In a repository with several packages and no root package, a change outside every package now stops the quality gate with an explicit "unverified" result instead of passing (#487)
- **cli** ([Release v3.28.2](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.28.2)): The CLI bundles Screenplay 4.66.0, Stage 4.24.1, the Arc source adapter 22.50.5, Chronicle 19.32.0 and Fundamentals 7.22.8. `cratis screenplay` validates with the same compiler Stage renders from; Stage still renders ESM v1 to v3 only (#253)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.32.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.32.2)): PII identifiers that start with Chronicle's reserved encrypted-value marker are now rejected before any key is provisioned or value encrypted. (#4553)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.32.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.32.2)): Revising an event and migrating stored events to a new generation now fail, rather than store empty values, when a protected value cannot be released because a key lookup or decryption fails. Values whose subject has been erased are migrated as erased. That includes ciphertext that no longer decrypts after a recorded erasure followed by a new key for the subject. (part of #4456) (part of #4525)
- **cli** ([Release v3.28.1](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.28.1)): `cratis screenplay mcp` run outside a project, with no path or `--project-root`, no longer creates a `Screenplay` folder in the working directory. The server picks its workspace itself: the host's workspace roots, else `Documents/Screenplay` in your home folder.
- **AI** ([Release v7.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.11.0)): Agents, skills, prompts and harness copies no longer treat `.cratis/screenplay/` as the model root. A Screenplay model lives where the project's `.play` files already are, else under `Source/` or `src/`, else in a `Screenplay/` folder at the repository root. A model already in `.cratis/screenplay/` keeps working.
- **AI** ([Release v7.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.11.0)): Screenplay model guidance describes the MCP default for chats with no project: `Documents/Screenplay` in the user's home folder.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.67.0)): The ChatGPT desktop plugin no longer pins a model directory in its plugin data folder; it uses the same defaults.
- **AI** ([Release v7.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.10.0)): The Screenplay MCP descriptor no longer declares `defaultRoot`, and the PI `cratis-mcp` extension starts without a `.cratis/screenplay` directory. The Cratis CLI locates the model: the existing `.play` files, else `Source/` or `src/`, else a new `Screenplay/` folder. An explicit `mcpServers.screenplay.root` is still honored. Requires the Cratis CLI release that ships this behavior.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.24.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.24.1)): Stage builds on Screenplay 4.66.0, Arc 22.50.5 and Chronicle 19.32.0, and the development host runs the matching Chronicle 19.32.0 kernel. Models up to ESM v3 render exactly as before; ESM v6 models stay refused (#200)
- **cli** ([Release v3.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.28.0)): `cratis ai install` and `cratis ai update` no longer create or require `.cratis/screenplay/`. Only an explicit `mcpServers.screenplay.root` in `.cratis/ai.json` names a model directory, and that directory must exist. A model already in `.cratis/screenplay/` keeps working.
- **AI** ([Release v7.9.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.9.3)): The Screenplay and Stage skills say plainly what event sources, streams and command routes reach today: they can be authored and validated, but they do not bind, their specifications do not run, and Stage does not render them, so their realization is gap-fill with the model as the contract (part of #500)
- **AI** ([Release v7.9.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.9.3)): The Chronicle .NET client and Arc command skills cover event source definitions: `IEventSource`, `[EventSource]` and `[EventStream]` with the routing fields on `EventForEventSourceId` (Chronicle 19.30.0 and later), and `[EventSource<TSource>(stream)]` on commands (Arc 22.49.0 and later) (part of #500)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.66.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.66.1)): MCP `executionReadiness` for commands, specifications, slices and the model now names every unadmitted feature with its tracking issue, in a fixed order, instead of a single required version number. (part of #309)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.4.0)): Strict read-model scenarios (`ReadModelOptions.StrictEventSubscription`) now decide whether a seeded event is subscribed the same way the kernel does for the projection. Children, joins, nested scopes, removal events and derived event types all count, and keys do not. Strict scenarios no longer refuse relationship, custom-key or mixed-All projections when they open. Unsubscribed seeds are still rejected in `Given` with `ErrUnsubscribedEventSeeded` before anything is appended. Reading results for those profiles still returns `ErrUnsupported` (part of #38)
- **release-action** ([Release v1.4.2](https://github.com/Cratis/release-action/releases/tag/v1.4.2)): HTML comments (``) are removed from the release notes before the release is created, so pull request template guidance left in a description is no longer published in the release body. Comments shown inside fenced or inline code are kept, and notes holding nothing but a comment count as empty, so GitHub generates them (#195)
- **release-action** ([Release v1.4.2](https://github.com/Cratis/release-action/releases/tag/v1.4.2)): Issues are closed from the published notes, after comments are removed: a `(#n)` written only inside an HTML comment no longer closes the issue (#195)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.3.0)): `captures.Service.Validate` and `Save` return `ErrUnsupported` for a definition that carries source authorization, before anything is sent to the kernel, so credentials are never silently dropped. The kernel's capture contract cannot carry authorization yet ([Cratis/Chronicle#4591](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/issues/4591)) (part of #37)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.3.0)): A webhook source rejects blank credentials and more than one authorization option at `Build`; C# keeps only the last option (part of #37)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.3.0)): `json.Marshal` and `json.Unmarshal` of capture `Source`, `Definition`, `Builder` and `SourceAuthorization` now fail with `ErrUnsupported` instead of producing `{}`, so credentials cannot be serialized or silently decoded as no authorization (part of #37)
- **AI** ([Release v7.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.9.0)): Discovery, slice design, streams, automations and translations, render and gap-fill, and toolchain guidance follow Martin Dilger and Nebulit GmbH's wording, checklists and output formats more closely, for example storylines, events with no fields, per-record translation contracts and stream decision trees (#497)
- **AI** ([Release v7.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.9.0)): The Screenplay Modeler, Screenplay Reviewer, Screenplay Renderer and Slice Implementer agents point to the adopted per-run protocol, business-question lens and delivery discipline (#497)
- **AI** ([Release v7.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.9.0)): Each affected skill's provenance and `LICENSE` now credit Martin Dilger and Nebulit GmbH, with their agreement, alongside the existing TrogonStack MIT notices (#497)
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.1)): Agent harness images (base, Claude, Pi and Copilot) now ship the latest tooling: Claude Code 2.1.285, Pi 1.0.3, GitHub Copilot CLI 1.0.92, Cratis CLI 3.27.1, rtk 0.51.0, Helm 4.3.0, Gradle 9.8.0, Kotlin 2.4.20, OTP 29.1.1, Playwright 1.63.0, mongosh 2.13.0, Headroom 0.39.1 and the Roslyn language server 5.12.0-1.26475.2.
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.1)): The bundled MCP servers and Pi extensions are updated to their latest releases, including `pi-mcp-adapter` 5.0.0 and `@gotgenes/pi-anthropic-auth` 3.4.2.
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.1)): A Pi worker now requires `DIRECT_MODEL`: Pi 1.x rejects `--provider` without `--model`, so a worker started without a model exits with a clear message instead of failing inside the CLI.
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): `cratis-screenplay-event-modeling` is now the entry point. It holds the model-first decision and routes to the method skills. (#493)
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): The language skills now state, at Screenplay 4.64.0, which compiler admits reactions, clocks and captures, and what binds and what renders. The MCP guidance covers identity-preserving edits, the source-map view and the `whenAppendedEvent` label. (#434)
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): `cratis-stage-rendering-and-sandbox` describes `cratis render` on Stage 4.24.
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): Contract-first agents:
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): The planner, coordinator, orchestrator and code reviewer choose the model level when a repository has opted in.
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): The slice implementer, backend developer, spec writer and frontend developer start from the contract.
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): The spec writer seeds prior state with `_scenario.Given`. (#492)
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): The rules, prompts and code-level skills find the contract first, and route model-level work back to Screenplay when a model covers it.
- **AI** ([Release v7.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.7.0)): The specification skills take their list of specs from the contract and cover read-model lifecycles, and the reactor skill covers repeated delivery.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.66.0)): The VS Code board and local language assistance remain available in Restricted Mode, while C# repair processes require workspace trust. Workspace overrides for source roots and repair settings are restricted until the workspace is trusted. (part of #341)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.65.0)): `implementation` blocks are rejected on concept rules, built-in property rules and whole-command `require`/`validate` bodies, with matching diagnostics in both compilers and the editors. Files without implementation blocks parse, print and serialize as before. (part of #307)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.64.0)): Valid `numbers exact` documents report `PLAY0268` when bound, because ESM v7 is not admitted yet. Files without the preamble parse, print and serialize exactly as before. (part of #285)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.64.0)): MCP tools refuse to return exact numbers through the older fixture format rather than rounding them. (part of #285)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.32.0)): Generated shared enums in the contracts now carry the documentation of their source enums, and optional enum query arguments are supported in generated requests (part of #4424)

## Fixed

- **Arc** ([Release v22.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.77.0)): The Screenplay generator no longer states a reactor that returns several events as `produces` when Chronicle could refuse the batch differently from Screenplay: Chronicle appends a returned batch as one unit, while a Screenplay reaction appends each fact separately. When a later event can be rejected by a constraint (a unique property, a class-level `[Unique]`, or an `IConstraint` target), an earlier event closes the stream, or a constraint cannot be read, the reactor keeps its `file` reference and `SP0070` explains why. (part of #3093)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.77.0)): The Screenplay generator reads screen source with a scanner that understands strings, template literals, regular expressions and JSX text and attributes, so `//` inside a value such as `title="https://example.com"` is no longer cut off as a comment and an `import` inside a template literal is no longer read as an import. A screen that renders other components or HTML beside the Cratis Components it reads now reports each part it does not read with `SP0028`, and a screen whose source cannot be scanned reports that once instead of stating a partial structure. (#3095)
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): Arc identity guidance: forwarded identity headers are trusted only with `TrustForwardedIdentityHeaders=true`, discovery endpoints need authentication outside Development, and the `.cratis-iden
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.51.0)): In-process MongoDB integration runs no longer intermittently overcount keyed events or let observers from an earlier test context consume the next context's events. (#4787)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.75.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.75.2)): The Screenplay generator no longer drops `then returns` when a scenario reads the result through the null-forgiving operator (`result!`) or passes it as an `in` argument; these are reads, not writes. (#3092)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.75.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.75.2)): The Screenplay generator no longer states a command response as `then returns` when the scenario changes it before asserting: a write through any member or element of the executed result (assignment, `++`/`--`, `ref`/`out`, including null-conditional chains), or a copy of the result or of a reference read through it (into a variable, field or initializer, a `foreach`, a pattern variable, or a returned, yielded or lambda value, also through `?:`, `??`, tuples and collection expressions), now keeps the response from being stated and reports the scenario instead. (#3092)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.75.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.75.1)): `CommandScenario` now runs a command as the caller a specification registers: a substituted `ICurrentPrincipalAccessor`, `ICurrentPrincipalOverride` or `IAuthorizationPolicyRuntime` added to the scenario's services is kept instead of being replaced by Arc's defaults, so specifications of role- or policy-protected commands are authorized or denied by that caller, as the documented setup describes. (#3184)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.50.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.50.1)): An observer no longer skips events when the storage lookup for its next relevant event fails. It keeps its position, stays in routing without delivering anything, and retries on the next watchdog pass instead of fast-forwarding to the tail. (#4770)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.50.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.50.1)): The observer documentation now matches the current behavior: projection replay and catch-up run as one ordered step, `[OnceOnly]` excludes replays rather than limiting a handler to one run per event source, and the quarantine and replay descriptions in the observers concept page are corrected. (part of #4748)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.74.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.74.0)): The Screenplay generator now reports two same-named concepts as ambiguous (`SP0031`) when they differ in `pii`, `secret` or the stated encryption scope, instead of declaring one with the other's compliance claims. (#3096)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.74.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.74.0)): The generator now leaves `pii` and `secret` off a concept only when the generated document actually uses it as an identifier, event-source identity, stream id, stream-id route mapping or `for` destination, which Screenplay rejects (`PLAY0515`). Concepts that are identifiers only in code the document does not state keep their annotations, and `SP0059` now names each use that caused an annotation to be withheld. (#3096)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.73.0)): Proxy generation consistently selects one image per assembly identity when application and framework directories contain different copies, and ignores optional native runtime images without hiding invalid required managed inputs. (#3163)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.72.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.72.1)): The Screenplay generator no longer states a command's source and stream route when one of its productions is explicitly routed or not proven to use the command's context, whatever the identifier type. Commands identified by a `Uuid`, a `Uuid`-backed concept, or no identifier type previously kept a route that claimed those events land on the command's stream; the whole route is now withheld and reported with `SP0054`, in both default and authoring output. (#3097)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.72.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.72.0)): Commands returned by a reactor marked `[ExecuteCommandsAsSystem]` no longer leave the caller authenticated as the system actor. The execution identity is contained within the async command scope and is cleaned up on success, rejection, exceptions and service-resolution failures. (#3177)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.71.0)): Model-bound command proxies no longer type Chronicle event values as client responses when their declared shape is handled by the event integration. Mixed returns retain the client identifier or DTO. MVC controller outputs, object collections, and unsupported collection shapes remain client responses. (#2885)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.50.0)): Reactor and reducer dispatch caches are isolated to each client's event registry, so registrations from one client cannot change which events another client handles. Registry changes invalidate cached dispatch maps. (part of #4732)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.50.0)): Event dispatch includes exact registry-known event types without an `[EventType]` attribute, while retaining base/interface dispatch and historical-generation exclusions. (part of #4732)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.50.0)): Reactor delivery identities, event-sequence inference and implicit external-store subscriptions consistently use registered event identities and store metadata. Private helpers referencing events outside a filtered registry no longer cause observer discovery to fail. (part of #4732)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.50.0)): Event-target read-model registration and child event-property mapping resolve event identities through the client registry, preserving existing publication checks and identity defaults. (part of #4732)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.139.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.139.0)): The event-generation documentation clarifies that an opted-in Chronicle projection pins the latest registered generation when it is registered. This provider registration rule does not change Screenplay's existing generation resolution.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.73.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.73.1)): The running Stage served the Scene document with each enumeration spelled like its .NET name (`"kind":"Dialog"`), while the Scene model reads `dialog`. The Scene navigation host therefore resolved a dialog destination to nothing and the toolbar's dialog item did nothing - found by running the public acceptance vector against CLI 3.53.0 / Stage 4.73.0, which a generated application never showed because the renderer plans the document with the model's spelling. `/stage/scene` now uses the same spelling (`SceneEnumerationNaming`'s lists, pinned together by a conformance specification) and writes nothing for a member with no value. (part of #237)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.69.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.69.1)): Observable queries over plain HTTP now apply read-model interception and emission guards to cached and later results, for both model-bound and controller queries. Denied results return no data, suppressed results wait for an allowed emission only when requested, and failures respect `ExposeExceptionDetails`. (#3155)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.69.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.69.1)): Async-enumerable queries now apply read-model interception over the query hub and plain-HTTP controller endpoints. (#3155)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.49.0)): Projection preview, used when querying a projection declaration from a client and in the Workbench projection editor, now returns the documents the saved projection would persist. Keyed child updates update the existing child instead of adding a duplicate, removed children are removed, and join events from another event source update every read model they join into instead of creating documents of their own (#4727)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.49.0)): Child projections computed on demand for a single read model no longer keep children removed with `RemovedWith` (part of #4542)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.49.0)): When a child is removed through `RemovedWithJoin`, the parent read models it belonged to still receive their every-event mappings (#4725)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.49.0)): The documentation examples for model-bound child updates, child joins and child removal through a join now match how child joins correlate, so they run as described (#4725)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.71.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.71.1)): Executing a command with a required property missing, or with a value that breaks a modeled rule, now answers with a validation failure naming the offending properties instead of a 500 "internal error". Before, the missing property was only caught by the event store's schema check, and the modeled validation rules were never run when a command executed.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.136.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.136.0)): The executable semantic model version table lists ESM v11 as released, from Screenplay 4.135.0, instead of claimed and unreleased. See https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/interoperability.md
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.135.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.135.1)): A specification `example` can now be typed by an event, command or read model imported from another document. When a document is checked on its own, the example is accepted like an inline `given`, and its values are checked when the compilation includes the imported declaration. The TypeScript compiler now reports PLAY0520 for an example whose type is unknown, ambiguous or of the wrong kind, matching the .NET compiler. See https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/specifications.md (#648)
- **AI** ([Release v7.29.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.29.1)): The Screenplay contract sync no longer fails after a clean comparison when the `screenplay-sync` label does not exist yet, so a real drift now opens its tracking issue (#602)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.135.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.135.0)): Whole-number literals with 16 or more digits, such as `9007199254740991`, and long fractions such as `0.10000000000000002`, now keep their exact value in the executable model instead of being rounded to 15 significant digits. A model with such a literal outside a route compiles at the new ESM v11. Every other model keeps its version, bytes and revision, and v1–v10 models from other producers are still accepted with any decimal. Consumers have to admit v11 explicitly. See https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0064-exact-number-literals-claim-esm-v11.md (#639)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.46.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.46.0)): A `ReactorScenario` handler that throws synchronously now surfaces its own exception instead of a `TargetInvocationException` wrapper (part of #3912)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.68.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.68.1)): Generated command handlers now refuse a whole-number stream-id value outside ±9007199254740991, the bound Screenplay applies. They return a validation error naming the property, and no event is appended. Composite stream ids check each part in order before producing any event. Before, a value such as `9007199254740992` became a stream id that Screenplay's reference rejects. (#324)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.16.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.16.3)): A job step whose job is removed now has its stored state deleted. It used to be saved again and stay until the job's own state was cleared (#71)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.45.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.45.0)): A model-bound `[Join<TEvent>]` inside a `[ChildrenFrom]` collection without `on:` now joins on the child's identifying property, so join events that happened before the child was added are applied. If the child has no identifying property of its own, building the projection fails with `MissingChildIdentityForJoin` instead of silently missing those events (#4724)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.45.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.45.0)): The declarations Workbench shows for registered projections target the full read-model identifier, so they save back unchanged (#4729)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.45.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.45.0)): The projection editor's generated declarative and model-bound C# now compiles and expresses the same projection as the PDL: child joins without `.On()`, keyed removals, `every` blocks, child removals, nested objects, child and nested records with typed properties (`DateTimeOffset`, `Guid`, nullable types, collections), and self-referential read models. Where an attribute or builder cannot express a construct, the generated code says so in a comment instead of emitting different behavior (#4728)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.133.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.133.3)): A property tooltip no longer stays open at a stale position after the board pans or zooms under a still pointer; it closes when its card is no longer under the pointer and follows the card while it is.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.133.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.133.3)): In features with sub-features, the slices of a parent and of its children start at the same height, so the row labels line up with every column.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.16.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.16.2)): An explicit `IJobsManager.Rehydrate()` call that arrives just as a background drain finishes now starts a new pass. It used to be skipped, so newly interrupted jobs waited for the next call or a host restart (#68)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.16.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.16.2)): A job step stopped before `PerformStep` runs (while waiting for a throttle slot, right after acquiring one, or before its work starts) now reports itself as stopped to its job exactly once, so the job completes its stop and runs `OnStopped`. Job steps that report this outcome themselves from a `PerformWork` override must remove that code, or the job counts the stop twice. A step whose grain deactivates without a stop request, for example on silo shutdown, still stays scheduled and resumes after restart (#69)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.65.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.65.1)): The `cratis/stage` Docker image build failed in the frontend stage because `sceneEnumerationNaming.spec.ts` imports the checked-in Scene enumeration map from outside the frontend build context, so `tsc -b` reported TS2307 and v4.65.0 published no image. The map is now copied into the frontend stage at the location the spec expects.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.18.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.18.1)): The PrimeReact data table states a column's sort order with `aria-sort` on the column header instead of on the sort button, where it is not allowed. (part of #73)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.18.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.18.1)): The PrimeReact progress bar has an accessible name: its `label` property, or `Progress`. The dashboard template's capacity bar is labeled `Warehouse capacity`. (part of #73)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.42.0)): A class implementing two `IProjectionFor<>` or two `IReducerFor<>` interfaces, directly or through a base class, is no longer silently skipped at startup. Discovery now fails with `TypeImplementsMultipleProjectionInterfaces` or `TypeMustImplementOnlyOneReducer`, naming the class, so split it into one projection or reducer per read model. (#4734)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.42.0)): `ContainerName()` on a fluent projection now sets the container (collection or table) the read model is stored in. It used to replace the read model identifier, so the projection pointed at a read model that did not exist. The read model keeps its identifier, and two projections that give one read model different container names are reported as a conflict. (#4735)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.67.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.67.1)): Model-bound queries in assemblies without generated query metadata are discovered again when another assembly contributes generated metadata. Generated metadata is still used for the assemblies it covers. (#3142)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.63.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.63.1)): The Stage Host can now rebuild its state from events whose enum values were stored as numbers, which is how Chronicle stores them for applications Stage renders. Before, rebuilding from those events failed. A number maps to the member at that position in the model, and declared member names still work. Out-of-range, fractional or undeclared values are refused rather than misread. (part of #296)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.63.0)): Specifications that Stage renders now escape text values correctly. Before, a value containing a newline, quote or backslash could produce generated C# that did not compile or that tested a different value.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.40.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.40.1)): A projection child that arrives before its parent is no longer lost when the write of the resolved child fails. The stored future is kept until its write succeeds, and the failure is reported as a failed partition so the partition is retried. (#4740)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.40.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.40.1)): The MongoDB storage no longer risks creating two clients for the same server set when the first connections happen at the same time. (#4741)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.40.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.40.1)): An append that is renumbered after a competing write took its sequence number now checks its concurrency scope again before writing. When the expectation no longer holds, the append returns a concurrency violation instead of being written. (#4742)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.40.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.40.1)): MongoDB 8.2 is now the default image for local development, the Aspire integration (`MongoDBTag`), the development image and the hosting examples. MongoDB 8.0 does not start on Linux kernel 6.19 and newer, which current Docker Desktop releases ship. (#4743)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.62.0)): The Stage Host and semantic executor now check a model's ESM version themselves and refuse versions Stage has not audited with `STAGE-ESM-016`. Before, a v9 public event or event origin outside a Translate slice loaded into the Host without being detected.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.61.0)): The renderer target guide states that `cratis render` forwards requirements, bodies and typed-context descriptors; the Host does not forward descriptors yet.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.16.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.16.1)): Stopping a job step while it waits for a throttle slot or performs its work now ends in a normal cancellation, instead of a `NullReferenceException` from `JobStep.PerformWork`. Code that caught `NullReferenceException` around `base.PerformWork` can drop that catch (#65)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.16.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.16.1)): A job step that was stopped can be started again in the same activation; `Start` used to fail with an unknown error (#65)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.65.5](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.65.5)): Hosted Arc applications using Chronicle now resolve `IEventSources` without manual registration, so commands with `[EventSource<T>]` and aggregate roots that declare an event source run outside `CommandScenario` too. Arc now references Cratis Chronicle 19.39.1. (#3119)
- **cli** ([Release v3.46.1](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.46.1)): `cratis screenplay mcp --project-root` no longer creates a `Screenplay/` folder when it starts in a repository without a model; the folder is created only when the first model document is written, so installing the Screenplay MCP server leaves no trace in a code-first repository; the reference documentation describes the new behaviour (#311)
- **cli** ([Release v3.46.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.46.0)): Corpus rules scoped to a `cratis/<profile>` (such as the CodeScene policy) are installed only when the repository selects that profile or one nested under it, instead of in every repository (#312)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.59.0)): The syntax-based `CratisRenderer` renders every case of a specification table instead of failing on the table. `SpecificationRenderer.Render` refuses a whole table with an `InvalidEventModel` that asks for one call per case. (#256)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.130.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.130.1)): `$` in a `matches` rule now means the end of the input in the C# compiler, as it already does in JavaScript. A value with a trailing newline, such as `"ABC\n"`, no longer passes `^[A-Z]{3}$`, and the built-in email rule rejects it too. An escaped `\$` and a `$` inside a character class stay literal. (#617)
- **Components** ([Release v4.26.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.26.4)): PivotViewer no longer leaves blank spaces in the card area after a large jump, such as dragging the scrollbar, pressing End or zooming out. Cards on screen are drawn first, and the cards around them keep loading on the following frames after you stop scrolling. Cards that are on screen are also no longer removed when a view transition finishes after the layout has changed. (#236)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.65.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.65.4)): An aggregate root without a declared event source no longer gets a concurrency violation when an event type it does not handle is appended for its id after it was loaded. Once the aggregate exists, its commit conflicts only with event types it applies. A new aggregate still rejects a concurrent create of any event type. (#3127)
- **Ante** ([Release v1.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Ante/releases/tag/v1.3.0)): Opening a second invitation link with the same login no longer replaces the session of the first, so the first invitation no longer fails with "Invitation is no longer pending" while it is still pending. Sessions are stored per invitation; the old per-login unique index is dropped when the application starts.
- **cli** ([Release v3.43.1](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.43.1)): `cratis screenplay generate`, `cratis screenplay conform` and `cratis view` no longer fail in the native single-file CLI on machines whose installed .NET SDK is newer than the CLI's bundled runtime; projects are evaluated only by the out-of-process build host, which selects the installed SDK for each project (#50)
- **cli** ([Release v3.43.1](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.43.1)): The native single-file CLI finds framework reference packs in the installed .NET SDK (through `DOTNET_ROOT` or the `dotnet` on `PATH`) instead of looking next to its bundled runtime (#50)
- **AI** ([Release v7.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.26.0)): The legacy-extraction skill uses `cratis screenplay generate` instead of a bare `screenplay generate`. (#529)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.39.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.39.1)): `AddCratisChronicle` now registers `IEventSources` as a scoped service, the same way as the event log, read models and the other per-store services. Code that declares an event source can now resolve it from the container in a hosted application: an Arc command with `[EventSource<T>("stream")]`, or an event-sourced aggregate root. Before, it failed with "No service for type IEventSources" before the command ran. (#4718)
- **cli** ([Release v3.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.43.0)): `cratis chronicle login` honours the connection string's `certificatePath` the same way every other command does: it presents it as a PKCS#12 client certificate, with or without a password, so login works against servers that require client certificates and against publicly trusted servers when a client certificate is configured (#198)
- **cli** ([Release v3.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.43.0)): `cratis chronicle login` reports a clear error when the configured client certificate is missing, invalid or has the wrong password (#198)
- **cli** ([Release v3.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.43.0)): A stored login made against an IPv6 server address is recognised when the same server is written differently, such as `[::1]` and `[0:0:0:0:0:0:0:1]` (#198)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.65.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.65.1)): A `CommandScenario` with Chronicle now runs commands that declare `[EventSource<T>("<stream>")]`, and commands on event-sourced aggregate roots, without registering any services by hand. The scenario provides `IEventSources` and the event serializer from its own event store. Appended events carry the route taken from the definition. `scenario.EventRoutes` now works without decision reads, and services a test registers itself still take precedence. (part of #3119)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.38.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.38.2)): Registering a new generation of an event type no longer overwrites stored event content. The backfill only adds the generations an event is missing, migrating from the generation the event was appended in, or from the highest stored generation when that is unknown. It never writes over a redaction or a revision made while it runs. The backfill still covers only the default namespace's event log (#4709)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.56.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.56.1)): Two domains with the same module, feature, slice and operation names can now be planned into one application, in one plan or in separate per-domain plans, without overwriting each other's authorization. When a domain is set, per-operation policy files, opaque policy bodies and typed-context wrappers go under `{Domain}/`, in domain namespaces. Each generated authorization policy name includes the domain. Before, rendering a second domain silently replaced the first domain's policy for an operation with the same name. Plans without a domain are unchanged. (#283)
- **cli** ([Release v3.41.1](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.41.1)): The Chronicle diagnose slash command that `cratis init` writes for AI tools now runs `cratis chronicle observers list`, `cratis chronicle failed-partitions list`, `cratis chronicle failed-partitions show` and `cratis chronicle recommendations list` instead of failing with "Unknown command" (#267)
- **cli** ([Release v3.41.1](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.41.1)): Help text, option descriptions, error hints and the package README now name Chronicle commands with their `chronicle` prefix, so commands copied from them run (#267)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.65.0)): Observable and collection queries that Screenplay now admits bind clean instead of being tolerated as refusals. Same-named queries in different slices are a known Screenplay binder limit: https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/issues/624. (#3090)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.127.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.127.1)): The editors now warn `PLAY0347` when an earlier `when` alternative of a click, double-click or select interaction already covers a later one, as the C# compiler does, with the same message (#576)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.55.0)): For ESM v6 models and later, a specification that appends an event directly now compares its `then` events only with the events that followed the append, as Screenplay's reference runner does. Before, the appended event itself could satisfy the expectation, so a specification could pass in Stage and fail in Screenplay.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.38.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.38.1)): Chronicle now records the generation each event was appended in, in MongoDB, SQL and in-memory storage, for single, batch and publication appends. Redacting an event keeps it. Events appended before this release read it as unknown. This prepares consistent generation delivery and lossless migration backfill (part of #4189) (#4708)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.124.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.124.1)): `@cratis/screenplay-language` publishes its runtime as one module per source file, so a consumer's bundler can split it. Before, everything shipped as one prebuilt shared chunk of about 508 kB, too large for a 500 kB chunk budget. Entry points and the exports map are unchanged (#612)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.124.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.124.1)): The package build fails when any published JavaScript file exceeds 500 kB, and the package README lists where the runtime's bytes go (#549)
- **cli** ([Release v3.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.41.0)): `cratis screenplay generate` no longer refuses a project that references a package adding compile items from inside its own folder, such as `Microsoft.NET.Test.Sdk`. Every application `cratis render` produces references it, so recovery always stopped with `CLI0017`. (part of #284)
- **cli** ([Release v3.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.41.0)): Recovering a rendered application now declares the read models its queries and projections return, so the recovered model binds. (part of #284)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.52.0)): A modeled value of an enum concept now renders as its enum member, so a generated backend that uses one in a command or specification compiles. (part of #236)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.52.0)): Generated query specifications declare only the result field their query assigns, so the generated Debug build has no unused-field warning. (part of #236)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.52.0)): An authored action with no planned route now uses the command route the running application registered, and stays disabled when there is none. (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.52.0)): After a successful command, Stage reads the screen's queries again once an asynchronous projection has had time to apply it, so the screen shows what the command did. (part of #237)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.38.0)): Projection tags sent at registration are now stored and returned by the kernel instead of being dropped. A change that only affects tags is saved without replaying the projection (#4512)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.38.0)): The projection tagging and filtering documentation now says that TypeScript projections can be tagged with `@tag` and `@tags` (#4513)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.38.0)): The constraint documentation now describes unique constraints as one value per event source, unique across event sources (part of #4641)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.64.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.64.1)): An aggregate root's commit now guards every event its rehydration read, so a concurrent append in another stream for the same event source id is reported as a concurrency violation instead of being silently missed (#2796)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.63.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.63.1)): Generated proxies for model-bound queries offer sorting helpers for the read model's fields instead of the query's parameters, so every helper names a field the server can sort by (#2998)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.51.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.51.3)): A screen's data binding now resolves to the query it names. A list declared as `data AllWorkItems` calls `all-work-items` and shows every work item, instead of the `WorkItemById` lookup over the same read model, which returned nothing without an argument. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.51.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.51.3)): Every modeled query is served under its own name, including queries without a `by` parameter, so `/stage/routes` maps `AllWorkItems`, `WorkItemById` and `CommentsForWorkItem` to their own routes. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.51.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.51.1)): A scene element querying a read model that backs more than one modeled query (for example `AllWorkItems` and `WorkItemById`, both returning `WorkItemSummary`) now resolves to the correct route. Previously, `StageSceneRoutes` matched candidates by the read model's type name alone and broke the tie by shortest route text; Arc names the conventional argument-less collection endpoint from the read model's pluralized name, which can be textually longer than a by-id route, so the by-id route was sometimes picked for a table that needs the collection. The element's own modeled query name (now carried in `SceneElementProperties.Query`) is tried first, and when that does not resolve, a by-id lookup is excluded from the remaining candidates before falling back to the shortest route. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.62.0)): Named tags set on a returned `EventForEventSourceId` are no longer dropped when the event is appended (#2570)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.37.9](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.37.9)): Reducer string-array properties round-trip through Mongo and SQL sinks and the reducer transport without being flattened to a scalar, with specs that guard parity across both storage providers and the PII and subject-erasure paths for array elements.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.37.8](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.37.8)): Projection futures now keep the full event context in storage, so a child resolved from a future after a restart, grain deactivation or replay is projected with the original event's occurred time, causation, correlation, caused-by, tags, routing and subject instead of defaults. Values mapped with `[SetFromContext]` (for example `Occurred`) are no longer silently wrong for such children. Futures stored by earlier versions still load as before. SQL storage gets an `EventContextJson` column on the projection futures table through a migration (#4667)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.50.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.50.1)): Modeled queries narrowed with `by` (for example `CommentsForWorkItem` by `workItemId`) are now served under their own route and return only the instances that match the argument, instead of every instance of the read model. A missing or empty argument returns an empty result. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.50.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.50.1)): The conventional `All<ReadModels>` route applies the same narrowing when a caller supplies a modeled `by` parameter, so `all-comment-views?workItemId=<id>` no longer returns other work items' comments. (part of #236) (part of #237)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.37.7](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.37.7)): Docker image publishing logs in to Docker Hub before setting up QEMU, so pulling the binfmt image is no longer subject to the anonymous pull rate limit that failed every image job of a release.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.37.6](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.37.6)): Reactor registration compares persisted metadata against the runtime's unrestricted sentinels, so unchanged definitions are no longer rewritten on every restart or reconnect.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.37.6](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.37.6)): Startup retries under storage saturation are bounded, honor shutdown, and never continue past a required step that failed.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.37.6](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.37.6)): SQL storage no longer writes an event type's display string into its id column for reactor definitions; malformed rows are rewritten on the next registration.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.37.6](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.37.6)): A read model's definition is not rewritten when unchanged, and a failed write no longer leaves in-memory state ahead of storage.
